# JAS goal loop (route JAS — joint ancestry)

- **ID:** jas-loop-2026-10-09
- **Stage:** 1-in-progress
- **Type:** CONJECTURE loop ledger
- **Statement:** Develop route JAS toward an unconditional Lean proof of RH by conjecture → proof/kill → next.

## Big picture (from the Lean corpus, 2026-10-09)
- Every JAS invariant in the 31 `JointAncestry*` modules is position-only: it is a function of
  `Re ρ` (prime-Poisson reflection sewing ⇔ Kakutani ⇔ `Re ρ = 1/2`; Haar vacuum ⇔ `σ > 1/2`;
  boundary flux ∝ `Im z`; half-density residue a function of `|2β−1|`). None consumes `ξ(ρ)=0`.
- The only zero-consuming objects near JAS: the theta kernel operator `B` (HS, nonzero spectrum
  `{-1/z : Ξ_C z = 0}`) and its source modes; integer anomalies with profile `shiftMultiplier·XiC`.
- Theta source = Gamma profile ⋆ Euler measure: `k(x) = Σ n^{-1/2} g(x + log n)`; evenness of `k` is
  modularity; prime-Poisson `μ_s` averages the Bohr lift to the `s`-shifted Euler source.
- Wall: prime-Poisson / Haar / 𝓗² structure sees `ζ` only where the Euler series converges
  (probability-cone wall); zero information must enter through modularity (theta).

## Open core (2026-10-10)
- JAS receiver converse: aggregate PSD ⟹ RH. Mechanism: multi-prime phase (cycles 3–4) + Kronecker amplification (7) + TV divergence (5). Obstruction: competitor off-line zeros with larger δ; prime sums Σ_p p^{-σ+δ}p^{iΔ} have pole main term ~X^{1-σ+δ}/(|Δ|log X), so the zero must be chosen with δ near sup and PNT-uniform control in Δ is needed.
| 9 | Single-sector gap law: c-dressed sector of off-line ρ (params p^{-(ρ+c)}, p^{-(1-ρ+c)}) non-positive at finite depth ∀ 0≤c<½+\|β−½\|; on-line dressed sectors positive ∀c≥0 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryDressedSectorGapLaw |
| 10 | Bernoulli tests: JasDressedPSD σ ⟹ Re Σ_ρ ∏_{p∈P}(1+z_pA_p+z̄_pB_p+\|z_p\|²)/(ρ(1−ρ)) ≥ 0 ∀ finite P, z (squarefree characters, multiplicative weights; exact factorization) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryBernoulliAggregate |
| 11 | Twisted Kronecker domination: z_p=e^{iφ}p^{iγ}; small uniform twist reaches any phase while keeping amplitude ≥ M·∏2(1+p^{-½-c}); on-line Bernoulli factors real in (0,2(1+p^{-½-c})]. ⟹ on-line sectors can never compensate one off-line pair (β<½, 0≤c≤1−β) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryTwistedKroneckerDomination |

## Walls map for JAS (2026-10-10)
- Existence (PSD at grade ≤1) = wall 1 (√X one-sided prime sums: pole part X^{1/2}(1+γ−log X) vs prime part X^{−1/2}Σ_{n≤X}Λ(n)(X/n−1)) and wall 6 (probability cone: dressed positivity is free only where all sector products converge absolutely, σ>½+Θ; below, any single off-line pair breaks it at finite depth).
- Converse residual (chains without a farthest zero) = wall 3 (clustering), explicit Gaussian-cluster mechanism (row 17); case Θ=1 additionally needs strip input.
- JAS ≅ Weil positivity for Dirichlet-polynomial tests (squarefree P-smooth frequencies) dressed by Poisson smoothing K_c; undressed case = corpus lag Gram.

Consequence: below 1+δ every off-line sector is individually out of budget, so positivity there needs cross-zero cancellation; the chain residual is a Gaussian cluster (≈log γ zeros, \|Δβ\|≲N^{−α}, \|Δγ\|≲N^{−α/2}) = S(T)=O(log T) wall | PROVED | R:JointAncestryPhaseBudgetPositivity |
| 20 | Chain exclusion by height-penalised maximiser + Turán power sums: target ρ* = argmax G_N(δ)−(2+C₂)log γ (excess X* ≥ C₂ log γ*); cluster ≤ C₁ log γ* zeros (local count); positivity under all small-prime rotations forces \|cluster sum\| ≤ on-line mass for every (N,σ′,τ); Turán should then give \|cluster\| ≥ e^{−O(k)}. Findings: (i) uncertainty principle — phase resolution 1/A vs gain resolution B^{−1/2}, A/√B≈√G, so ≈√G phase-distinct, gain-unresolved positions; (ii) dense flat clusters (≥√G·log γ zeros in a B^{−1/2} window) are excluded by S(T)=O(log T); (iii) sparse clusters can cancel exactly at isolated parameters; uniform cancellation across the parameter family costs Turán/Vandermonde e^{−k log(1/h)} with h = min spacing, and no unconditional lower bound on h exists | FAILED (needs polynomial spacing lower bound for distinct off-line zeros) | — |

(b) Chain attack at the critical dressing σ′=1+δ*: all sectors are inactive, gains are polylogarithmic, and target ρ_n has twisted magnitude ≈\|w_n\|/ε_n (ε_n=δ*−δ_n); on-line mass is bounded; zeros within PNT-uniform height range H=exp(c/√ε_n) are controlled by \|ζ(1+ε+it)\|≪log t; far zeros are killed by weights when ε_n≪(log γ_n)^{−2}. What remains: (i) off-line near-ties (\|Δβ\|≲ε_n, \|Δγ\|<η₀), and (ii) low off-line zeros at height distance ≫H, whose value ζ_N(1+ε_ρ+i(γ_n−γ_ρ)) is fixed by the target's alignment and is Diophantine in (γ_n−γ_ρ)log p (Kronecker alignment of distant zeros; walls 3/10). Fast separated chains (ε_n=o(\|w_n\|)) are excluded only modulo (ii) | FAILED (needs Diophantine control of zero ordinates vs log p) | — |
| 25 | Localized JAS: weights G_{T,Δ}(ρ)/(ρ(1−ρ)), G=exp((ρ−½−iT)²/(2Δ²)), \|G\|=exp((δ²−(γ−T)²)/(2Δ²)). Proved: RH ⟺ ∀T,∀Δ>0 the localized dressed aggregate is PSD at σ=½+c (any c∈(0,½]), with NO chain caveat. Converse: f_T=δ²−(γ−T)² has a maximiser (finitely many candidates); a small shift of T makes its height unique (linear tie equations); a finite P with Σ_P W_p>π lets the maximising pair's sector be rotated to a negative real (IVT along extremal angles); Δ→0 suppresses all others by e^{−κ/(2Δ²)}. HONEST CAVEAT: on the trivial character alone the localized form is Re Σ_ρ G_{T,Δ}(ρ)w_ρ = Gaussian smoothing of Φ̂ at T; ∀T,Δ positivity ⟺ Φ̂≥0 ⟺ Φ PD ⟺ RH (classical lag criterion). Chain-freeness comes from localization, not from JAS structure | PROVED (substance: BRIDGE/REDISCOVERED) | R:JointAncestryLocalizedCriterion |
| 26 | Why canonical JAS has chains (paper): JAS detects off-line zeros only through multiplicative (Euler-product) amplification, gain e^{G(δ)} with G(δ)≈N^{1−σ+δ}/log N; its γ-localisation is limited by the log-size variance of the test vector, B≈G·log²N, i.e. Gaussian factor e^{−Δγ²B/2}. A farther zero's advantage G(δ′)−G(δ)≈N^{1−σ+δ′}/log N outgrows Δγ²B for every fixed Δγ as N→∞. Prime-supported or plain integer-supported amplifiers localise well but are diagonal-dominated (no detection: δ cancels in A(ρ)·conj(Ã(ρ))). So detection and isolation trade off intrinsically; the canonical chain gap is an amplification–localization uncertainty, not a missing estimate | INSIGHT (mechanism of the chain gap) | — |
| 27 | Pattern-covering attack on the canonical chain (paper): rotate the depth-N score maximiser by twists on many pairwise-orthogonal prime patterns R_k (exponentially many, e^{cπ(N)}). In the weighted squared-angle metric a competitor with Kronecker offset c beats the target at pattern x_k only if ⟨x_k,c⟩>(\|c\|²+D)/2; by Bessel it blocks ≤\|x\|²/D patterns. Averaged over patterns, the competitors' total equals their total magnitude at the target's aligned point. Distant competitors, including ones that happen to align in amplitude, are therefore handled. The survivors are competitors whose offset is close to the uniform phase-opposing twist (Σr_pφ_p≈π, Σr_pφ_p²≲π²/G): near-ties at Δγ≈π/A, or far zeros aligned to that twist. Separating them needs a twist costing the target's whole amplitude (uncertainty principle of row 26) | FAILED (residual = phase-opposing near-alignment; walls 3/10) | — |
| 28 | Positive survivor of the chain obstruction (no-go discipline): exact amplitude profile \|ℓ(θ)\|²=ℓ(0)²/((1+2r₁(1−cosθ)/(1−r₁)²)(1+2r₂(1−cosθ)/(1−r₂)²)); peak ℓ(0)=(1−r₁r₂)/((1−r₁)(1−r₂)) at the Kronecker point bounds the amplitude everywhere; for dressed radii r₁=a/t, r₂=at the peak is strictly increasing in t=p^{\|δ\|}: at every depth the farther zero has the strictly larger peak, and amplitude decay away from the peak runs at the rate set by the same radii | PROVED | R:JointAncestrySectorAmplitudeLaw |
| 29 | Big-picture check against the corpus (search: near-double / cluster / isolation): the JAS chain residual (phase-opposing near-coincident off-line zeros, rows 20/24/26/27) is the same object as the Montgomery-form dichotomy (memory offline-isolation-dichotomy: evaders = phase-balanced clusters / periodic AP blocks of difference 2πc/log T), the Hodge(a) cluster-robust exclusion (R:ClusterRobustHodgeExclusion, level ≳ window count·log²) and level-a Weil companions (R:IsolatedDeepZeroWeilDetection). Mechanism split: undressed JAS on bounded log-levels is Weil level-a (localises, detects deep zeros below a horizon); the dressing σ>0 buys the abscissa gap but forces multiplicative amplification, which cannot localise (row 26). So the JAS-specific open core coincides with wall 3, shared by all lanes, and existence with wall 1 | INSIGHT (convergence of lanes) | — |
| 30 | σ-variation against clusters (paper): PSD holds on the whole up-ray, so a cancelling cluster must cancel for every σ′∈[σ,σ+1/log N]. There the target's phase sensitivity A(σ′) shrinks by a factor e, so the cluster sum becomes an exponential sum in A over an interval of length ≈A with frequency spread W≈B^{−1/2}, i.e. ≈√G degrees of freedom. By Ingham (resolved frequencies) it cannot vanish on average unless the cluster has ≳√G resolved members. With S(T)=O(log T), clusters are defeated whenever G* ≫ log²γ*. A plain maximiser does not guarantee this. A height penalty h(γ)=C log²γ does, but lets higher zeros exceed the target's actual magnitude, and their control needs Diophantine non-alignment of far zeros (issue (ii)). So the two residuals trade off exactly: near-ties need a large penalty, far zeros need a small one | FAILED (trade-off near-ties ↔ far alignment; walls 3/10) | — |
| 31 | Where issue (ii) sits (paper): a far zero beats the twisted target only if its Kronecker point (p^{iγ′})_{p≤N} lies in a large-deviation neighbourhood of the target's twisted point, i.e. Σ_{p≤N}log p·p^{i(γ′−γ)} is not o(θ(N)). PNT in the t-aspect rules this out only for \|γ′−γ\| ≤ exp((log N)^{3/2−ε}) (Vinogradov–Korobov range). The maximiser at depth N has log γ up to ≈N^{1−σ+δ}, far beyond that range. Under RH the no-alignment range would extend to exp(N^{1/2−ε}). So excluding the chain needs t-aspect prime-sum control of zero-free-region strength: chain exclusion is circular with quasi-RH input (wall 1 in the t-aspect), on top of wall 3 for near-ties | VERDICT: chain residual is RH-strength (CIRCULAR without new t-aspect input) | — |
| 32 | Abstract chain conjecture (zeta-free form of the converse): every quartet-symmetric configuration of points in the strip with positive summable weights whose dressed aggregate is PSD at some σ≤1 lies on the line. Proven abstract cases: finite configurations and attained supremum (row 12 uses only weights, symmetry and prime sums). Paper evidence: (a) same-height (Kronecker-free) chains are coherent — near-maximisers at depth N span Δδ≲1/A, so a small-prime twist makes them all phase π; a flat score profile along the chain can resonate at isolated N only; (b) sparse chains fall to pattern covering (row 27); (c) chains without near-ties fall to σ-variation plus Ingham (row 30). The only abstract adversary left needs, at every depth and dressing, phase-balanced clusters with ≳√G resolved members in the γ-direction plus their own compensation (infinite regress). A proof would close the chain gap without zeta input; a countermodel would show the converse needs arithmetic of the zeros (Diophantine input, row 31) | OPEN CONJECTURE (evidence for) | — |

The two named residuals are exactly the open content of route JAS: Existence (wall 1) and NoChain (walls 3/10 + t-aspect prime sums) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryRouteCapstone |
| 34 | One-prime chain problem (paper). On the lattice (log p)ℤ, PSD of k↦p^{−σ\|k\|}Φ(k log p) means a_k=Σ_ρ w_ρ z_ρ^k (z_ρ=p^{ρ−½−σ}, k≥0) are the Fourier coefficients of a positive measure λ. So H(x)=2Σ_ρ w_ρ/(1−z_ρx)−a_0 continues from \|x\|<1/R* to a Herglotz function (Re H≥0) on the unit disk. An isolated pole 1/z_ρ reachable from the inner disk contradicts this, which excludes an attained supremum. For a chain the poles accumulate on \|x\|=1/R*. Near pole n (distance d_n≈p^{σ−δ*}(δ*−δ_n)log p outside), the term has Re≈−\|w_n\|/d_n, so Re H≥0 fails when \|w_n\|/d_n→∞, i.e. fast chains δ*−δ_n=o(γ_n^{−2}) are excluded (modulo coincident pole arguments γ_n log p mod 2π). Slow chains survive. This matches row 24 (critical dressing): every method so far excludes fast chains only | PARTIAL (fast chains excluded on paper; slow chains = residual) | — |
| 35 | Fast-chain exclusion in the nearly-critical regime (paper). Take σ′=1+δ*−η with η=ε_n+1/log M, so the chain zero ρ_n is barely active (ε′log M=1); every gain is polylog, the target's twisted magnitude is ≈e·\|w_n\|log M, and on-line sectors are O(1/δ*²) (σ′>1). The target beats the on-line and far mass once log M≫γ_n², and farther chain zeros stay at gain ≤e²log M as long as ε_n log M≲1. Both fit iff ε_n≲c₀/γ_n² (fast chain). All heights involved are poly(1/η), inside the Vinogradov–Korobov range exp((log M)^{3/2}), so issue (ii) disappears. Near-ties within 1/log M rotate in phase as M ranges over [e^{Cγ_n²}, e^{1/ε_n}] (Ingham over a long A-interval). With ≤C log γ_n of them (local count), only partners closer than ε_n survive, and those are coherent. Verdict: PSD(σ≤1) ⇒ no fast chain (liminf (δ*−δ_n)γ_n²>0) appears provable unconditionally; slow chains survive, including every chain to the 1-line (ZFR forces ½−δ_n≥c/log γ_n). A heavy formalisation (t-aspect PNT, local counts, Ingham) for a partial NoChain only, leaving Existence untouched | PARTIAL (paper; fast chains only) | — |
| 36 | Chain exclusion under Θ<1 (paper). Fix σ′=1+δ*−η. (i) Target selection: walk from an active zero to a farther zero within height c₀log γ. The corpus Selberg near-line density (#{Re ρ≥σ}≪T/((σ−½)²log T) per dyadic window) stops the walk, giving a target ρ_t with no farther zero within c₀log γ_t at height ≥G/2. This removes the slow-chain problem of row 35: the target can be amplified to E≈(log γ_t)^{1+s} before farther zeros overtake. (ii) Far zeros: Lorentzian suppression a/\|Δ\| of the prime-sum main term, plus log ζ/arg ζ bounded via the corpus ζ′/ζ≪log t/(σ−½−α) under XiZeroHalfPlaneBound. Far alignment (row 31) disappears when Θ<1. (iii) Residual = near-tie clusters: k≤C log γ off-line zeros within \|Δγ\|≲√(K/E)/U and \|Δδ\|≲K/(EU) of the target, with relative phases EUΔγ. Turán-type lemmas need unit weights (aligned designs) or an exact power structure at precision e^{−O(k log k)}; neither is available, because ρ↦w(ρ)∏ℓ_p^ρ(θ_p) is holomorphic in ρ (A_pB_p=p^{−(1+2c)} is ρ-free). Mean-value obstruction: at every test point, every circle around the target has an unsuppressed direction | REDUCED (paper): chain∧Θ<1 ⇒ near-tie clusters | — |
| 37 | No chain ⇒ zero-free half-plane and power-saving PNT. Without a chain and RH, a farthest zero exists and every zero has Re ρ≤½+(σ*−1). Without a chain at all: XiZeroHalfPlaneBound α for some α<½, and \|ψ(t)−t\|≤Ct^θ with θ<1 (via corpus generalized von Koch). PSD(σ) with σ≥1 plus no chain gives Re ρ≤σ−½. So the NoChain half of the capstone already contains quasi-RH. Positive survivor of the chain no-go | PROVED | R:JointAncestryChainZeroFreeHalfPlane |
| 38 | Amplification–resolution uncertainty (paper). Any amplifying design rotates relative phases of near-tied zeros at rate X=E·avg log p ≥ E log 2 per unit offset. Fourier probes in extra primes q evaluate the cluster polynomial g(L)=Σ b_j e^{z_jL} on L∈[X,X+K/α_t] (exact exponential structure through q^{k z_j}), but the margin K≤E caps their reach at K/α_t. Near ties at offsets in [1/X, α_t/K] are neither constructive in the design nor resolved by the probes, and the gap factor α_t·avg log p≥α_t log 2 is never <1. Positive survivor: the zero kernel 𝒦_w(s)=w(s)∏ℓ_p^s(w_p) is holomorphic in the zero s on the dressed strip, so by the maximum principle every circle around a zero position carries a point with ‖𝒦‖≥‖𝒦(s₀)‖, one with Re𝒦≥Re𝒦(s₀), and one with Re𝒦≤Re𝒦(s₀) | PROVED (survivor); chain∧Θ<1 residual = near ties | R:JointAncestryHolomorphicZeroKernel |
| 39 | Exact cluster polynomial via fixed-cumulant designs (paper). A twisted test at prime set P multiplies cluster member j relative to the target by exp(Σ_m z_j^m κ_m/m!) with κ_m=Σ_{p∈P} r_p(log p)^m e^{iφ_p}. Choose ~2k prime ranges so that κ_2,…,κ_M are fixed while κ_1=X varies over [X_lo,X_hi], X_hi≤2X_lo, with amplitude κ_0≥E (a moment-cone slice). Then the cluster sum is exactly g(X)=Σ b_j e^{z_jX}+O(E(K/E)^{(M+1)/2}/(M+1)!), with b_j≈positive, so g(0)≥1. Nazarov–Turán on [0,X_hi]⊃[X_lo,X_hi] gives sup|g|≥e^{−X_hi max|Re z_j|}(2C)^{−k}. This beats non-cluster suppression only if the members' δ-offsets have a multiplicative gap (pigeonhole costs Λ^k), i.e. k≲loglog γ. Also a harmonic-measure bound: cancellation depth ≤ e^{−C√(KE)}, which always exceeds e^{−K}. Verdict: under Θ<1, chains need near-tie clusters of size ≫loglog γ, of height (log γ)^{−(1+s)/2}/U and matching δ, at every admissible target. Contrast: PSD(0)⇒RH has no chain problem because Σ w_ρ/(s−ρ+½) is meromorphic on ℂ with discrete poles; any dressing σ>0 replaces this by multi-prime generating functions whose poles accumulate on circles | REDUCED (paper): bounded/loglog clusters excluded | — |
| 40 | Chain-free converse. PSD(σ) gives n^{−σ}Φ(log n)≤Φ(0) on integer characters. With the corpus integer-interval bound Φ(L)≤Φ(log⌊e^L⌋)+30 this gives Φ(L)≤Me^{σL}, and the corpus Landau growth criterion then gives Re ρ≤½+σ for 0≤σ<½ (two-sided by reflection). Unconditionally Re ρ≤½+σ*, so σ*≥Θ−½ always, while σ*≥Θ+½ off a chain: the chain gap is exactly 1, the exponent at which Σp^{−s} starts to diverge. JAS existence at any level σ<½ is therefore at least quasi-RH Θ≤½+σ, with no chain caveat | PROVED | R:JointAncestryChainFreeHalfPlane |
| 41 | Explicit unconditional existence: JasDressedPSD 2, so σ*≤2. At c=3/2 both radii are ≤p^{−3/2}, so each width is ≤(8/7)p^{−3/2}. Over any finite prime set Σp^{−3/2}≤2^{−3/2}+Σ_{odd n≥3}n^{−3/2}≤3/(2√2), using n^{−3/2}≤(n−1)^{−1/2}−(n+1)^{−1/2}. The weight argument is ≤1/|γ|≤1/14 (corpus γ²≥431/2 off the line). Total ≤1.29<π/2. Existence-axis map now in Lean: σ*∈[Θ−½,2] always, σ*∈[Θ+½,2] off a chain, RH⇔σ*=0, and PSD(σ<½) gives quasi-RH Θ≤½+σ | PROVED | R:JointAncestryDressingTwoExistence |
| 42 | Complementarity test (paper). The one-integer Herglotz route (row 34): PSD on m^ℤ makes the periodization G_m(s)=Σ_k G(s+2πik/log m) of the lag pole series G(s)=Σ w_ρ/(s−ρ+½) analytic and Re≥0 on Re s>σ. Near the accumulation line Re s=δ*, cluster members add constructively (Re w/(δ*−δ+ε)>0), so near ties help there; but boundedness fails only when k_n w_n/(δ*−δ_n) beats the O(log m) tail, i.e. for fast or heavily clustered chains. Amplification (rows 35–39) handles slow chains but meets destructive clusters. A combined adversary (slow chain, δ*−δ_n≥1/γ_n, with clusters of size loglog γ_n≪k_n≪γ_n log m) defeats both. NoChain stays open; this does not reduce it further | FAILED (complementarity insufficient) | — |
| 43 | Height-averaged tests (paper, failed). Averaging D over test heights τ with a kernel K≥0 is legitimate under PSD, and a Gaussian heuristic suggested that each zero is then seen at its own peak, which would make clusters constructive. Exact Fourier: ∫K D = Σ_ρ w_ρ Σ_n c_n^ρ e^{iφ\|n\|}e^{i(γ_ρ−γ_t)lag(n)} K̂(lag n), so a window W in τ keeps only \|lag\|≲1/W. Exponential tilting at t=δ maps the off-line coefficients c_n e^{δ lag} to the on-line coefficients p^{−σ′\|n_p\|}, so balanced characters carry only on-line mass: amplification vanishes exactly (the e^{E/2} heuristic was a Gaussian large-deviation error). General form: the amplifying lag law has mean X=E·avg log p and spread √Y=√E·rms log p (central limit), so offsets in [π/X, 1/√Y] are either kept with phase ΔX or killed together with the amplification. Local non-uniform shifts change relative phases only at second order (Im(log ℓ)″=−r₂ sin φ≈0 at the peak). This confirms that no linear positive test resolves near ties | FAILED (uncertainty is CLT-sharp) | — |
| 44 | Local existence at the target level σ=1. Restrict to characters on a finite prime set S (JasDressedPSDOn S σ). At c=½ the radii r₁=p^{−(β+½)}, r₂=p^{−(3/2−β)} lie in [t³,t] (t=p^{−1/2}) with r₁r₂=t⁴, so r₁²+r₂²≤t²+t⁶ and the sharp width bound is tan W_p≤√(p/(p²+p+1)), attained as β→0,1. Hence PSDOn S 1 holds unconditionally whenever Σ_{p∈S}√(p/(p²+p+1))+1/14≤π/2, e.g. S={2,3,5}: the first unconditional positivity at the dressing JAS actually needs. Per-zero budgets diverge over all primes (Σ√(p/(p²+p+1))=∞), so global existence at σ=1 is compensation between zeros over infinitely many primes (wall 1). Also: the one-prime Herglotz route only sees δ>σ and is subsumed by row 40; a smooth (Dirichlet) cutoff mirrors the sharp cutoff, with amplification stopping at the accumulation line x*=1+δ*−σ (slow chains survive) | PROVED (local) | R:JointAncestryLocalExistence |
| 45 | Signed stationary designs (paper, failed). A real-signed design μ=Σε_p r_p δ_{log p} with m₁=∫λdμ=0, m₀>0, m₂>0 exists (e.g. weights 4,−4,1 at scales 1,2,4). With m₃=0 also arranged, near-tied members at height offsets Δ have phases O(Δ³m₃)→O(Δ⁵). But G(z)=∫e^{iλz}dμ is holomorphic in z=Δ+i(δ_t−δ), so Re G is harmonic. Stationary designs turn the target into a saddle: zeros slightly nearer the line (z=id) gain amplitude m₂d²/2, and members with both offsets get phase ≈m₂Δd. Aligned designs suppress one direction at rate X and leave the other neutral with phase X·distance. A design is flat on a cluster only at radius ≪1/√Y. For a single design, clusters of size k leave destructive residues (k−1)e^{−π²/(2k)}. The ζ′/ζ bound ≪log t/(σ−Θ) and Jensen allow clusters of up to C log γ off-line zeros at the Θ-boundary, so the residual is untouched by known bounds | FAILED (holomorphic uncertainty) | — |
| 46 | Analytic continuation of the vertical criterion (paper). Z_σ(τ)=Σ_ρ w(ρ)ζ(ρ+c+iτ)ζ(1−ρ+c−iτ), with continued ζ rather than partial Euler products (P=∞). An off-line zero's Kronecker value w ζ(σ+δ)ζ(σ−δ) is negative, with no twist needed, exactly in the activity window \|σ−1\|<δ (ζ<0 on (0,1)); at σ=1 it is ≈−w/δ². So Z_1 detects near-line off-line zeros (δ≪1/γ) that have no on-line partner within ~δ, and is blind to far zeros. Near τ=−γ, Z_1(τ)≈w(s₀)(ξ′/ξ)′(s₀)+smooth with s₀=½−iτ, i.e. the first Laguerre inequality for Ξ: necessary for RH, not sufficient. Finite-P JAS and its continuation detect complementary regimes (Euler-product amplification favours far zeros; continuation favours near-line zeros). Chains live at δ*>0, so continuation does not help the NoChain residual | INSIGHT (complementarity); no closure | — |
| 47 | Scale-averaged Gaussian–Gram variant (paper). At scale X with test height τ, the cluster sum is g(τ;X)=Σ_j b_j e^{iΔ_jX}e^{−(τ−Δ_j)²Y/2}, with b_j≈positive, so ∫\|g\|²dτ=√(π/Y)·v*Gv (G = Gaussian Gram, v_j=b_je^{iΔ_jX}). Gaussians with distinct centres are independent, so g≢0. But λ_min(G) is exponentially small for clusters inside the width 1/√Y. Averaging v*Gv over X: unresolved pairs (\|Δ_j−Δ_k\|X_max≤1) are constructive, resolved pairs average out, but intermediate pairs leave sinc residues ≥−0.22 (one-sided ranges keep the phase e^{i·sep·X_c}). Exact form: the zeroth moment M₀(X)=Σb_j⁰e^{z_jX} with z_j=d_j+iΔ_j, and Nazarov from M₀(0)=Σb_j⁰≥1 loses (2C)^k·e^{X_max·max\|d_j\|}. The δ-offset factor doubles the suppression threshold unless a multiplicative gap exists in the d_j (pigeonhole cost 2^k). Same threshold as row 39: clusters of ≲loglog γ members are excluded, larger ones are not | FAILED (same loglog γ threshold) | — |
| 48 | Sharpened chain residual (paper). Same-δ near ties (pure phases) fall to Turán II with positive weights (b_j≈positive, so partial sums ≥b_t): loss C^{−k}≥γ^{−C′}, against margin e^{−(E−2log γ)} with E up to (log γ)^{a/ε_t}. δ-offset members need a multiplicative gap in their projected offsets (Nazarov decay e^{2A} versus suppression e^{−A′}, A′>2A+B). The rotatable first-order direction χ (twist) and the choice of target vertex leave only gapless hierarchical clusters, i.e. members at every dyadic scale between 1/X_max and √(K/Y), needing ≥½log₂E≈(a/2ε_t)log₂log γ members. Local count allows ≤C log γ, so a counterexample needs a slow chain, ε(γ)≫loglog γ/log γ, which forces a zero-free strip of that width just below Θ up to height γ, together with gapless hierarchical off-line clusters at every admissible target. Not excluded by any corpus bound | REDUCED (paper) | — |
| 49 | Exactness budget (paper). With a power-saving zero density (classical, Carlson/Ingham; not yet in Lean), the target window is h=γ^{c″} and E_max≈γ^{c″a/ε_t}. This affords M≈c·log γ fixed cumulants (signed interpolation masses ≈E·e^{O(M)}≤E_max), so the exponential-sum exactness error drops to γ^{−Ω(log γ)}. Then same-δ (pure-phase) clusters of ANY size ≤C log γ fall to Turán II with positive weights (no decay factor). δ-offset clusters still need a multiplicative gap; doubling shells A_{i+1}=2A_i+B′ with B′≈C log γ must stay ≤c′(log γ)² (exactness), which caps the gap argument at ≈log₂log γ levels. Residual (under Θ<1): near-tie clusters whose members carry a gapless dyadic ladder of ≥log₂log γ distinct real-part offsets inside (log γ)²/X of the target, at every admissible target. A first optimistic version (slow chains removed and all clusters handled) was withdrawn after checking the exactness cost of large A | REDUCED (paper; residual = δ-ladders) | — |
| 50 | Weighted separation-free Turán (Lean). If some \|z_i\|≥1 and every weight lies in the sector \|β_i\|≤2Re β_i, then for every M some ν∈[M+1,M+N] has \|Σβ_iz_i^ν\|≥(Σ_{\|z_i\|≥1}\|β_i\|)/(4eN(6(N+1)(M+2N+2))^N). Sector weights cannot cancel on the outer points of the corpus separating polynomial: Re Σ_O β_oQ(z_o)≥Σ\|β_o\|/4. This is the cluster ingredient of row 48: same-δ near ties with nearly positive weights cannot keep a power sum small on a window of N consecutive exponents | PROVED | H:PowerSumTuranWeighted |
| 51 | Polynomially isolated real-part peaks (Lean). For ½<σ₀<1 there are 0<c<1 and G₀ such that, for every G≥G₀ and every zero q₀ with Re q₀≥σ₀ and Im q₀≥2G, some zero q has Re q≥Re q₀, Im q≥G and Re q′≤Re q for all zeros q′ with \|Im q′−Im q\|≤(Im q)^c. Proof: a walk to strictly farther zeros within height (Im)^c visits distinct zeros of real part ≥σ₀. Crossing a dyadic box [T,2T] downwards or upwards costs ≳T^{1−c} such zeros, which exceeds the corpus Ingham–Huxley count D(1+log T)^B T^{(A+ε)(1−σ₀)} once e+c<1. Local finiteness forces the climb. This is the target-selection step (rows 36/49) of the chain attack, now unconditional in Lean | PROVED | R:JointAncestryIsolatedRealPartPeaks |
| 52 | Chains have isolated peaks at every height (Lean). Under JasOffLineChain, every off-line zero ρ₀ with Re ρ₀>½ and every H yield a zero ρ with Re ρ≥Re ρ₀ and Im ρ≥H that maximises Re on \|Im ρ′−Im ρ\|≤(Im ρ)^c. The chain iterates to distinct zeros of increasing distance, so their heights are unbounded; they are moved to the upper right quarter by 1−ρ̄ and conjugation, then fed to the density walk of row 51. Consequence: target selection is never the obstruction to NoChain. What remains is near-tie interference at the peak (rows 45–49) and t-aspect prime sums for far heights (a shifted Perron formula under XiZeroHalfPlaneBound, not yet in the corpus) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryChainPeaks |
| 53 | Height-uniform shifted Perron formula (Lean). Under XiZeroHalfPlaneBound α, for ½+α<σ₁<1, η>0 there is K with \|Σ_{n≤N}Λ(n)n^{−s₀} − x^{1−s₀}/(1−s₀) + ζ′/ζ(s₀)\| ≤ K·x^{σ₁−Re s₀}(1+log(x+\|Im s₀\|+9))² for all s₀ with σ₁+η≤Re s₀<9/8, s₀≠1, x=N+½. Contour w=s−s₀ over [σ₁−Re s₀, 9/8−Re s₀]×[−T,T], T=x+\|Im s₀\|+7; the two poles w=0 and w=1−s₀ are separated by dslope (integrand = dslope h 0 + h(0)/w, h(w)=−ζ′/ζ(s₀+w)x^w). This is the far-height input of the chain attack: the target-scale prime sums Σp^{−α−iΔ} with α=σ−δ_t>Θ sit right of the frontier exactly when Θ<1, and their main term N^{1−α}/(1−α+iΔ) shows magnitude decorrelation only at \|Δ\|≳1−α, with a pure phase e^{−iΔ log N} below that scale (the CLT window of row 43). Classical content; corpus infrastructure | PROVED (classical) | R:Sieve.ShiftedPerronHalfPlane, R:Sieve.ShiftedPerronUniform |
| 54 | Chain exclusion under quasi-RH (Lean). If every zero has Re ρ ≤ ½+α with α<½, the off-line zeros form a chain and JasDressedPSD σ holds, then σ ≥ 1+sup\|Re ρ−½\|. Hence RH ⟺ JasDressedPSD 1 ∧ ∃α<½, XiZeroHalfPlaneBound α, and under Θ<1 the abscissa is bimodal, σ* ∈ {0} ∪ [½+Θ, 2]. Mechanism: test with log-weight Bernoulli vectors centred at the left copy ξ of a chain peak, over the primes in (n, n²], n = ⌊γ^{75/θ}⌋, with per-prime amplitude τ log p/p (σ drops out of the factor and only enters admissibility). Every zero q then sees the exponent τ(e^{iφ}S(ξ−q) + e^{−iφ}S(q+ξ̄−1)), S(z) = Σ(log p/p)p^z. Zeros within height 1 of the peak have essentially positive weights, and the scale τ enters all their phases linearly, so simultaneous Dirichlet approximation (scale range 8^K, K ≤ 16(C+log γ)) puts every cluster phase within π/4 of the target phase: the cluster adds to the negative peak instead of cancelling it. This removes the δ-ladder residual of rows 48–49, which came from resolving near ties with Turán/Nazarov instead of aligning them. The rest is suppressed by the Mertens deficit 1−Re S/M (window), the uniform shifted Perron formula of row 53 (heights up to T₀ = e^Q), and the weight tail Σ_{\|γ\|>T}\|w_q\| ≤ 80(C+log(3T+4))/T. What remains of the chain problem is Θ = 1 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLogWeightBernoulli, LogWeightPrimeSum, LogWeightExpansion, ClusterPhase, ZeroTailBound, QuasiRHAmplification, QuasiRHCore, QuasiRHParameters, QuasiRHChainExclusion |
| 55 | Abscissa gap (Lean, corollary of row 54 and the chain-free half-plane bound). Unconditionally σ* = 0 or σ* ≥ ½, so RH ⟺ σ* < ½ ⟺ ∃σ<½, JasDressedPSD σ. Under a half-plane bound α<½: σ* = 0 or σ* ≥ 1 + δ*. And 0 < σ* ≤ 1 forces sup Re ρ = 1. So, unlike the de Bruijn–Newman constant, any unconditional upper bound below ½ already proves RH | PROVED | R:JointAncestryAbscissaGap |
| 56 | Conjecture: Θ = 1 chains also force σ* ≥ ½ + Θ, which would make RH ⟺ JasDressedPSD 1 hold unconditionally. Attacked with three designs. (a) Window (n,n²] with n = γ^A: a farther zero at height h adds excess τM(n^{2Δ}−1) to the exponent. Dominating it needs either Δ ≲ 1/log γ, or prime-sum cancellation up to heights e^{τMn^{2Δ}}, which a zero-free region of VK strength cannot supply. (b) Short window n = polylog γ: this needs large-value bounds for a length-n² Dirichlet polynomial at the zero ordinates up to γ^C, and the moment method leaves √γ exceptional zeros. (c) σ ∈ (½,1) with finite P: the excess is bounded, but the method needs torus separation of the zero points (p^{iγ}), which is a prime-twisted pair-correlation statement. Positive survivor (paper only): the row-54 proof goes through without quasi-RH whenever infinitely many records ρ have every zero up to height \|Im ρ\|^{C} within κ/log\|Im ρ\| of Re ρ. dlVP-hugging and slowly approaching chains violate this | FAILED (record-fast survivor, paper) | — |
| 57 | Two-sided abscissa law (Lean). Under a half-plane bound α ≤ 1/20, JasDressedPSD(1 + 3α) holds, by per-zero phase budgets: a zero with \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ α has width ≤ (8/3)α log p·p^{−(1+2α)}, and Σ_p log p·p^{−s} ≤ −ζ'/ζ(s) ≤ 1/(s−1) + 1. With row 54, off RH under quasi-RH with δ* ≤ 1/20: 1 + δ* ≤ σ* ≤ 1 + 3δ*. Dictionary: JAS positivity at level 1 + ε is, up to the factor 3 and the Θ = 1 exception, the same statement as the zero-free half-plane Re s > ½ + ε. So σ* − 1 is a bi-Lipschitz proxy for Θ − ½; σ* → 1⁺ as δ* → 0⁺, and RH is the isolated value σ* = 0 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryHalfPlaneExistence |
| 58 | Zero-free capstone (Lean). RH ⟺ (every arithmetic Fejér form has Re ≥ 0) ∧ (Σ(Λ(n)−1)n^{−σ} converges for all σ > θ, for some θ < 1). Both conditions involve only primes, ψ, Σ_{n≤X}Λ(n)/n and constants. Also RH ⟺ ∃σ<½, JasDressedPSD σ. Reading: JAS positivity at σ = 1 is exactly the bridge from a power-saving PNT to RH. Proving it unconditionally would show that every zero-free strip forces RH, so it is at least as hard as "quasi-RH ⇒ RH" | PROVED | R:JointAncestryArithmeticCapstone |
| 59 | Sharp per-zero constant (Lean). For K > 1 with log((K+1)/(K−1)) < π/2, i.e. K > coth(π/4) ≈ 1.525, every half-plane bound α ≤ α₀(K) gives JasDressedPSD(1 + Kα). The phase width is (p^{−a} − p^{−b})(1 + O(p^{−2})), with a, b = 1 + Kα ∓ d. Integrating Σ_p log p·p^{−s} ≤ 1/(s−1) + 1 over [a, b] gives Σ_p (p^{−a} − p^{−b}) ≤ log((b−1)/(a−1)) + (b−a), and the weight argument is ≤ d/7. Concrete: off RH, under quasi-RH with δ* ≤ 1/100, 1 + δ* ≤ σ* ≤ 1 + (5/3)δ*. coth(π/4) is exactly where the per-zero budget Σ_p W_p → log((K+1)/(K−1)) reaches π/2, so this is the best constant per-zero sectors can give. Below it, any proof needs cross-zero (on-line mass) information | PROVED | R:JointAncestrySharpAbscissaLaw |
| 60 | Θ = 1 obstruction in additive form (paper). In a log-weight test at target ρ_t, a bulk zero q' sees a large window sum S(ξ − q̂') only through the explicit formula. That needs a spoiler zero ρ'' with q̂' + conj ρ_t ≈ ρ'' to within 1/log n; then Re ρ'' ≈ 1 − Δ with Δ = δ' − δ_t. The zero-free region Re ρ'' ≤ 1 − c/log γ'' forces Δ ≥ c/(B log γ) − C/(A log γ). So zeros less than c'/log γ farther than the target are safe unconditionally, and only zeros farther by ≥ c'/log γ below height T₀ can hide a chain. Exclusion therefore needs either (i) a record whose height-γ^C successors exceed it by < c'/log γ (plateau records), or (ii) no near-1 additive triples ρ₁ + ρ₂ ≈ ρ₃. Counting cannot supply (ii): geometric ordinate sequences 2^k satisfy Z ⊂ Z − Z and fit every density bound. Single-prime Herglotz tests at σ = ½ also fail: the negative real part is ~ γ^{−4}η^{−1} against the on-line mass. Verdict: Θ = 1 invisibility is a self-consistency problem; the zeros would have to spoil exactly the prime sums that detect them | FAILED (obstruction sharpened, paper) | — |
| 61 | Haar-singular certificates (Lean). A JAS certificate at σ is a finite measure on the prime torus whose Fourier coefficients are the dressed aggregate; it forces JasDressedPSD σ. For 0 < σ < ½ every certificate is singular to Haar. Reason: it forces RH (abscissa gap), and under RH it equals Σ_ρ w_ρ μ_{σ,−γ_ρ}, a countable sum of prime-Poisson laws, each Haar-singular by Kakutani's Σ_p p^{−2σ} = ∞. Hence no Haar-absolutely-continuous measure (no L¹/L² Haar density) certifies JAS positivity below ½. Above ½, under RH, the canonical certificate is Haar-absolutely continuous. So the ½ of the abscissa gap is the Kakutani threshold. This links the receiver to the corpus superselection walls: any RH-deciding producer must build a Haar-singular measure, i.e. stay in the prime-Poisson class; Haar or regular-representation constructions are excluded | PROVED | R:JointAncestryHaarSingularCertificate |
| 62 | Lebesgue-decomposition view (paper; candidate route for Θ = 1). Fix σ ∈ (½, 1) and assume a certificate ν exists. Split the zeros at a threshold κ < σ − ½. The pieces with \|δ\| ≤ κ sum in L²(Haar) to A, because each local factor deviates from 1 by O(p^{−(σ−\|δ\|)}). So B = ν − A is a complex measure, although each near-1 piece has divergent total variation (Σ_p p^{−2(σ−\|δ\|)} = ∞). Positivity gives B_P ≥ −A_P on every T^P, so the negative part of the near-1 sum is bounded in L² uniformly in P. One near-1 pair has phase spreading by independent local arguments, which forces a negative part of the same size as its growing L² norm, so a single pair is excluded. Several near-1 zeros reintroduce cross terms Π_p⟨h_p(q), h_p(q')⟩, i.e. prime sums at height γ − γ' with exponent 2σ − δ − δ'. That is the same bottleneck as rows 56/60, now in L² rather than in sup norm. Open core: a distributional (CLT-type) lower bound for the negative part of Σ_q w_q Π_{p∈P} h_p(q) | OPEN (paper) | — |
| 63 | Large-prime abscissa is exactly ½ + Θ for chains (Lean). JasDressedPSDAbove X σ means positivity on characters supported on primes > X. Upper bound: under Re ρ ≤ ½ + α, large-prime positivity holds at 1 + α + ε once log X ≥ 1/ε + 1. Per-zero widths are ≤ (8/7)p^{−(1+ε)} and the tail Σ_{p>X} p^{−(1+ε)} ≤ (1/ε + 1)/log X. With α = ½ this is unconditional: large-prime JAS positivity holds at every σ > 3/2. Lower bound: the chain-exclusion theorem is refactored to need only JasWindowPositivity (log-weight tests on windows (n, n²], n ≥ N₀), so chains under quasi-RH force σ ≥ 1 + δ* for large-prime positivity too. Hence lim_{X→∞} σ*_{>X} = 1 + δ* = ½ + Θ exactly: on large primes the per-zero method is sharp and the coth(π/4) gap of row 59 disappears. Open: the non-chain (farthest-zero) case. It needs the maximal off-line exclusion redone on primes in (X, N] instead of ≤ N | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAbscissa, R:JointAncestryQuasiRHChainExclusion (refactor) |
| 64 | Large-prime abscissa = ½ + Θ in every quasi-RH configuration (Lean). The maximal off-line exclusion is ported to primes in (X, N] (R:JointAncestryLargePrimeMaximalExclusion). It uses the primes only through S_α(X,N) → ∞, S_{α'} = o(S_α), and a fixed proportional loss under twists p^{iΔ}; all three follow from the ≤ N versions, since the primes ≤ X contribute a bounded amount. Together with row 63 (chains): under any half-plane bound below ½, off RH, large-prime positivity at σ forces σ ≥ 1 + δ*, and for every ε it holds at 1 + δ* + ε above some X. So lim_{X→∞} σ*_{>X} = ½ + Θ exactly. Dictionary on large primes: JAS positivity at level σ ∈ (1, 3/2) ⟺ zero-free half-plane Re s > σ − ½ (quasi-RH), with no constant loss | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeMaximalExclusion |
| 65 | Height-averaged log-weight tests for Θ = 1 (paper). Admissibility depends only on Re ξ, so positivity survives averaging the centre over Im ξ ∈ [T, 2T]. At level Re ξ = ½ − δ*, every zero within 1/(τM log n) of the supremum has its self-peak at its own height with the same phase, so the near-sup band aligns automatically and needs no Dirichlet step. Zeros far from [T, 2T] add ≈ T·Φ(0) > 0, and the bulk enters only through mean values of exp(τE), which are unconditional (MV theorem). Failure: the large-value set of S costs T ≥ e^{2τM} by moments, while the band beats the positive bulk only for T ≤ e^{τM/3}. The large values are spoiler resonances (row 60), whose phases ψ₀ are not aligned, so this is the same obstruction in averaged form | FAILED (paper) | — |
| 66 | Weil–Euler bridge (Lean). For c > 0 and w ∈ T^P, jasZeroSumDensity c P w = Π_{p∈P}(1 − p^{−(1+2c)})·Σ_{m,n P-smooth} e_w(m) e_w(n)‾ (mn)^{−σ} Φ(log n − log m), with e_w(m) = Π w_p^{v_p(m)}. Proof: each local factor is a product of two geometric factors, i.e. Euler products of m ↦ e_w(m)m^{−(ρ+c)} over P-smooth integers (Mathlib factoredNumbers); then Fubini, since \|term\| ≤ \|w_ρ\|(mn)^{−c}. Consequences: for σ > ½, JasDressedPSD σ ⟺ Weil's lag form ≥ 0 on every Euler vector m ↦ e_w(m)m^{−σ}; and RH ⟺ (Weil form ≥ 0 on every harmonic Euler vector e_w(m)/m) ∧ quasi-RH. This states JAS existence as classical Weil positivity on an explicit family parametrised by finite tori, which the corpus Weil lanes can use directly | PROVED | R:JointAncestryWeilEulerBridge |
| 67 | Unconditional Weil positivity on Euler vectors (Lean). Weil's lag form Σ_{m,n} c_m c̄_n Φ(log n − log m) is ≥ 0, with no hypothesis, on every Euler vector c_m = e_w(m)m^{−2} (all finite P), and on every Euler vector c_m = e_w(m)m^{−(3/2+ε)} over primes > X(ε). Under a half-plane bound α it is ≥ 0 at exponent 1 + α + ε over primes > X(ε). By row 64 this is sharp off RH: large-prime Euler-vector Weil positivity at exponent σ ∈ (1, 3/2) is the zero-free half-plane Re s > σ − ½. Also: positivity on a prime set S gives nonnegative densities and Weil positivity on every sub-torus P ⊆ S | PROVED | R:JointAncestryWeilEulerUnconditional |
| 68 | C65 height-mixture rigidity (Lean). A height mixture at dressing σ is a finite positive measure m on ℝ with A_σ(n) = e^{−σ‖n‖}∫e^{−it(log u − log v)}dm(t) for every character n = u/v, i.e. the certificate ∫μ_{σ,−t}dm(t). For every real σ such an m exists ⟺ RH, and it is unique: m = Σ_ρ w_ρ δ_{γ_ρ}. Proof: integer characters give \|Φ(log n)\| ≤ m(ℝ), which bounds Φ and so gives RH; under RH the zero measure works; uniqueness because charFun m = Φ on the dense set log(a/b) (Mathlib Measure.ext_of_charFun). Reading: off RH the unconditional certificate at σ = 2 exists but is not a superposition over heights, so any producer must use the prime coordinates independently, not only through the vertical flow | PROVED | R:JointAncestryHeightMixture |
| 69 | Θ = 1 via sign-aware tests and the Steinhaus picture (paper). (a) Phase: at the aligned test point a spoiler whose effective rotations p^{i(γ₀−γ')} ≈ 1 has the same phase as the head and reinforces it; only spoilers with phase offset Ψ' = Σ_p a'_p ψ'_p ≈ π hurt. Ψ' does not depend on the head's phase design θ, but the prime weights r_p move it, and averaging the test over windows y ∈ [Y, Y^K] equidistributes it unless \|η' − η₀\| ≲ 1/log y and the triple relation holds to 1/log y. So robust spoilers are 1/log y-matched copies of the target, and a fixed target is blocked only by triples whose height defect tends to 0 (or vanishes). (b) Steinhaus form: since Σ_n \|A_1(n)\|² < ∞ unconditionally, PSD(1) ⟺ D₁(X) = Σ_ρ w_ρ ζ_X(½+ρ)·conj ζ_X(3/2−ρ̄)/ζ(2) ≥ 0 a.s. for the Steinhaus random Euler product ζ_X. Random spoilers fall to union bounds with VK zero density. Coherent spoilers need \|log ζ(1+ε+iΔ)\| ≳ ½ log(1/η₀) at Δ = γ' − γ₀, i.e. extreme values of ζ on the 1-line. Exponent-gap law (heuristic): a chain hugging a zero-free region η ≥ (log γ)^{−θ_Z} is excluded only if \|log ζ(1+it)\| ≤ (θ_Z/2 − κ) loglog t. VK gives θ_ζ = θ_Z = 2/3, and Borel–Carathéodory links tie θ_ζ to θ_Z, so this gap is never available from zero-free-region technology | FAILED (obstruction = extreme values of ζ(1+it) at near-1 zero height differences; paper) | — |
| 70 | Positivity at infinity (Lean). The large-prime farthest-zero exclusion needs no hypothesis, so: (i) positivity on characters of primes > X at a level σ ≤ 1 gives RH or an off-line chain; (ii) RH ⟺ (∀σ>1 ∃X: JAS positivity at σ on primes > X) ∧ no chain, and the same with quasi-RH in place of "no chain"; (iii) Weil form: RH ⟺ no chain ∧ ∀σ>1 ∃X(σ): Weil's lag form ≥ 0 on every Euler vector e_w(m)m^{−σ} supported on X-rough integers; (iv) with no hypothesis, positivity at infinity at every level above 1 gives RH or zeros approaching Re s = 1. New lemma: pointwise positivity on the sub-tori of S gives positivity on S. Reading: the existence half lives entirely in the absolutely convergent range σ > 1 and on arbitrarily large primes. There it holds unconditionally above 3/2, and in (1, 3/2] it is the zero-free-half-plane problem. Small primes and the conditionally convergent range play no role | PROVED | R:JointAncestryPositivityAtInfinity |
| 71 | Plateau records and the reduced Θ = 1 core (Lean + paper). Lean (unconditional): every off-line chain has, for every κ > 0, C > 1, records ρ of arbitrarily large height (no zero of smaller height is farther from the line) such that every zero up to height \|Im ρ\|^C has δ ≤ δ(ρ) + κ/log log\|Im ρ\|. Proof: otherwise records improve by κ/loglog γ within polynomial height steps, and Σ_j κ/(L₀ + j log C) diverges against δ ≤ ½. The slack scale is sharp for this argument: (log γ)^{−θ} with θ > 0 gives a convergent sum. Paper consequence: in a window test on primes ≤ N = (log γ)^A at a plateau record, (i) farther zeros up to height γ^C have bounded excess; (ii) zeros nearer than δ(ρ) − O(1/log N) are dominated whatever their phase (the deficit beats the weight ratio); (iii) the only possible spoilers are zeros with \|η' − η\| = O(1/loglog γ) whose height difference Δ = γ' − γ lies in the Bohr set {p^{iΔ} ≈ 1, p ≤ (log γ)^A}, with anti-aligned phase. By VK zero density there are ≤ exp(c log γ/(loglog γ)^{3/2}) such zeros, while moments bound the relative measure of the Bohr set by exp(−c' log γ/loglog γ). So the Θ = 1 core is now a coincidence statement: near-1 zeros sitting in Bohr translates of every plateau record. Counting cannot exclude it, but it is the only remaining configuration | PROVED (lemma) / OPEN (core) | R:JointAncestryPlateauRecords |
| 72 | C72: near-1 zeros are forced to align on small primes, so plateau-record spoilers would reinforce (paper). FAILED. (a) The rigorous form of forced alignment is the Hadamard inequality Σ Λ(n)n^{−σ}cos(γ log n) ≤ ½log γ − Σ_{ρ'} Re 1/(s−ρ') + O(1). It gives a weighted mean of cos(γ log p) ≤ −½ only when η ≪ 1/log γ, and VK excludes that range. Pair versions (\|1 + e^{iθ} + e^{iθ'}\|² ≥ 0) only give η ≥ c/log γ for two zeros and do not use alignment. Positive survivors: (b) averaging the plateau-record test over log N ∈ [A, KA]·loglog γ, with amplitude tuned so that τS ≡ C log γ, equidistributes the phase of any spoiler whose mean phase offset varies across scales. Near-coincident zeros with \|Δ\| ≪ 1/(K log γ loglog γ) reinforce the head, so the only robust spoilers have p^{iΔ} ≈ e^{iM₀} with M₀ ≈ π/(C log γ) on all p ≤ (log γ)^{KA} (Bohr alignment). (c) Letting the amplitude run up to τS = M log γ (allowed when κ ≲ ε/(MA) in the plateau lemma) kills twins with alignment rms ψ² ≳ 4/M. So for κ → 0 the surviving spoilers are near-perfectly Bohr-aligned near-1 twins. (d) VK prime sums exclude Bohr alignment on (N/2, N] for 1 ≤ \|Δ\| ≤ exp(c(log N)^{3/2}), so spoilers sit at \|Δ\| ∈ [exp(c(KA loglog γ)^{3/2}), γ^{1+ε}]. Method no-go (exponent gap, now sharp): plateau control needs log N = O(loglog γ), while excluding alignment at polynomial Δ via a zero-free region η ≥ (log γ)^{−θ_Z} needs log N ≥ (log γ)^{θ_Z}. These are compatible only for θ_Z = 0, i.e. a zero-free strip (quasi-RH). So JAS window tests close Θ = 1 iff one adds either quasi-RH (circular) or a non-alignment theorem for pairs of near-1 zeros at polynomial height differences | FAILED (method circular without a non-alignment input; positive salvage (b)–(d)) | — |
| 73 | Amplitude averaging (paper). In the plateau-record test the spoiler phase relative to the head is Ψ_j(τ) = τS₁M₀^{(j)}, linear in the amplitude, and the A-factors and weights add only O(1/γ). Averaging the positive tests over τS₁ ∈ [C log γ, MC log γ] with log-uniform weight gives average cos Ψ ≥ −0.47/log M for every frequency, since Ci is bounded by 0.47. So after normalising the head, spoilers can subtract at most (0.47/log M)·Σ_{cluster}\|w_j\|/\|w_target\|. Taking the target to be the heaviest (lowest) member of its near-band cluster, the cluster must contain ≳ (log M)/0.47 mutually Bohr-aligned near-1 zeros. M is limited only by the plateau slack (MCκA < 2ε). Hence, for every κ > 0, Θ = 1 needs every κ-plateau record to sit in a cluster of ≳ log(1/κ) near-equal-real-part zeros, mutually Bohr-aligned on primes ≤ (log γ)^A at height differences in [exp((loglog γ)^{3/2}), γ^{1+ε}]. Large-sieve and moment bounds give no constraint for polylog polynomials at polynomial heights. No corpus result on non-alignment of zero ordinates exists (searched) | OPEN (core sharpened: aligned near-1 clusters of unbounded size) | — |
| 74 | C74: cluster self-averaging (paper; new mechanism). Average the PSD point tests over the design points of all cluster members j, with weights u_j = \|w_j\|·A_j, and give each design point its own radial dressing r^{(j)}_p = p^{−(η_j + 2s − η*)}. On the b-side (n ≤ 0, the dominant side for near-1 zeros) member k's term at design j has coefficient p^{−c\|n\|}·g_j(n)g_k(n) with g_j = e^{−(η_j+s−η*/2)L(\|n\|)}. So the averaged cluster contribution is EXACTLY Re Σ_n p^{−c\|n\|} e^{in·θ}\|Σ_j u_j g_j(n) e^{in·ψ_j}\|², with ψ_j the member's prime-phase pattern. With θ = (π/S)·1 the bulk degrees m ≈ S ± M√S carry cos(tm) = −1 + O(M²/S), and low degrees m < S/2 have weight ≤ γ^{−0.15C}. Hence the cluster contributes ≈ −G(0)·Q with Q = ‖Σ_j u_j V_j‖², V_j = ⊗_p(√(1−b_p), √b_p e^{iψ_{j,p}}) unit product vectors, Q ≥ 0. Margins: for an anti-phase pair, 1 + Re⟨V₁,V₂⟩ ≥ 1 − e^{−D} with D ≥ Ψ²/(2S) (Cauchy–Schwarz on the pattern), so Q ≥ u₁u₂π²/S. Three-member 120° cancellation also has margin ≍ 1/S. Error terms: dominated zeros and the positive bulk are ≤ γ^{2−C}; weight phases O(1/γ). The a-side asymmetry is made O(Δη·N^{−½}) by testing on one prime shell (N/2, N] with N^{½}/log N ≈ C log γ. So twins and small clusters cannot rescue PSD. Residual: large structured clusters whose offset distribution μ has μ̂(m) vanishing to order r at the bulk degree m ≈ S (example: constant offsets s_j·1, uniform on [0, 2π/S], gives Q/Σ\|⟨V_j,V_k⟩\| ≍ S^{−r}). These would need ≳ S^{r} members arranged like a smooth measure, and must survive window changes that move the bulk degree | PARTIAL (paper): twins/small clusters excluded; residual = high-order structured clusters | — |
| 75 | Cluster identity (Lean) and the rescue bound (paper). Lean (R:JointAncestryClusterIdentity): Σ_{j,k} u_j u_k Π_p (1 − θ_p ψ_{j,p} ψ̄_{k,p} p^{−z})⁻¹ = Σ_{m P-smooth} e_θ(m) m^{−z} \|Σ_j u_j e_{ψ_j}(m)\|² (exact, any real weights, unimodular patterns, Re z > 0); for θ ≡ 1 this is a sum of squares. Also (Σ b sin x)² ≤ 2(Σ b)Σ b(1 − cos x). Paper consequences, with θ_p = e^{iτ}, τ = π/S, on one prime shell: (a) Re(averaged cluster) = Z·E_μ[cos(τΩ)\|Ĉ\|²], where μ is the product-geometric law of P-smooth m and Ω = number of prime factors. This equals −Z·Q + R with Q = E\|Ĉ\|² and R = Z·E[2cos²(πΩ/2S)\|Ĉ\|²] ≥ 0. (b) Twins never rescue: log\|g_p\| is concave near 0 and decreasing beyond it, so \|G(τ+x)\| + \|G(τ−x)\| ≤ 2\|G(τ)\|e^{−E(1−π²/S)}, using L² ≤ (π²/S)·2E (the Lean inequality). (c) In general R/(ZQ) ≤ (π²/2)Var_{P_C}(Ω)/S² + tail, where P_C is the \|Ĉ\|²-tilt of the Ω-law. Since Ω ~ S ± √S, rescue needs the tilt to move mass to \|Ω − S\| ≍ S, which forces Q ≲ (Σu)² e^{−cS}. Taking constant offsets as the model, Turán's lemma then needs ≳ cS = cC log γ members, and window variation (S over a range) needs more. Residual: clusters of ≳ log γ mutually aligned near-1 zeros, real parts equal to within 1/(log γ loglog γ), with offsets forming an exponentially precise annihilator of the Ω-bulk. VK density (polylog count near the VK boundary) does not exclude this | PARTIAL: identity [L]; twins and clusters of size o(log γ) excluded (paper) | R:JointAncestryClusterIdentity |
| 76 | Multi-shell refinement of the cluster residual (paper). Test on a union P ∪ P' of prime shells. The averaged cluster series factors as Σ_j u_j e_j(m₁)e'_j(m₂). For it to vanish on most of the bulk, the annihilator space W ⊂ ℂ^J of the P-characters must be invariant under the diagonal unitaries diag(e'_j(m₂)). Those generate a torus, so W is a sum of weight spaces: each class of members with equal P'-pattern must already annihilate on P. Aligned members still have P'-phases spread by ~√D over the bulk, so only near-exact equality classes survive. Iterating over L disjoint dyadic shells (L ≍ A loglog γ) and swapping the roles of the shells forces a grid: a rescuing cluster needs ≳ S^L = (log γ)^{c loglog γ} members, any two in the same class nearly perfectly Bohr-aligned on all shells but one. Still not excluded rigorously: VK density allows this for chains with η* ≫ (loglog γ)^{4/3}/(log γ)^{2/3}, and Bohr alignment on polylog primes at polynomial height differences is the open non-alignment problem | PARTIAL (paper): residual needs superpolylog grid clusters | — |
| 77 | Twin inequality (Lean). Let G(y) = Π_{p∈s}(1 − b_p e^{iy_p})⁻¹ with 0 ≤ b_p ≤ 1/10, cos τ ≥ 9/10, sin τ ≥ 0 and (Σb_p) sin²τ ≤ 1/100. Then \|G(τ+x)\| + \|G(τ−x)\| ≤ 2\|G(τ)\|·exp(−(1/16)Σ b_p(1 − cos x_p)) (jasTwin_sum_le). So when the design makes G(τ) negative real, the pair-averaged value is ≤ −\|G(τ)\|((u₁−u₂)² + 2u₁u₂(1 − e^{−E/16})) < 0 unless the patterns agree mod 2π (jasTwin_re_le). Exact ingredients: q(τ+x)q(τ−x) − q(τ)² = 4b(1−cos x)((1+b²)cos τ − b(1+cos x)); q(τ+x) − q(τ−x) = 4b sin τ sin x; cosh y ≤ e^{y²/2}; Cauchy–Schwarz with sin² ≤ 2(1 − cos). This is the rigorous twin case of ledger rows 74–75: a pair of aligned near-1 zeros cannot rescue the JAS test of its members on a prime shell with small b_p | PROVED | R:JointAncestryTwinInequality |
| 78 | Exponential coherence threshold (Lean). For a prime set P with b_p = p^{−σ} ≤ 1/100, S = Σb_p, and the phase design θ_p = e^{iπ/S}: Re Σ_{j,k} u_ju_k Π_p(1 − e^{iπ/S}ψ_{j,p}ψ̄_{k,p}b_p)⁻¹ ≤ −½ Σ_{j,k} u_ju_k Π_p(1 − ψ_{j,p}ψ̄_{k,p}b_p)⁻¹ + 3(Σ\|u_j\|)² Π_p(1−b_p)⁻¹ e^{−S/60} (jasCluster_re_le). Proof: cluster identity → cosine series Σ_m cos(πΩ(m)/S) m^{−σ}\|Ĉ(m)\|²; on 2S/3 ≤ Ω ≤ 4S/3 the cosine is ≤ −½; Chernoff tails via tilts (6/5)^Ω, (5/6)^Ω, with the new Euler product hasSum_jasEulerHom_of_lt for arbitrary local parameters ‖w_p‖p^{−σ} < 1 and only 1 − 1/x ≤ log x ≤ x − 1. Consequence: a cluster can cancel the negative JAS test of its members only if its untwisted average (a sum of squares) is ≤ 6e^{−S/60} times the fully coherent value (Σ\|u\|)²Π(1−b)⁻¹. This turns the paper conclusion of row 75 into a theorem: rescuing clusters must be exponentially incoherent on the test primes | PROVED | R:JointAncestryClusterCoherence |
| 79 | Lower bounds for cluster coherence (paper, FAILED). Tried four ways to show that exponential incoherence (row 78) forces an impossible cluster. (a) Schur/Hadamard: for disjoint shells P ⊔ P', the untwisted average is Σ_{m'} m'^{−σ} Untw_P(u∘e'(m')), so each shell's Gram matrix must be exponentially ill-conditioned. But any two members agreeing at one prime already make that prime's Gram singular, so the bound is vacuous. (b) Low-degree multi-indices carry μ-mass e^{−S}, which is the same scale as the threshold. (c) Turán/Remez in the constant-offset model needs K ≳ S/60·log C members, which a polylog count of near-1 zeros permits. (d) Nazarov's lemma for D(t) = Σ u_j e^{iΔ_j t}: the law of log m on bulk P-smooth m is dense at scale 1/γ (count γ^{C loglog γ}), but its Fourier transform at the aligned frequencies Δ_j is large by assumption, so Lebesgue transfer fails. Reading: every route to a lower bound needs equidistribution of P-smooth log m at the cluster ordinates, i.e. the non-alignment statement itself | FAILED (circular: needs non-alignment) | — |
| 80 | Cluster test = ordinate mean square over smooth numbers (Lean). The prime-phase pattern of a height shift Δ is p ↦ p^{iΔ}, and on P-smooth m its Euler coefficient is m^{iΔ}, via log m = Σ_p v_p(m) log p (jasEulerCoeff_jasHeightPattern). Hence the untwisted cluster average equals Σ_{m P-smooth} m^{−σ}\|D(log m)\|² with D(t) = Σ_j u_j e^{iΔ_j t} the ordinate sum of the cluster (sum_sum_prod_inv_eq_tsum_ordinates), and the coherence threshold reads: Re(twisted) ≤ −½ Σ_m m^{−σ}\|D(log m)\|² + 3(Σ\|u\|)²Π(1−p^{−σ})⁻¹e^{−S/60} (jasCluster_re_le_ordinates). Statement of the open core in classical terms: a Θ = 1 chain survives JAS only if, at every plateau record, the weighted ordinate sum of its cluster of near-1 zeros, Σ_ρ u_ρ m^{i(γ_ρ − γ*)}, is exponentially small in mean square over the P-smooth integers (P = test primes ≤ (log γ)^A). By row 79, proving this impossible requires equidistribution of {γ log p} for those ordinates, i.e. non-alignment | PROVED (reformulation) | R:JointAncestryClusterOrdinates |
| 81 | Dirichlet-polynomial form of the cluster test (Lean). The cluster identity and coherence threshold now hold for patterns in the closed polydisc (‖ψ_{j,p}‖ ≤ 1; refactored R:JointAncestryClusterIdentity/Coherence, with Euler products for arbitrary local parameters moved to the identity module). With the pattern p ↦ p^{−s} of a complex shift s, Re s ≥ 0 (R:JointAncestryClusterDirichlet): Σ_{j,k} u_ju_k Π_p(1 − p^{−(s_j + s̄_k)}p^{−σ})⁻¹ = Σ_{m P-smooth} m^{−σ}\|Σ_j u_j m^{−s_j}\|², and the twisted (design e^{iπ/S}) average is ≤ −½ of it + 3(Σ\|u\|)²Π(1−p^{−σ})⁻¹e^{−S/60}. Members may now have different real parts, and the family can be any finite set of zeros normalised by the farthest member. Open core in its final form: for a Θ = 1 chain the Dirichlet polynomial Σ_j u_j m^{−s_j} of each record's cluster of shifts would have to be exponentially small in mean square over the P-smooth integers | PROVED | R:JointAncestryClusterDirichlet |
| 82 | Global all-zeros test (paper). (a) For a zero ρ with β > ½ let s_ρ = 1 − ρ̄ (Re s_ρ = 1 − β ∈ (0, ½)). Take the design point w^{(ρ)}_p = e^{−iπ/S} p^{−(1−ρ)}, an interior point of modulus p^{−(1−β)}, so PSD gives D(w^{(ρ)}) ≥ 0 via the Poisson extension. Take the positive test measure κ = Σ_{β>½} \|w_ρ\| δ_{w^{(ρ)}} (finite since Σ\|w\| < ∞). By the mirror identity ℓ^{1−ρ̄}(w) = conj ℓ^ρ(w) (valid at interior w), D = (positive on-line part) + 2Re Σ_{β>½} w_ρ ℓ^ρ. Hence ∫D dκ is the cluster double sum over ALL off-line zeros with shifts s_ρ, up to A-side errors O(Σ_{shell} p^{−1}) and weight phases O(1/γ). With R:JointAncestryClusterDirichlet extended to countable families, PSD(1) implies, at every shell: E_μ\|Z_off(m)\|² ≤ 6(Σ_off\|w\|)² e^{−S/60} + O((Σ_off\|w\|)Φ(0)e^{−S}), where Z_off(m) = Σ_{β>½} \|w_ρ\| m^{−(1−ρ̄)}. This needs no record, plateau or cluster selection. (b) Writing E_μ\|Z\|² = Σ_k P(Ω=k)·u^T C^{∘k} u, with C the Gram matrix of p ↦ p^{−s_ρ} in L²(π), and noting that uniform radial dressing keeps C fixed while moving the bulk degree: PSD(1) forces u^TC^{∘k}u ≲ (Σu)²e^{−k/60} on a long range of k. Diagonal amplitudes are u_ρ² N^{−2η_ρ k}, so a zero is visible iff η_ρ log N ≤ 1/120. (c) Detectability limit: visibility plus head-over-bulk (k ≳ 240 log γ) plus shell capacity (k ≲ N^{½}/log N) are compatible iff η ≲ 1/(240 loglog γ). So JAS tests at σ = 1 with single-shell designs never see zeros with η ≫ 1/loglog γ. A Θ = 1 chain approaching 1 more slowly than 1/loglog γ is invisible to them, and must be handled through the record/spoiler structure. (d) For visible families, the VK exponents 2/3 (zero-free region) and 3/2 (density) make the needed non-alignment δ ≳ (log N)^{−3/2} and the available one (VK prime sums for log Δ ≤ c(log N)^{15/4}) scale identically. With the range parameter c small the visible part closes, except for members at heights beyond exp(c(log N)^{15/4}), which the e^{−k/60} weight cutoff does not remove at k ~ N^{½} | PARTIAL (paper): global test found; detectability limit η ≲ 1/loglog γ is intrinsic | — |
| 83 | Correction: the a-side factor (paper). The cluster analysis of rows 74–82 used only the b-side factors (1 − w̄_p b_p)⁻¹ of the JAS local density. The full factor is (1−ab)(1 − w a)⁻¹(1 − w̄ b)⁻¹. With member-specific dressing the a-side term of zero ρ' at design ρ is e^{−iπ/S}p^{−(1+c+ρ'−ρ)}, which is not of the form f(ρ)·conj f(ρ'); this asymmetry is what makes off-line zeros non-positive-definite in the first place. On a shell at scale N it perturbs each pair term at relative order ε_A = Σ_{p∈P}\|w_p a_p\| ≈ S/N (≈ 1/(4S log²N) on a single dyadic shell, S/N with radial dressing on larger shells). Consequences: (i) the Lean theorems (rows 75–81) stand as exact statements about the b-side kernel; (ii) the twin conclusion survives, since the margin π²/(2S) exceeds ε_A once N ≫ S; (iii) the "exponential incoherence" reading of rows 78–82 is wrong for actual zeros. A cluster evades the test once its untwisted average is ≲ ε_A·(fully coherent value), i.e. polynomial incoherence. Phase cancellation of order r gives ≈ S^{−r}, so with N = S^a, structured clusters of about a members suffice. Visibility η log N ≤ c then caps a at ≈ c/(η loglog γ). The global all-zeros test (row 82) loses its exponential threshold the same way. Residual: for chain zeros with η ≲ c/loglog γ, structured clusters of ≳ c/(η loglog γ) near-1 zeros with r-th order phase cancellation; slower chains stay invisible. STATUS entries renumbered (MUST-1201–1215, note 1216) after collisions with a parallel session | CORRECTION (residual weaker than claimed) | — |
| 84 | Exact a-side analysis (paper). For a cluster with a COMMON real part β, a uniform radius r symmetrises both sides. The a-side term of ρ' at design ρ is (θ̄ r p^{−(β+c)})^n p^{in(γ_ρ−γ')} and the b-side term is (θ r p^{−(1−β+c)})^{\|n\|}p^{−i\|n\|(γ_ρ−γ')}, both Hermitian. So the full two-sided JAS kernel averaged over the members' design points equals exactly Σ_{m,m' P-smooth} e_θ̄(m)e_θ(m') m^{−a}m'^{−b} \|Σ_ρ u_ρ e_ρ(m) ē_ρ(m')\|², and the exponential coherence threshold holds rigorously, with the phase τ(Ω(m') − Ω(m)) and Chernoff on both counts. For unequal real parts no choice of radii symmetrises both sides: Hermitian factorisation of the a-side forces r_ρ ∝ p^{−β_ρ}, the b-side forces r_ρ ∝ p^{−(1−β_ρ)}, and these agree only if β_ρ is constant. Replacing the a-side real exponent by the symmetric 1 + c leaves a correction p^{n(η_ρ'−η_ρ)}, i.e. relative error ≈ \|Δη\| N^{−½} per pair. Pairs whose visibility N^{−η} differs by more than about 0.18 are exponentially negligible (AM–GM gap (N^{−η}−N^{−η'})²/2 > 1/30). Pairs of comparable visibility but unequal real parts (\|Δη\| ≲ 1/log N) keep an error of order N^{−½}/log N, far above e^{−S/60}. Reading: exactly homogeneous clusters cannot rescue; a rescuing cluster needs real-part dispersion, which is the generic case. The a-side asymmetry is the off-line non-positive-definiteness itself, so the cluster method stops at polynomial incoherence. The cluster line is closed here: the residual (structured near-1 clusters with r-th order phase cancellation, real parts within 1/log N) needs a non-alignment theorem that no corpus or classical tool provides | CLOSED (method limit identified; NO POSITIVE SALVAGE beyond rows 75–81) | — |
| 85 | The global test is weaker than the prime number theorem (paper, no-go). The global off-line sum is Z_off(m) = Σ_{β>½}\|w_ρ\| m^{ρ̄−1}. Up to weight phases O(1/γ) it equals m^{−½}·conj Φ(log m) minus the on-line and mirror parts, and those are O(m^{−½}). Φ(log m) is arithmetic (integerLag: (m − ψ(m))/√m + Mertens terms), so for bulk P-smooth m (log m ≈ S log N), VK's PNT gives \|Z_off(m)\| ≲ e^{−c(S log N)^{3/5}} pointwise. The untwisted global average G₀·E_μ\|Z_off\|² is then far below the threshold (Σ_off\|w\|)²·(S/N + e^{−S/60}), because (Σ_off\|w\|)² is a fixed constant set by the lowest off-line zero. So for high clusters the global test certifies nothing beyond what PNT with the VK error term already implies, and it cannot yield quasi-RH. Reason: normalising by the total off-line weight makes the low zeros set the threshold, while the explicit formula forces the aggregate zero sum to be incoherent at large m. Local cluster tests avoid this normalisation but bring back cross terms with non-cluster zeros (rows 74–84) | FAILED (global test ≤ PNT strength) | — |
| 86 | Record and plateau selection against single and collective spoilers (paper, no-go). (a) Single spoilers die at records. By row 60, a bulk zero ρ' (distance δ') at relative height Δ = \|γ' − γ_t\| is aligned with the target only through a zero ρ'' within C/log N of 1 − (δ' − δ_t) + iΔ. If Δ < γ_t, then ρ'' lies below the target. If moreover 1 − β_t > C/log N + (δ' − δ_t), then ρ'' is farther from the line than the target, which contradicts the record property. For Δ ≤ γ_t^C, ρ'' contradicts the κ-plateau whenever 1 − β_t > (2κ + C/A)/loglog γ_t. So at plateau records that are not visible, no single zero spoils at polynomial height differences. (b) Collective spoilers survive. A zero of depth d = 1 − Re ρ'' contributes at most 2N^{−d/2}/\|ρ'' − s\| to the window sum. A unit window holds ≍ log Δ zeros, so ≍ log Δ zeros of depth ≤ 2 loglog Δ/log N = 2/A align the window without any zero deeper than the target. Jensen with the VK bound \|ζ(σ+it)\| ≤ t^{B(1−σ)^{3/2}}(log t)^{2/3} allows ≍ A^{−3/2} log Δ such zeros in a unit window, so collective spoilers are consistent with every unconditional count. (c) Larger shells do not help. With log N = (log γ)^θ the target needs super-plateau records: a deficit ≤ (log γ)^{−θ} up to height γ^{1+(log γ)^{θ−θ'}}. For chains with 1 − β ≍ (log γ)^{−θ'} these exist iff θ < θ′. VK non-alignment at polynomial Δ needs θ ≥ 2/3, and VK itself forces θ′ ≤ 2/3. (d) Changing σ does not help. At σ = 3/2 − η₀ the natural shell is log N ≍ loglog γ/(η₀ − (1 − β_t)), and plateau control forces η₀ − (1 − β_t) ≳ κ, so again log N = O(loglog γ). Verdict: the exponent gap of rows 69/72 is invariant under target selection (records, plateaus, super-plateaus), shell size and dressing level. JAS chain exclusion adds nothing to a zero-free region of exponent θ_Z except chains that hug its boundary | FAILED (exponent gap invariant; no positive salvage beyond (a)) | — |
| 87 | Two-sided cluster identity and threshold (Lean; positive survivor of row 84). On the circle the JAS local factor is (1 − ab)(1 − aw)⁻¹(1 − bw̄)⁻¹ (jasHoloLocal_eq_mul_inv_mul_inv). For members with a common real part (a-side p^{−a}ψ̄_k, b-side p^{−b}ψ_k), averaged over the design points θψ_j with real weights u_j u_k: Σ_{j,k} u_j u_k Π_p (1 − θ_pψ_{j,p}ψ̄_{k,p}p^{−a})⁻¹(1 − θ̄_pψ̄_{j,p}ψ_{k,p}p^{−b})⁻¹ = Σ_{m,n P-smooth} e_θ(m)ē_θ(n) m^{−a}n^{−b} \|Σ_j u_j e_{ψ_j}(m)ē_{ψ_j}(n)\|² (jasTwoSidedCluster_eq_tsum; patterns in the closed polydisc, Re a, Re b > 0). Threshold (jasClusterTwoSided_re_le): with b_p = p^{−σ} ≤ 1/100, S = Σ b_p, an a-side p^{−a} ≤ 1/100 with Σ_p p^{−a} ≤ S/100, and θ = e^{iπ/S}, Re(twisted) ≤ −½(untwisted) + 5(Σ\|u_j\|)²Π(1 − p^{−a})⁻¹Π(1 − p^{−σ})⁻¹e^{−S/60}. Proof: the term (m, n) has twist cos(π(Ω(n) − Ω(m))/S), which is ≤ −½ once Ω(m) ≤ S/12 and 3S/4 ≤ Ω(n) ≤ 4S/3; the three tails are Chernoff bounds with tilts 2^{Ω(m)}, (5/6)^{Ω(n)} and (6/5)^{Ω(n)} (the 3S/4 cut makes the lower tail exactly e^{−S/60}). For actual zeros, a = β + c and b = 1 − β + c with Σ_p p^{−(β+c)} ≪ S. This removes the a-side caveat of MUST-1211–1215 for clusters with exactly equal real parts; dispersed clusters keep the \|Δβ\|N^{−½} error of row 84 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryClusterTwoSided |
| 88 | Rescuing clusters are aligned or large (Lean + paper). Lean (R:JointAncestryClusterDichotomy). The untwisted cluster average is a Gram form whose diagonal entries all equal the coherent factor G = Π_p(1 − b_p)⁻¹. If every off-diagonal entry has modulus ≤ εG (pairwise non-alignment), the form is ≥ G((1+ε)Σu_j² − ε(Σ\|u_j\|)²) (re_sum_sum_ge_of_offdiag_le). With the coherence thresholds (rows 78, 87), a cluster whose twisted average is ≥ 0 satisfies Σu_j² ≤ (ε + 6e^{−S/60})(Σ\|u_j\|)² (jasCluster_sum_sq_le_of_nonaligned; 10 in place of 6 for the two-sided kernel with a common real part, jasClusterTwoSided_sum_sq_le_of_nonaligned). In ordinate form the alignment of a pair is the partial Euler product ratio \|Π_p(1 − p^{iΔ}p^{−σ})⁻¹\|/Π_p(1 − p^{−σ})⁻¹ at the height difference Δ (jasCluster_sum_sq_le_of_nonaligned_ordinates). Paper consequences. (a) Homogeneous clusters (exactly equal real parts) at depth η need effective size ≥ min(1/(2ε), e^{S/60}/12) ≈ γ^{c} with S ≍ C log γ. VK density allows ≤ γ^{Bη^{3/2}+o(1)} near-1 zeros of comparable weight. So once Bη^{3/2} < C/60, a rescuing homogeneous cluster must contain a pair with alignment defect Σ_p b_p(1 − cos(Δ log p)) ≤ (B/C + o(1))η^{3/2}S. Such pairs are ever more perfectly Bohr-aligned as the chain deepens, and VK equidistribution forces \|Δ\| > exp(c(log N)^{3/2}). (b) Dispersed clusters (real parts within 1/log N, not equal) carry the a-side error of row 84, so their threshold is polynomial and they need only effective size ≳ N^{1/2} = (log γ)^{A/2}. VK density allows that except for chains hugging the VK boundary, η ≲ (loglog γ/log γ)^{2/3}: there Ford's (log T)^{15} factor caps the count, and A > 32 excludes non-aligned rescue. Reading: the binding residual of the cluster line is real-part dispersion plus Bohr-aligned pairs at super-VK height differences. Counting alone excludes only chains that hug the zero-free region | PROVED (Lean dichotomy) / PARTIAL (paper) | R:JointAncestryClusterDichotomy |
| 89 | Producer side: attained large-prime positivity (Lean). If `σ₀ > 1`, `d_ρ < σ₀ − 1` for all zeros and `Σ_ρ \|w_ρ\|/(σ₀ − 1 − d_ρ) < ∞`, then one `X` gives positivity above `X` at every `σ ≥ σ₀`. Each zero adds at most `\|w_ρ\|(u^{−(σ₀−d)}v^{−σ₀} + u^{−σ₀}v^{−(σ₀−d)})` to `A_σ(u/v)`. Large-prime numerators and denominators are `1` or `> X`, so the off-diagonal row mass is `≤ Σ_ρ 2\|w_ρ\|((1+T_X(σ₀−d_ρ))(1+T_X(σ₀)) − 1)`, which tends to 0 by dominated convergence (`T_0(a) ≤ 1 + 1/(a−1)`); diagonal dominance against `Φ(0) > 0` finishes. Unconditional at `σ₀ = 3/2` (zero-free region `1/(½−d) ≤ 58 log\|γ\|`, `Σ\|w\| log(2+\|γ\|) < ∞`): one `X` for all `σ ≥ 3/2`. The corpus result was `∀σ > 3/2 ∃X(σ)`, with `X(σ) → ∞`; per-zero budgets cannot reach `3/2`. Under quasi-RH, a chain and `Σ\|w\|/(δ*−d) < ∞`, the abscissa `1 + δ*` is attained (`IsLeast`). Reading: with the farthest-zero exclusion, the large-prime producer at level σ is exactly "sup d < σ − 1" up to chains and boundary summability. JAS adds no positivity source on large primes; below 3/2 any producer is a zero-free half-plane theorem | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 89b | Large-prime producer law, hypothesis-free (Lean, same module). `jasDressedPSDAbove_zeroFree_or_edge`: positivity above X at σ ≥ 1 ⟹ every zero has `\|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ σ − 1`, or `sup \|Re ρ − ½\| = ½`. A farthest zero is ruled out by large-prime maximal exclusion; a chain with sup < ½ by the chain lower bound under its own half-plane bound. `largePrime_producer_law` packages both directions: strip `< σ−1` plus boundary summability ⟹ producer; producer ⟹ closed strip or edge accumulation. So a large-prime JAS producer below 3/2 IS a zero-free strip, modulo Θ = 1 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 90 | Endpoint exclusion and attainment (Lean). (i) Exponent-one twisted prime sums: for Δ ≠ 0 there is κ < 1 with `‖Σ_{p≤N} p^{−1+iΔ}‖ ≤ κ Σ_{p≤N} p^{−1}` eventually (`exists_twisted_jasPrimeRpowSum_one_le`). Proof: Abel summation against `k^{−1/2}`. Both sums run through the same weights `ω_k = (k^{−1/2} − (k+1)^{−1/2})k^{1/2}/log k`, with twisted factor `1/\|½+iΔ\| < 2` and untwisted factor 2, from the corpus exponent-½ asymptotics. (ii) Large-prime maximal exclusion extended in place to `σ ≤ 1 + d₀`, endpoint included (`not_jasDressedPSDAbove_of_maximal_offLine_of_le`; strict versions kept as corollaries). (iii) With a farthest zero, the producer set is exactly the open ray `(1 + d₀, ∞)` (`largePrime_producer_set_of_farthest`). A least level excludes farthest zeros; off RH it forces a chain (`jasOffLineChain_of_isLeast_largePrime`). With row 89: under quasi-RH the producer set is closed iff the chain case holds, summable boundary series sufficing | PROVED | R:JointAncestryPrimeSumOneLine; R:JointAncestryLargePrimeMaximalExclusion; R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 90b | Small primes cost a strict amount (Lean). The all-prime abscissa σ* is attained (PSD is closed). With a farthest zero, the large-prime set is the open ray (1+d₀, ∞), so σ* > 1 + d₀ strictly (`lt_jasAbscissa_of_farthest`). Off RH, σ* ≤ 1 + sup d forces a chain (`jasOffLineChain_of_jasAbscissa_le_one_add_sSup`). Reading: equality of all-prime and large-prime abscissae is a chain signature | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 90c | Single-level large-prime capstone (Lean). RH ⟺ (∃X, JasDressedPSDAbove X 1) ∧ (some half-plane bound α < ½). The corpus form needed all σ > 1. More generally, under quasi-RH, large-prime positivity at σ ≥ 1 gives the half-plane bound σ − 1 (`xiZeroHalfPlaneBound_of_jasDressedPSDAbove_of_quasi`). Reading: modulo Θ = 1, one large-prime level is a zero-free strip | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 91 | Log-window producer (Lean, MUST-1260). For σ > 1, κ < σ − 1: positivity on X-rough characters with pairwise log-ratios ≤ κ log X, for all large X. Proof: Gershgorin with the strip bound e^{\|L\|/2} on the window, plus the pair-sum bound (1+T)²−1 with T_X(σ) ≤ X^{−(σ−1)/2}(1+2/(σ−1)). Reading: below 3/2 the unconditional producers are local in log-scale (windows ∝ (σ−1) log X) or in dressing (σ ≥ 3/2). The receiver's amplifying tests use log-diameters ≫ log X, outside both regions. Paper kills this cycle (no Lean): Landau Ω-results for a prime-side receiver need cross-scale coherence (chain obstruction); twist-averaged (dlVP-type) producers only reach short-support Weil positivity; FE-reflected prime-Poisson laws give only Kakutani measure-class rigidity, no sign | PROVED (positive survivor) | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 92 | Producer channels, round 2 (paper kills). (a) Localized aggregates (Gaussian height weights) do not localize the amplification: a far zero with d > σ−1 still has unbounded large-prime amplification, so the localized producer law equals the global one. (b) VK is in the corpus (`riemannZeta_ne_zero_vinogradovKorobov`, `exists_xiZero_re_le_vinogradovKorobov`), but it improves the log-window producer only by O((log X)^{3/5}) and does not move the 3/2 threshold (no half-plane). (c) Hecke/translation invariance of the Gram gives nothing (any translation-invariant kernel commutes). (d) An exact prime-side identity making Z_{3/2}(τ) a square fails: the contour at Re s = 3/2 meets ζ(2−s) inside the strip (wall 1). (e) The partial receiver result for Θ = 1 (VK-hugging chains, row 88) needs a Ford-type density N(1−κ,T) ≪ T^{Cκ^{3/2}}(log T)^{C}. The corpus has only Ingham's density (Hoheisel tower), so (e) is a long infrastructure project | KILLED (a,c,d); BLOCKED (e) | — |
| 92b | Producer law in Weil form (Lean). For σ > ½, large-prime JAS positivity ⟺ Weil's lag form ≥ 0 on Euler vectors e_w(m)m^{−σ} over finite sets of primes > X. Consequences: one X gives Weil positivity at every exponent ≥ 3/2; Weil positivity on large-prime Euler vectors at exponent σ ≥ 1 forces the strip \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ σ − 1 unless Θ = 1; RH ⟺ (Weil positivity on large-prime harmonic Euler vectors) ∧ quasi-RH. The last puts the JAS single-level capstone into the WL/RW Weil language | PROVED | R:JointAncestryWeilEulerProducerLaw |
| 93 | Few-primes producer at the target level (Lean). For σ ≥ 1, positivity holds on every set of at most (7/5)√X primes > X, any exponents (`jasDressedPSDOn_of_card_le_sqrt`). Per-zero width at σ = 1 is ≤ p^{−1/2} ≤ X^{−1/2} (corpus sharp bound), so the budget π/2 − 1/14 holds. Three unconditional local producers are now in Lean: dressing (σ ≥ 3/2), log-scale (log-ratio ≤ κ log X, κ < σ−1), and number of primes (≤ (7/5)√X at σ = 1). Detecting an off-line zero at σ = 1 needs ≫ √X log γ primes with large log-ratios, outside all three | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 94 | Random subset-twist designs against Bohr-pair rescue (paper). Idea: replace the uniform twist e^{iπ/S} with twists π/2 on random prime subsets T (P(p∈T) = q_p, Σ q_p W_p ≈ π). These form an exponentially large family of mutually distant patterns, so a partner matching one pattern should miss the rest. The average over T factors (independence across primes): E_T F_ρ = w_ρ Π_p[(1−q_p)ℓ_p^ρ(a_p) + q_p ℓ_p^ρ(i a_p)]. Same-depth aligned partners reinforce; different-depth aligned partners are cancelled on average by band selection, since their phase ratio p^{α−α'} varies across bands. KILLED, however, by phase additivity. A partner whose relative Kronecker pattern is a uniform offset ψ_p ≈ π/S (total weighted phase π) sits at phase (twist phase) − π ≡ 0 under every design with twist phase ≡ π. Its magnitude reduction e^{−2Σ_T r} matches the target's, so one such partner rescues all subset twists at once. Positive survivor (universal-rescuer observation): every design's negativity is a phase-π condition, and a π-offset partner cancels all of them. This explains why the Bohr-pair residual (rows 86–88) is robust under all twist designs: excluding it needs arithmetic non-alignment (B2), not better designs | KILLED (survivor: universal rescuer) | — |
| 95 | Zero repulsion against the Bohr-pair residual (paper). Deuring–Heilbronn-type repulsion near the 1-line excludes same-depth partners of a zero at depth κ within height distance ≈ exp(c/κ). It is not in the corpus. VK twist control excludes alignment at distances with log Δ ≤ (log N)^{3/2}. At the target's visibility scale (log N ≈ η^{−1} loglog γ for strongly active targets), the uncovered range is Δ ∈ [exp(c/κ), γ]. Barely active targets (critical dressing, κ ≈ η) need log N ≈ γ^c, which puts every comparable-weight partner inside VK range. But then farther zeros are amplified doubly-exponentially and spoil unless they are doubly-exponentially higher. VK-hugging chains violate this. Reading: repulsion plus VK leaves exactly the row-86 exponent gap; [B] needs both B2-align and a growth law for farther zeros | REDUCED (no closure) | — |
| 91b | Log-window producer sharpened (Lean). With the corpus tail bound `tsum_shift_rpow_le` (T_X(σ) ≤ X^{1−σ}/(σ−1)), the window becomes κ log X for every κ < 2(σ−1), optimal for the strip-bound method (the window costs e^{a/2} against tail X^{1−σ}). Detection at σ < 3/2 on large primes needs log-ratios beyond 2(σ−1) log X | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 96 | Low-dressing and small-prime channel for [B] (paper). At σ = ½ + ε, zeros near the 1-line have near-unit local radii p^{−(κ+ε)}, so each prime contributes phase ≈ π/2 and amplification ≈ 2/((κ+ε) log p). About log γ / loglog γ small primes then suffice for detection (versus ≫ √X log γ large primes at σ = 1). Rescue still needs partners aligned on the primes p ≲ log γ. The Bohr box {Δ ≤ γ : p^{iΔ} ≈ e^{iψ_p}, p ≤ y} has expected measure γ·ε^{π(y)}, so a heuristic count against Ingham's γ^{3κ} near-edge zeros predicts no partners. A proof needs to place specific zero differences outside these boxes. Weyl/Erdős–Turán discrepancy, using the trivial bound \|log(a/b)\| ≥ 1/max(a,b), and Baker-type linear forms (corpus `general Baker`) constrain alignment only on average, or for exact rational relations with offset ψ = 0. They do not constrain a uniform offset ψ ≈ π/S. So B2 is unchanged; the PSD(½) ⟹ RH form of [B] is equally blocked | REDUCED (no closure) | — |
| 97 | Window sweeping against universal rescuers (paper). For windows W₁ ⊂ W₂ = (X, N] ⊂ (X, N'] with uniform twists θ_W = π/S_W, one partner rescues both only if Σ_{W₂∖W₁} W_p ψ_p ≡ 0 (mod 2π). Its alignment on W₂ forces ψ_p ≈ π/S_{W₂} there, so the sum is ≈ π S_{W₂∖W₁}/S_{W₂}, a contradiction once the ratio lies in (ε, 1−ε). Near-tie partners (ψ ∝ log p): sweeping X makes their phase Δ Ψ(X) average to 0, so they cannot rescue every X unless Δ Ψ_max ≪ 1, and then they reinforce. Consequence: rescue needs a separate uniform-offset Bohr partner for each essentially distinct window, ≳ log(S_spoil/S_vis)/ε of them across the target's plateau. Local counts do not forbid this (far partners are uncounted). Residual sharpened from one B2 pair to a window-indexed family of B2 pairs; not closed | REDUCED (sharpened residual) | — |
| 93b | Few-primes producer at every level (Lean). For σ ≥ 1, positivity on every set of at most (7/10) X^{σ−½} primes > X (`jasDressedPSDOn_of_card_le_rpow`). Each local width at dressing c ≥ ½ is ≤ 2p^{−c} (`jasPhaseWidth_le_two_exp`), and the per-zero budget π/2 − 1/14 holds. The admissible count interpolates from √X (σ = 1) to X (σ → 3/2), where the global producer takes over | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 93c | Few-primes producer under a half-plane bound (Lean). If Re ρ ≤ ½ + α (α ≤ ½), then for σ ≥ 1 positivity holds on every set of ≤ (7/10) X^{σ−α} primes > X (`jasDressedPSDOn_of_card_le_rpow_of_halfPlane`, via `jasPhaseWidth_le_two_exp_of_halfPlane`: width ≤ 2p^{−(c+½−α)}).
