Dictionary: the zero-free-region exponent α fixes the prime-count exponent σ − α of positive tests. α = ½ is the unconditional case X^{σ−½}; RH (α = 0) gives X^σ. A farthest zero at depth d₀ breaks positivity at counts X^{1−d₀+ε} (σ = 1, paper), so at σ = 1 the exponent 1 − d₀ = 3/2 − Θ is sharp in farthest-zero configurations | PROVED (paper sharpness) | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment |
| 98 | Producer variants, round 3 (paper kills). (a) Degree-1 tests (characters = single primes > X): Gershgorin fails for σ ≤ 3/2, since row p sees Σ_{q>p} q^{½−σ} = ∞. Off-line zeros are detected with polynomial (not exponential) amplification (Σ_p p^{−(σ−δ)})² against weight γ^{−2}, so degree-1 positivity below 3/2 is also ZFR-hard. (b) Squarefree/Bernoulli tests: ZFR-hard (rows 10, 15b). (c) Character twists χ(a)χ̄(b) of the dressing are PD multipliers with PD inverses, so they give the same positivity. (d) Complex dressing breaks Hermiticity. (e) σ-analytic continuation of finite-P densities: the singularities sit exactly at σ = \|Re ρ − ½\|, a restatement of zero locations, not a producer. Every channel either equals the zero-free-strip law or is invariant | KILLED | — |
| 99 | Bohr-weighted zero density against the Θ = 1 residual (paper). Idea: bound the competitors' alignment collectively rather than one by one. The weighted count Σ_{near-edge ρ'} W(γ') over a Bohr majorant W = Π_{p≤y}(1 + Re c_p p^{−iγ}) of degree 1 per prime, via y-rough zero detectors, would equal (Ingham count) × (mean of W). The orthogonality needs nu = mv to be impossible, plus dual length M·P(y) ≤ T^{1−δ}. Aligned near-edge zeros then number ≤ T^{Cκ}·2^{−π(y)}, which is 0 for κ ≤ c/loglog T. KILLED as a closure of [B]. (i) Uncertainty limit: mean values certify the Bohr structure only for prime sets with Π p ≤ T, so π(y) ≤ log T/loglog T and the saving is at most exp(−c log T/loglog T). (ii) Detection of a target at the VK depth κ ≈ (log γ)^{−2/3} needs amplification γ² at dressing σ → ½, i.e. y ≈ 3 log γ, beyond the limit by an exponent factor 3. At σ = 1 it needs y ≈ (log γ)². (iii) With localized (Gaussian height) weights the threshold drops to the window count, but the window length H then caps y at log H, and closing forces log H ≲ (log γ)^{2/3}: exactly the VK width. Positive survivor: the exponent-gap invariance of row 86 extends to collective (density) control and to weight localization. Mean-value methods see at most π(log T) primes, and every detection scale for near-edge zeros lies beyond that, at the VK exponent | KILLED (survivor: uncertainty-limited Bohr saving) | — |
| 100 | Product-test capstone (Lean). The receivers use only rank-one Bernoulli product tests: the farthest-zero exclusion at the twisted Kronecker point, and the log-weight window tests of the chain exclusion. New predicate `JasProductPositiveAbove X σ`: positivity of Re Σ_ρ w_ρ Π_{p∈P}(1 + z_p A_{ρ,p} + z̄_p B_{ρ,p} + \|z_p\|²) for every finite P of primes > X and every z ∈ ℂ^P. Proved: the maximal exclusion for σ ≥ ½ (`not_jasProductPositiveAbove_of_maximal_offLine_of_le`); the chain bound (`one_add_sSup_le_of_jasProductPositiveAbove_of_chain`); the producer dichotomy (`jasProductPositiveAbove_zeroFree_or_edge`); RH ⟺ (∃X, product positivity at σ=1) ∧ quasi (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasProductPositiveAbove_one_and_quasi`); and the hierarchy collapse (block-positive ⟹ PSD under quasi, `jasDressedPSD_one_of_exists_jasProductPositiveAbove_of_quasi`). The PSD versions are now corollaries, with no duplicated proofs. MUST-1267 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAttainment, R:JointAncestryLargePrimeMaximalExclusion, R:JointAncestryLargePrimeAbscissa |
| 101 | Cosine-kernel form of the JAS producer (Lean). Product tests at \|z_p\| = r_p are nonnegative combinations, over subsets T ⊆ P, of unit-modulus tests (factor (1−r)² + 2r·unit). Unit-modulus aggregates are continuous in the phases (domination 2^{\|T\|}\|w_ρ\|). Kronecker density (`primeTorus_kronecker_dense`) puts the phases on the line p^{it}, where the unit factor is 1 + p^{−σ}cosh((ρ−it−½)log p). Hence JasProductPositiveAbove X σ ⟺ JasCosinePositiveAbove X σ for σ ≥ ½, and RH ⟺ (∃X: Re Σ_ρ K_P(ρ−it)/(ρ(1−ρ)) ≥ 0 ∀ finite P ⊂ primes > X, ∀ t) ∧ quasi. K_P > 0 on the critical line (`jasCosineKernel_critical_pos`). MUST-1270. Reading: the minimal JAS producer target is a one-parameter Weil positivity against translates of large-prime cosine products. Equivalently, Φ is positive along the symmetric prime walk (steps ±log p, weights p^{−1}/2) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryPrimeCosineKernel |
| 102 | Ω-balanced ratios against the universal rescuer (paper). Suppose a partner at height difference Δ is ε-aligned with a uniform offset ψ on a window W: Δ log p ∈ ψ + 2πℤ ± ε for p ∈ W. Then for W-smooth u, v with Ω(u) = Ω(v) = k the offset cancels: Δ log(u/v) ∈ 2πℤ ± 2kε. For 2kε < ½ this excludes Δ ∈ (4πkε, π)/\|log(u/v)\|. The row-94 universal rescuer needs ε ≈ 1/S, so k up to S/8 is allowed, and products of k ≈ S/log Y primes reach exclusion heights exp(cS). That is exactly the range where a rescuer's weight could compete. Corpus Baker (row 96) misses this because it treats the offset as a free parameter; Ω-balance removes it. Gaps. (i) Covering every scale needs ratio-density of Ω-balanced W'-products, with W' the aligned primes for that Δ (a dense, Δ-dependent subset). That is a short-interval statement for k-fold prime products and is not proved. (ii) It controls only near-perfect alignment (ε ~ 1/S). The binding residual of row 88, dispersed clusters, needs η-level non-alignment (a positive fraction of weighted mass misaligned). The trick then gives k = O(1) and heights Y^{O(1)}, inside the VK range already. Verdict: single near-perfect rescuers are excludable modulo product ratio-density; collective dispersed rescue is untouched, so [B] does not move | REDUCED (survivor: offset-cancelling Ω-balanced ratios; open: product ratio-density) | — |
| 103 | Single-prime level law (Lean). Single-prime test at p, level σ: (1+\|z\|²)Φ(0) + 2Re z·p^{−σ}Φ(log p). Positivity on primes > X ⟺ \|Φ(log p)\| ≤ Φ(0)p^σ. Below ½ these tests are equivalent to quasi-RH: Hoheisel interpolation plus the growth criterion give Re ρ ≤ ½ + max(σ,¼); a half-plane α gives \|Φ\| ≤ e^{αL}Σ\|w\|. At σ = 0 they are RH, by the corpus prime-lag criterion. JAS-internal capstone: RH ⟺ ∃X: single-prime positivity at some σ < ½ ∧ product/cosine positivity at σ = 1. MUST-1272. Reading: the JAS family has a division of labour. The WL one-sided prime bound is JAS at σ = 0; quasi-RH is single primes below ½; RH-given-quasi is multi-prime at σ = 1. The single-prime abscissa equals sup\|Re ρ − ½\| once that is ≥ ¼, and is 0 iff RH | PROVED | R:JointAncestrySinglePrimeLevel |
| 104 | Level monotonicity and the single-condition capstone (Lean). A Bernoulli factor at level σ′ is the factor at level σ evaluated at p^{−(σ′−σ)}z, plus the constant \|z\|²(1 − p^{−2(σ′−σ)}) ≥ 0. Expanding over subsets shows product positivity is monotone up in σ (`JasProductPositiveAbove.mono_level`). Hence RH ⟺ ∃X ∃σ ∈ [0,½): product positivity on primes > X at σ (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasProductPositiveAbove_lt_half`). The product abscissa takes no value in (0,½): it is 0 under RH, ≥ ½ off RH, ≥ 1 + δ* under quasi-RH, and ≤ 3/2 unconditionally | PROVED | R:JointAncestrySinglePrimeLevel |
| 105 | Unconditional end of the single-prime scale (Lean). e^{−L/2}\|Φ(L)\| → 0, by dominated convergence with Re ρ < 1. Hence single-prime positivity at σ = ½ holds on all large primes unconditionally (`exists_jasSinglePrimePositiveAbove_half`). The single-prime abscissa lies in [0, ½]: below ½ ⟺ quasi-RH, equal to 0 ⟺ RH. The single-prime scale is the PNT scale: ½ is PNT (o(x) error), [0,½) is a power-saving PNT, 0 is RH. [B] reads: does multi-prime positivity at σ = 1 push the single-prime abscissa below its PNT value ½? | PROVED | R:JointAncestrySinglePrimeLevel |
| 106 | Cosine equivalence at every level, and the cosh-square criterion (Lean). The unit-factor bound 1 + p^{\|σ−½\|} replaces the uniform bound 2, so product ⟺ cosine positivity at every σ. Hence RH ⟺ ∃X ∃σ ∈ [0,½): cosine positivity on primes > X (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasCosinePositiveAbove_lt_half`). At σ = 0, K_P(s) = ∏ 2cosh²((s−½)log p/2) (`jasCosineKernel_zero`), so RH ⟺ ∃X: Re Σ_ρ ∏_{p∈P}cosh²((ρ−½−it)log p/2)/(ρ(1−ρ)) ≥ 0 for all P, t (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasCosinePositiveAbove_zero`). Expanded, this is Weil's lag form on the divisor vectors (d^{−it})_{d\|N} of squarefree X-rough N. The single-prime case N = p already carries everything (the prime-lag criterion), so multi-prime N add nothing at σ = 0 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryPrimeCosineKernel, R:JointAncestrySinglePrimeLevel |
| 107 | [B] at the boundary level σ = ½ (paper). At σ = ½ a near-edge zero (depth ½ − η) has cosine factor 1 + ½p^{−η}e^{iφ_p}. Its amplification exp(½Σ_P p^{−η}) is unbounded in Y (P = primes in (X, Y]), with no e^{1/η} cutoff. Competitors with generic phases have log-magnitude mean 0 by Jensen (E log\|1 + a e^{iφ}\| = 0 for \|a\| < 1). VK gives twisted-sum cancellation for \|γ′ − t\| ≤ exp((log Y)^{3/2}). Pairs of primes from a dense aligned subset, with Huxley short intervals, exclude weighted-aligned partners only up to Y^{O(1)}, which is inside the VK range. The live competitors are deeper zeros at heights with log γ′ ∈ [(log Y)^{3/2}, Y^{1−η′}/log Y]. Their twisted prime sums at Δ = γ′ − t are large only if ζ has near-edge zeros near 1 + iΔ, which is exactly the record/collective-spoiler analysis of row 86. Verdict: σ = ½ reproduces the σ = 1 obstruction. The only differences are that amplification is uncapped in Y and that single coefficients are uninformative at both levels | KILLED (reduces to row 86) | — |
| 108 | Level dictionary on [¼,½) (Lean) and a t-averaging no-go (paper). Lean: any half-plane bound α < σ gives single-prime positivity at level σ on large primes (`exists_jasSinglePrimePositiveAbove_of_xiZeroHalfPlaneBound_of_lt`). With row 103, for ¼ ≤ σ < ½ the single-prime level equals the half-plane level, up to the boundary (`jasSinglePrime_level_eq_halfPlane_level`). Paper: averaging the cosine test of a zero over t with a bump φ of width h replaces e^{(ρ−½)L_k} by e^{(ρ−½)L_k}φ̂(L_k). A bump with compactly supported transform cuts the walk at \|L_k\| ≤ 1/h, so the averaged amplification is ≤ e^{1/(2h)}·∏(1+1/p). Beating the weight ratio γ² needs h ≤ 1/(4 log γ), below the zero spacing. So any mean-value (t-averaged) control of competitors destroys the amplification it would have to certify; [B] needs information about individual ordinates. This is the formal content of the uncertainty limit of row 99 | PROVED (Lean part); KILLED (averaging route) | R:JointAncestrySinglePrimeLevel |
| 109 | Infrastructure for [B]: VK growth at every depth (Lean). ‖ζ(σ+iτ)‖ ≤ C·log τ·exp(864·κ^{3/2}·log τ·loglog τ) for w(log τ) ≤ κ = 1 − σ ≤ 1/(3·2^24), with C = 8 + 4(12e^{20}+2761) (`RHTower.Sieve.norm_riemannZeta_le_vinogradovKorobov_general`). The corpus proof is adapted block by block. The dyadic saving δ/4 ≥ ℓ²/(746496 L² log² L) is σ-free. κℓ − ℓ³/A ≤ κ^{3/2}√A with √A = 864 L log L. The derivative test absorbs Y^κ. The initial segment is absorbed because κ ≥ w(L). This is the input for Jensen counts of zeros near Re s = 1, the unconditional constraint in the collective-spoiler analysis of row 86. Card: vk-growth-general-sigma | PROVED (growth part) | R:Sieve/VinogradovZetaGrowthGeneralSigma |
| 110 | Local zero counts next to the one-line (Lean). The zeros of ζ in the disc of radius 5κ/2 around 1 + 2κ + iτ number ≤ log(M/m)/log(6/5) = O(κ^{3/2}L log L + log L + log(1/κ)) (`zeta_disc_zeroCount_le_near_one_line`). Proof: Jensen (`HTGeometry.sum_divisor_le_of_bounds`). The sphere bound uses corpus VK within the width and row 109's general-depth bound beyond it. The centre bound is the corpus quartic lower bound ‖ζ(1+x+iτ)‖⁴ ≥ x³/(756 log τ). Reading: a unit window may hold ≍ √κ·L log L zeros of depth ≤ κ, so the collective spoilers of row 86 (≍ L zeros at depth ≤ 2/A) are consistent with every unconditional local count, now formally. [B] still needs input beyond VK–Jensen | PROVED | R:ZetaNearOneLineLocalZeroCount |
| 111 | [B] for abstract configurations becomes a prime-Diophantine question (paper). For an abstract FE-symmetric zero configuration obeying Riemann–von Mangoldt, VK and Ingham density, rescuing a near-edge target needs partners whose ordinate differences Δ lie in an η-Bohr set of {log p : p ∈ P}. Here P is the large-prime window that makes the target dominate its weight, and the η-Bohr set is where the short Euler product over P is near-maximal with a uniform offset. Ingham's count allows ≈ γ^{3κ} near-edge partners, enough for collective rescue once 3κ ≥ 2η, provided the η-Bohr set has elements at heights ≲ γ^{O(1)}. So abstract [B] ⟺ no resonance of short prime Euler products at moderate heights, a property of the primes, not of ζ's zeros. Mean values cannot decide it at any height: the 2k-th moment over [0,T] needs T ≥ (length)^k, and then the measure bound T(k/S_eff)^k ≥ 1 never drops below the persistence length 1/log N of a resonance. Reading: within the JAS framework the obstruction to [B] lives in the Diophantine structure of prime logarithms. Actual ζ-zeros carry extra structure (they are where ζ vanishes), and any proof of [B] must use it | REDUCED (survivor: [B]_abstract ⟺ prime Euler-product non-resonance) | — |
| 112 | Power-saving prime-lag criterion and the sharp single-prime law (Lean). If a_p = Φ(log p) ≤ M p^σ on the primes p > X with 0 ≤ σ < ½, every zero has Re ρ ≤ ½ + σ (`re_le_half_add_of_primeLag_le_rpow'`; the corpus had only σ = 0). Proof for σ ≤ ¼: the corpus Landau argument with coefficients (Mp^σ − a_p)Λ(p)/p on primes > X. Their series splits into M·Σ_{p>X} p^σΛ(p)/p·p^{−s}, absolutely convergent on Re s > σ; the prime-only series at M = 0 (continuation primeOnlyG 0); and a finite small-prime sum. Assuming Θ > ½ + σ, every point used lies in Re s > σ. For σ ≥ ¼ the growth bound gives it. Consequence: the single-prime JAS level equals sup Re ρ − ½ exactly. Single-prime positivity at σ ⟹ half-plane σ, and half-plane α < σ ⟹ single-prime positivity at σ (`jasSinglePrime_level_eq_halfPlane_level_sharp`); the ¼ artifact of row 103 is removed | PROVED | R:PrimeLagLandauPowerSaving |
| 113 | Power-saving criterion from below, and one-vector JAS tests (Lean). If Φ(log p) ≥ −M p^σ on the primes p > X with 0 ≤ σ < ½, every zero has Re ρ ≤ ½ + σ (`re_le_half_add_of_primeLag_ge_rpow`; the corpus had only σ = 0). Proof: the lag-primitive transfer from the prime nearest below e^L (Huxley, gap 2e^{−2L/5}) gives Φ(L) ≥ −K e^{max(σ,1/10)L}. The corpus lower growth criterion then settles σ ≥ 1/10. For σ < 1/10 the first pass gives Θ ≤ 3/5, so |ψ(x) − x| ≪ x^{13/20} and the loss becomes O(e^{−L/4}). With row 112: each one-sided power-saving bound at the primes is exactly a zero-free half-plane. In JAS terms the single-prime test at level σ needs only one vector: z = 1 alone (`xiZeroHalfPlaneBound_of_jasSinglePrime_plus`) or z = −1 alone (`..._minus`) gives Re ρ ≤ ½ + σ, and RH ⟺ ∃X: the z = 1 single-prime aggregate at level 0 is ≥ 0 on all primes > X (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasSinglePrime_plus_zero`) | PROVED | R:PrimeLagPowerSavingLowerBound |
| 114 | Complexity–abscissa law (Lean). Each Bernoulli factor is (1+\|z\|²)(1+u) with \|u\| ≤ \|z\|(\|A\|+\|B\|)/(1+\|z\|²) ≤ X^{−(σ−α)} under \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ α ≤ σ. Hence the product test on P (primes > X) is ≥ ∏(1+\|z_p\|²)·(Φ(0) − ((1+X^{−(σ−α)})^{\|P\|} − 1)Σ\|w_ρ\|) (`jasProductAggregate_re_ge_of_halfPlane`). Consequences. (i) Few-primes producer at every level σ ≥ α: positivity on all sets of ≤ c X^{σ−α} primes, c = log(1 + Φ(0)/Σ\|w\|) (`jasProductPositiveAboveCard_of_halfPlane_of_le`). Unconditionally this holds for every σ ≥ ½ (`jasProductPositiveAboveCard_half`), extending row 93b (full PSD, σ ≥ 1) to product tests on [½, 1). (ii) For fixed k ≥ 1 and 0 ≤ σ < ½, k-prime positivity above some X ⟹ half-plane σ, and half-plane α < σ ⟹ k-prime positivity above some X (`exists_jasProductPositiveAboveCard_iff`). So every bounded complexity has abscissa Θ − ½, the single-prime value; RH ⟺ ∃X: level-0 positivity on all k-sets (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasProductPositiveAboveCard_zero`). Reading: off RH, the unrestricted product abscissa Θ + ½ (rows 89–90) exceeds every bounded-complexity abscissa by exactly 1. That extra 1 is carried only by tests on ≫ X^{σ−α} primes, with matching failure at X^{σ−d₀+ε} in farthest-zero configurations (row 93c, paper). Both [B] and RH-given-quasi live entirely in the high-complexity regime | PROVED | R:JointAncestryProductComplexity |
| 115 | Zero-sum control of product tests and an unconditional producer beyond X^{σ−½} primes (Lean). Per zero: \|A\|,\|B\| ≤ p^{−(σ−\|Re ρ−½\|)}, so the product test on P (primes > X) is ≥ ∏(1+\|z_p\|²)·(Φ(0) − Σ_ρ\|w_ρ\|((1+X^{−(σ−\|Re ρ−½\|)})^{\|P\|} − 1)) whenever \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ σ for all ρ (`jasProductAggregate_re_ge`; the half-plane form of row 114 is now a corollary). Each zero's excess is ≤ exp(K X^{\|Re ρ−½\|−½}) − 1 for \|P\| ≤ K X^{σ−½}, which → 0 because \|Re ρ − ½\| < ½. Dominated convergence (bound \|w_ρ\|(e^K − 1)) then gives: for σ ≥ ½ and every K, positivity on all sets of ≤ K X^{σ−½} primes > X once X is large (`eventually_jasProductPositiveAboveCard_of_le_mul_rpow`). This improves the unconditional count c√X at σ = 1 (row 93b) to ω(√X), with no zero-free region used. Reading: the unconditional JAS complexity is X^{σ−½}·g(X) with g → ∞; the growth rate of g is exactly a zero-free-region statement (dVP: g ≈ log X/loglog X; VK: g ≈ (log X)^{3/2−o(1)}, paper; half-plane α: g = X^{½−α}) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryProductComplexity |
| 116 | Torus-measure receivers for Θ = 1 (paper, killed). Idea: product positivity ⟺ positivity of f_P(θ) = Σ_ρ w_ρ F_{d_ρ}(θ + γ_ρ ℓ) on the torus 𝕋^P (ℓ = (log p)), every zero contributing a translate of one function F_d. Average f_P over the target's large-negative set T and bound the others by Hölder, ‖F_d‖_q ≤ exp(cqΣ_p p^{2d−2}) (finite for d < ½, uniformly in P), with no phase control of competitors. Killed. (i) With q = 2 the target itself sits in the "others" bound, and R√\|T\| ≤ ‖F_{d_t}‖₂ (Chebyshev). (ii) Large deviations give \|T\| ≈ c(λ)^{\|P\|}, which forces q ≳ Y^{1−d_t}. Then competitors closer to the 1-line (η_j < (½+η_t)/2) have ‖F‖_q ≈ exp(K Y^{½+η_t−2η_j}), unbounded against 1/γ_j². (iii) Union bounds over competitors' large-value sets reproduce the depth/height exponent tradeoff of rows 86–88. Universality-type approximations by Euler products fail the same way: far zeros see F only through \|F(d+iτ)\| ≤ ∏(1+\|z_p\|p^{d−σ}). Verdict: measure relaxations on the torus are equivalent to the record/plateau analysis; no new handle on [B] | KILLED | — |
| 117 | Prime-budget producer (Lean, unconditional). Hypothesis-free zero-sum bound: the product test on any finite P is ≥ ∏(1+\|z_p\|²)·(Φ(0) − Σ_ρ\|w_ρ\|(exp(Σ_{p∈P} p^{−(σ−\|Re ρ−½\|)}) − 1)) (`jasProductAggregate_re_ge_exp`; ‖∏(1+u)−1‖ ≤ exp(Σ‖u‖)−1). Hence for every σ and K, once X is large, positivity holds on every finite P ⊂ primes > X with prime budget Σ_{p∈P} p^{−(σ−½)} ≤ K (`eventually_jasProductPositive_of_primeBudget`). Proof: each zero sees ≤ K X^{\|Re ρ−½\|−½} → 0; dominated convergence with \|w_ρ\|(e^K−1). This one statement contains the few-primes producer K X^{σ−½} (row 115, now a corollary), the global producer for σ > 3/2 (all P have budget → 0), and the σ ∈ (1, 3/2) windows of relative width ≍ K log X·X^{σ−3/2}. Reading: the unconditional JAS producer is exactly "bounded prime budget". Any explicit budget B(X) → ∞ is a zero-free-region statement. Magnitude-only receivers at growing budget fail in Θ = 1 configurations, because the zero sum Σ\|w_ρ\|e^{B X^{−η_ρ}} is not dominated by its largest term when many near-edge zeros have similar visibility. The residual is the phase conspiracy of rows 86–111 | PROVED | R:JointAncestryProductComplexity |
| 118 | Two structural channels for [B] (paper, killed). (a) Functional-equation pairing. K_P(1−ρ̄−it) = conj K_P(ρ−it) and w_{1−ρ̄} = conj w_ρ, so a mirror pair contributes exactly 2Re(w_ρ K_P(ρ−it)). The FE is already the real part; no extra sign law. (b) Kolmogorov/Bochner form. The marginals f_P dθ are projective (integrating out θ_p gives f_{P∖p}). So positivity for all P ⟺ a positive measure M on 𝕋^∞ (primes > X) whose Fourier transform is supported on the cube {−1,0,1}^{(∞)} with c(ε) = ∏_{ε_p≠0}(½p^{−σ})·Φ(⟨ε, log p⟩), i.e. c is positive definite on ⊕ℤ. Necessary conditions from M ≥ 0 along one direction give only \|c(ε)\| ≤ c(0)/2, i.e. \|Φ(log(a/b))\| ≤ Φ(0)2^{ω(ab)−1}(ab)^σ, weaker than the trivial e^{\|L\|/2} bound at σ ≥ ½. Representations of c as mixtures of product functions are far from unique (3^{\|P\|} coefficients against 2\|P\| parameters per product), so there is no rigidity forcing off-line product parameters to be Riesz-admissible. Positive survivor: the clean equivalence "product positivity at level σ ⟺ c_σ positive definite on ⊕_{p>X}ℤ" (receiver-side reformulation, not formalized) | KILLED (reformulation survives) | — |
| 119 | **[B] closed: level-one product positivity alone implies RH (Lean).** New idea: the diagonal Bochner bound. Restrict the product test to uniform vectors z_p = r e^{−iθ} on primes in a narrow window log p ∈ [u, u + e^{−δu}] and read off the first Fourier coefficient in θ. Positivity for all θ gives \|ĉ₁ + conj ĉ₋₁\| ≤ 2 Re ĉ₀ (Bochner on the discrete circle). Per zero, write p^{ρ−½} = e^{(ρ−½)u}β_p with \|β_p^{±1}\| ≤ e^{h/2}. A weighted ℓ¹ bound on the Laurent remainder then gives ĉ₁ = Σ a_pΦ(log p) + O(e^{αu}K²) and ĉ₀ = Φ(0) + O(K²), where K ≍ total weight. With total weight e^{−cu}, this bounds the window average of Φ(log p) by O(e^{cu}) + O(e^{(α−c)u}). Coherence on the window (weight tail of the zeros) costs O(u e^{(α−δ/2)u}). Huxley supplies enough primes when c > σ − 1 + δ. The growth criterion then gives sup\|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ max(0, σ − 1). Results: product positivity at any level σ ∈ [0, 1] ⟹ RH, and RH ⟺ ∃X: product positivity at level 1 (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasProductPositiveAbove_one`, no quasi-RH). The abscissa law Θ ≤ σ − ½ holds for every configuration including Θ = 1 chains (`xiZeroHalfPlaneBound_of_jasProductPositiveAbove`). Why rows 60–118 missed it: they built negativity witnesses that amplify one target zero, which competitors can rescue. The diagonal bound instead derives an upper bound on Φ itself, by coherently summing ≍ X^{σ−c} single-prime coefficients in one Fourier mode. All zeros are then handled at once by the Landau-type growth criterion, which is where discreteness of the zeros enters. A continuous off-line "zero measure" would pass every product test | PROVED | R:DiagonalLaurentCoefficients, R:JointAncestryDiagonalBochner, R:JointAncestryDiagonalBochnerLevel |
| 120 | Side-condition-free JAS capstones (Lean), plus the producer reading. RH ⟺ JasDressedPSD 1 ⟺ ∃X JasDressedPSDAbove X 1 ⟺ ∃X JasCosinePositiveAbove X 1 ⟺ ∃X WeilEulerPositiveAbove X 1 ⟺ ∀σ>1 JasDressedPSDAtInfinity σ (R:JointAncestryLevelOneCapstones). The quasi-RH and no-chain hypotheses are gone from every JAS capstone. Producer reading: with row 89 (half-plane α ⟹ positivity at levels > 1 + α) and row 119 (positivity at level σ ⟹ half-plane σ − 1), level-σ product positivity is equivalent to the growth exponent of Φ being ≤ σ − 1, up to the boundary. The level-1 producer is therefore exactly Φ(L) = O(e^{εL}) for every ε > 0, i.e. ψ(x) − x = O(x^{½+ε}). The receiver uses only diagonal tests: uniform phase, windows of relative width x^{−δ}, total weight x^{−c}. To first order these say \|avg_{window} Φ(log p)\| ≲ Φ(0)/κ. Route JAS is now a closed equivalence, and its producer coincides with the classical growth form of RH (the WL producer) | PROVED (capstones) / READING | R:JointAncestryLevelOneCapstones |
| 121 | Minimal JAS producer family (Lean). The receiver of row 119 uses only constant test vectors z_p = z on the primes of a single interval (y, y + L]. RH ⟺ ∃X: for every interval (y, y+L] with y ≥ X+1 and every z ∈ ℂ, Re Σ_ρ w_ρ ∏_{y<p≤y+L}(1 + z p^{−(ρ+½)} + z̄ p^{−(3/2−ρ)} + \|z\|²) ≥ 0 (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasIntervalProductPositive_one`). The chain now takes only `JasConstProductPositiveOn P σ` per window (`abs_sum_weilLagFunctionReal_le_of_constPositive`). Producer target: a two-parameter family (interval, z) of explicit aggregates, with no Kronecker phases and no arbitrary prime sets. On the line each factor is \|1 + z̄A\|² + \|z\|²(1 − \|A\|²) ≥ 0; off-line factors lose this square structure | PROVED | R:JointAncestryDiagonalBochnerLevel |
| 122 | Budget law: intermediate JAS producers are exactly zero-free half-planes (Lean). JasBudgetProductPositive X σ λ: constant-vector product positivity on every set of primes in a dyadic range (y, 2y], y ≥ X+1, with prime budget Σ_P p^{−(σ−½)} ≤ y^λ. Producer: a half-plane α with λ < ½ − α gives budget positivity at every level (`exists_jasBudgetProductPositive_of_halfPlane`; each zero sees ≤ y^{λ−(½−α)}, then the hypothesis-free zero-sum bound). Receiver: for σ ≥ ½ and λ > 0, budget positivity forces \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ max(0, σ − 1, ½ − λ) (`abs_re_sub_half_le_of_jasBudgetProductPositive`). Proof: subsets of the Huxley windows with weight ≍ e^{−cu} have budget ≲ e^{(½−c)u}, and the diagonal Bochner receiver now runs on abstract positive windows (`JasPositiveWindow`, `abs_re_sub_half_le_of_windows`). At level 1: quasi-RH ⟺ budget positivity for some λ > 0 (`exists_halfPlane_lt_half_iff_exists_jasBudgetProductPositive`), and RH ⟺ budget positivity at λ = ½ (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasBudgetProductPositive_half`). The JAS producer side is graded exactly: budget exponent λ ↔ half-plane ½ − λ, sharp up to the boundary. Unconditionally only bounded budget is known (row 117, λ = 0) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryBudgetZeroFreeLaw |
| 123 | Best unconditional JAS producer: logarithmic budget (Lean). For every 0 ≤ A < 1 there is Y such that, at every level σ, the product tests are positive on every finite set of primes p > y ≥ Y with prime budget Σ_P p^{−(σ−½)} ≤ (log y)^A (\`exists_jasProductPositive_logBudget\`). Proof: the explicit region 1 − β ≥ 1/(100 log\|γ\|) for \|γ\| ≥ 4 (corpus, both sides via conjugation and reflection), plus finiteness below height 4. Zeros up to T = exp((log y)^β), β = (A+1)/2, see ≤ y^{−1/(100(log y)^β)}(log y)^A → 0. Zeros above T see ≤ (log y)^A, but their weight O(log T/T) beats e^{(log y)^A}. Then the hypothesis-free zero-sum bound. Producer gap, quantified with row 122: unconditional budget (log y)^{1−ε} versus y^λ (λ > 0) for quasi-RH and y^{½} for RH. A Vinogradov–Korobov version would reach (log y)^{3/2−ε}; the power barrier is the zero-free-half-plane problem | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLogBudgetProducer |
| 124 | Zero-free regions → JAS producers, general and at Vinogradov–Korobov strength (Lean). Height-split producer (`jasProductAggregate_re_nonneg_of_heightSplit`): a width ω for zeros up to height T, y^{−ω}B ≤ t₁, and the weight tail above T give positivity at budget B. Power-of-log transfer (`exists_jasProductPositive_logBudget_of_powerLogWidth`): a zero-free region \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ ½ − c(log\|γ\|)^{−θ} gives positivity on all prime sets with budget (log y)^A for every A < 1/θ. The corpus VK region gives \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ ½ − (log\|γ\|)^{−θ} for every θ > 2/3 (`exists_powerLog_width_vk`, via `eventually_powerLog_le_vkWidth`). Hence the unconditional JAS producer reaches budget (log y)^{3/2−ε} (`exists_jasProductPositive_logBudget_vk`). With the budget law (row 122), the JAS producer frontier now reads: budget (log y)^{3/2−ε} proved; budget y^λ for some λ > 0 ⟺ quasi-RH; budget y^{½} ⟺ RH. Every gain in a power-of-log zero-free region (θ ↓) transfers to budget (log y)^{1/θ} | PROVED | R:JointAncestryLogBudgetProducer |
| 125 | Route-JAS closure (paper verdict). The product test at level 1 is a nonnegative combination Σ_S C_S·W(E_S) of Weil forms on Euler polynomials E_S(z, s) = ∏_{p∈S}(1 + z p^{−s}): use AB = p^{−2}, so each factor is (1 + zA)(1 + z̄B) + \|z\|²(1 − p^{−2}). So JAS positivity is Weil positivity on the cone of \|Euler polynomials\|², and its producer is the Weil producer. The frontier-map "Needs" scan finds no other route with a receiver gap the diagonal Bochner method could close; every route lacks a source/producer. Verdict: route JAS complete as an equivalence (receiver closed, producer graded by zero-free regions); NO INDEPENDENT PRODUCER FOUND | VERDICT | — |
| 126 | WL window-mean receiver, ported from JAS (Lean). The diagonal Bochner analysis showed that the JAS producer is an averaged WL lag bound, so the WL Need ("bound a_p … or the corresponding signed barycentric means") gets a sharp native receiver. For 7/12 < θ < 1: if \|Σ_{x<p≤x+x^θ} Φ(log p)\| ≤ C x^c·#(window) for all large x, then \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ max(0, c) (`abs_re_sub_half_le_of_primeWindowMean`). Proof: coherence on the window costs O((1+u)x^{−(1−θ)/2}e^{αu}); then the growth criterion with exponent max(c, α − (1−θ)/4). The converse holds (`abs_sum_primeWindow_le_of_xiZeroHalfPlaneBound`). RH ⟺ signed Huxley-window means O(x^ε) for every ε > 0 (`riemannHypothesis_iff_forall_primeWindowMean`); quasi-RH ⟺ power-saving window means (`exists_halfPlane_lt_half_iff_exists_primeWindowMean`). This relaxes WL's pointwise source target to signed means over windows of length x^θ, θ as low as 7/12+, with any subpower slack | PROVED | R:WeilLagPrimeWindowMeanReceiver |
| 127 | Lag frontier at VK strength (Lean). \|Φ(L)\| ≤ C e^{L/2} e^{−cL^θ} and \|a_n\| ≤ C√n e^{−c(log n)^θ} for every θ < 3/5 (`weilLagFunctionReal_frontier_rpow`, `integerLag_frontier_rpow`). This upgrades the corpus frontier from θ = 1/10 using `Sieve.chebyshevPsi_sub_id_isBigO_vinogradovKorobov`. Window means (row 126) are within x^{θ/2} of pointwise values, so the unconditional window-mean frontier is the same VK saving. The gap to quasi-RH is e^{−cL^{3/5}} versus e^{−δL} | PROVED | R:WeilLagFunctionFrontierVK |
| 128 | Weighted JAS family: receiver plus producer (Lean, 5 modules). A *dressing* g is bounded on the strip, nonzero at the zeros, with g(s̄) = conj g(s) and g(1−s) = g(s). Weight each zero by g(ρ)/(ρ(1−ρ)). Receiver: for every dressing, weighted positivity at level σ gives \|Re ρ − ½\| ≤ max(0, σ−1); for a dressing positive on the line, RH ⟺ ∃X weighted positivity at level 1 (`riemannHypothesis_iff_exists_jasWeightedProductPositiveAbove_one`). The proof ports the diagonal Bochner bound, coherence, windows and Landau growth to Φ_g. Producer: for the Gaussian g_τ = e^{\|τ\|(s−½)²} (the Mellin transform of a Gaussian kernel, so a genuine Weil test) and small τ ≠ 0, positivity holds unconditionally at budget exp((log y)^A) for every A < 3/5 (`exists_gaussWeighted_positive_expLogBudget`, via VK). This compares with (log y)^{3/2} for the undressed family. The undressed stop came from the γ^{−2} tail of 1/(ρ(1−ρ)), not from the zero-free region; dressing exposes the VK exponent itself (3/5 = 1/(1+2/3)) | PROVED | R:JointAncestryWeightedLag, …LagGrowth, …Bochner, …Level, …Producer |
| 129 | Weighted producer at near-power budget, plus the weighted budget law (Lean). With the doubly exponential dressing g_κ(s) = exp(\|κ\|(1 − cos 3(s−½))), we have \|g_κ(ρ)\| ≤ e^{\|κ\|}exp(−\|κ\|cos(3/2)cosh 3γ), and g_κ is positive on the line. For small κ ≠ 0 and every θ' > 2/3, weighted positivity holds unconditionally on every prime set p > y with budget ≤ exp(log y·(log log y)^{−θ'}) (`exists_cosWeighted_positive_budget`). Height split at T = log B; the tail exp(−cB³) beats e^B; VK width (log log B)^{−θ} beats log B/log y. Weighted budget law, for every dressing: budget positivity at y^λ ⟹ \|Re ρ−½\| ≤ max(0, σ−1, ½−λ). If Φ_g(0) > 0, then quasi-RH ⟺ ∃λ > 0 budget positivity (`exists_halfPlane_lt_half_iff_exists_jasWeightedBudgetPositive`). Net: the weighted JAS producer is unconditional up to y^{(log log y)^{−2/3−ε}}, and every fixed power is quasi-RH. The gap between proved and needed is exactly the VK exponent's (log log y)^{−2/3} | PROVED | R:JointAncestryWeightedCosProducer, R:JointAncestryWeightedBudgetLaw |
| 130 | Cycle audit, no new Lean. Checked the corpus for zero-location inputs stronger than VK: none; the zero-free lane analysis in R:ZeroFreeLaneNotCapped and R:ExponentChainKappa puts quasi-RH at κ = ∞. Considered and rejected:
- a lossy Turán/mean-square converse: dressing costs height ↔ log-height, so the dictionary is not tight;
- scale-dependent dressings: the receiver needs τ_u ≥ e^{−δu}, which brings the budget back to exp((log y)^{1/3});
- resonant test phases: these sharpen the receiver only;
- level interpolation: the level only adds |z|²(p^{2σ}−1);
- one-sided prime bounds: blocked by the parity barrier and by the duality.
Final classification written on the JAS card | AUDIT | card jas-quasi-rh-chain-exclusion |
| 131 | Conjecture: window JAS positivity at power budget has a Bessel–Toeplitz producer. For P in a narrow window at y = e^u with constant z = re^{−iθ}, the aggregate ≈ Σ_{n∈ℤ} I_n(2κ) e^{inθ} Φ(nu) (Bessel generating function; coherence handles the window). Positivity for all θ means n ↦ I_n(2κ)Φ(nu) is positive-definite on ℤ. KILL: at budget y^λ, κ ≍ y^{λ−½}, and the n = 1 Herglotz constraint alone is \|Φ(u)\| ≤ Φ(0)e^{(½−λ)u}/2, i.e. quasi-RH growth. The higher Bessel modes add no leverage. Lesson: power-budget JAS is literally the pointwise growth of Φ at the first lattice mode | FAILED | — |
| 132 | Conjecture: the one-sided criterion (Φ ≥ −Me^{aL} suffices) plus upper-bound sieves gives a producer. Prime side: Φ(u) = −e^{u/2}∫_{e^u}^∞ (ψ(t)−t)t^{−2}dt + O(e^{−u/2}), so Φ ≥ −Me^{au} ⟺ ∫_x^∞ (ψ(t)−t)t^{−2}dt ≤ Mx^{a−1}. KILL: the integrand's main term cancels exactly. The other sign needs lower bounds for primes, which pure sieves cannot give | FAILED | — |

| 134 | Conjecture: averaging the JAS aggregate over a Dirichlet-character family (z_p = rχ(p), χ mod q) gives positivity plus variance control, hence a producer. Averaging yields density information only | FAILED | — |

Treat the aggregate as a multiaffine polynomial in (z_p, w_p) restricted to w = z̄; each on-line factor 1 + zA + wB + zw is stable, and Asano contraction might propagate positivity. KILL: sums over ρ of stable products are not stable. Off-line factors are not stable once \|A\|\|B\| ≠ 1 symmetric. Asano contraction (a + bz₁ + cz₂ + dz₁z₂ → a + dz) does not preserve the real slice w = z̄ that JAS needs. The function-field benchmark fails too: nothing here re-proves \|α\| = √q | FAILED | — |

For z_p = rω^{k_p} (ω = e^{2πi/N}, N ≥ 3), the number of phase vectors k : P → ℤ/N with Re(aggregate) < 0 is ≤ N^{\|P\|}(W/Φ(0))²(∏_P(1 + 2a²p^{1−2σ}) − 1), a = r/(1+r²), with no hypothesis on the zeros (`card_jasPhaseAggregate_re_neg_le`). Mechanism: phase averaging kills the ω^{±k} and ω^{±2k} terms, so E\|∏−1\|² = ∏(1 + a²(\|A\|²+\|B\|²)) − 1 ≤ ∏(1 + 2a²p^{1−2σ}) − 1 uniformly over zeros; then weighted Cauchy–Schwarz over zeros and Markov. At σ = 1 the bad proportion is O(Σ_P 1/p), small for every P ⊂ (y, y^{1+ε}], at budgets up to y^{½−o(1)}. Insight: failures of JAS positivity are confined to resonant phase vectors (ω^{k_p} ≈ e^{−iθ}p^{iγ}), and the constant vectors on narrow windows that carry the RH receiver are resonant. Caveat (verified in the analysis): a random-phase receiver gives its own budget law, y^λ ⟺ Θ ≤ (5/2−λ)/2. | 159 | Parseval identity for random-phase JAS (Lean). Σ_k\|agg − mean\|² = (1+r²)^{2\|P\|}N^{\|P\|}Σ_{e≠0}κ_e²Φ(L_e)², over sign patterns e : P → {0,+,−} (`sum_normSq_jasPhaseAggregate_sub_mean`). | 160 | Conjecture (Tate-limit producer): build W as a canonical limit, as with the Néron–Tate height ĥ = lim 4^{−n}h(2^nP). | 162 | Batch of quick conjectures, all killed.
(a) Mean square over scales y ∈ [Y,2Y]: P(y) moves discretely, so there is no Parseval structure, and smooth versions reduce to row 151.
(b) Random subsets P (each prime kept with probability π): E_P[agg] is the constant-vector aggregate with weights πa, so the budget law applies. Var_P = 4π(1−π)a²Σ_pΦ(log p)²/p² + …, the same prime mean square as the phase variance (row 159).
(c) Dressings built from ξ itself, g(s) = ξ(s+c)ξ(s−c)·…: every admissible dressing obeys the weighted budget law (R:JointAncestryWeightedBudgetLaw), so the classification is unchanged.
(d) Li coefficients and p-adic Li: power sums over nodes that are unimodular iff RH, the same wall as row 157.
(e) Twists by residue-class indicators with Bombieri–Vinogradov: the principal main term survives, so the problem is RH-hard.
(f) Sum-product cancellation in Σ Φ over lag sets: Φ is a fixed function; no producer.
(g) The two-prime Gram on {2,3}: needs Φ(log 6), whose explicit formula needs the whole prime tail ∫_6^∞(ψ−t)t^{−2}; any certificate would be numerical theatre.
NO POSITIVE SALVAGE FOUND | FAILED | — |
| 163 | (i) Quick kills.
- Schur and tensor positivity across disjoint prime sets: the JAS Gram on P₁ ∪ P₂ is the diagonal part of the tensor product, not the Schur product, so positivity on P₁ and P₂ does not transfer.
- Log-convexity or Phragmén–Lindelöf in the level σ: the aggregate is a signed Dirichlet polynomial in σ, with no propagation principle.
- Prime-tail producer target, ∫_x^∞(ψ−t)t^{−2} ≥ −C log x/√x ⟹ RH: REDISCOVERED (R:WeilLagFunction `weilLagFunction_eq_prime_side`, R:WeilLagFunctionOneSided `riemannHypothesis_iff_weilLagFunctionReal_bddBelow`).
(ii) Paper analysis of the two-sided exceptional law (lower bound matching MUST-1320).
- Attained case: if some zero ρ₀ attains δ₀ = sup\|Re ρ−½\|, a 2D exponential tilt of the phases steers X_{ρ₀} to a target R e^{iφ*} with R ≈ log(W/\|w₀\|). The covariance of (Re u, Im u) is isotropic for N ≥ 3, and V ≍ y^{2δ₀−1}/log y ≪ 1.
- This gives a bad proportion ≥ exp(−C y^{1−2δ₀} log y). Twin zeros with equal real part reinforce, since Re w > 0, and they can be separated by choosing φ*.
- So in the attained case, lim log(−log Bad*)/log y = 1 − 2Θ: the random-phase exceptional exponent characterizes Θ.
- Obstruction in the chain case: steering toward ρ' shifts higher-δ zeros by t·y^{δ''−δ'}·corr. Taking ρ* with δ* ≥ Θ − log 2/log y repairs the exponent, but introduces a (log γ*)² factor, where γ* is the uncontrolled height of near-supremum zeros.
VERDICT: survivor on paper, conditional on attainment. Not formalized: it needs a 2D Kolmogorov lower bound and conditional control of the other zeros, about 2000 lines for a conditional statement | PAPER (conditional) | — |
| 164 | Conjecture (single-phase receiver): positivity of the real-amplitude kernels `K_{P,t}(s) = ∏_{p∈P}(1 + t p^{−σ} cosh((s−½)log p))`, `t ∈ [0,1]`, on large primes is as strong as complex product positivity. Attack: real tests cannot rotate phases (the diagonal subgroup `{±1}` has two characters, so the Bochner extraction aliases all odd levels and loses `√|P|`; this explains row 161's phase-count law). Survivor: the edge `N` of the prime range supplies the zero's own frequency, since `Σ_{X<p≤N} p^{−(σ−δ−iγ)} ~ N^{1−s}/((1−s) log N)` turns with `N^{iγ}` (PNT only). A maximal off-line zero at minimal height dominates: competitors at the same depth have main terms smaller by `‖1−s‖/‖1−s'‖ < 1`, shallower zeros have `o(S_α)`, the tail is weight-small. THEOREM (Lean): a maximal off-line zero at distance `δ` excludes real-amplitude positivity at every level `δ < σ < 1+δ` (`not_jasRealPositiveAbove_of_maximal_offLine`). Capstones: RH ⟺ ZerosApproachLine ∧ ∃X real positivity at level 1; RH ⟺ FiniteOffLine ∧ …; under ZAL real positivity at `σ ≥ 1` gives `|Re ρ−½| ≤ σ−1`. Open: the chain case (non-attained sup) for real tests | PROVED (attained) / OPEN (chains) | R:JointAncestryRealAmplitudeDetection, R:JointAncestryRealAmplitudeCapstone |
| 165 | Producer-core paper cycle (no Lean). (a) Function-field dictionary for the real family: an elliptic curve gets Hasse from (Rosati) `αᾱ = q` + (integral trace) + (counting) `deg(a+bF) = #ker ≥ 0`. Over ℚ, JAS has Rosati for free (`A_pB_p = p^{−(1+2c)}`), and real-amplitude positivity is the `deg ≥ 0` analogue for the traces `p^{−σ}cosh((ρ−½)log p)`. The missing input is the reality of traces: `Im cosh((ρ−½)log p) = sinh(δ log p)sin(γ log p)`, and reality at two primes forces `δ = 0`, so "reality" is RH per zero. REDISCOVERED (F₁ lane: no lattice carries the Frobenii; six exponentials). (b) Edge recursion: on initial segments `G(N) = G(N−1) + (t/N)Φ^{(N−1)}(log N)`, with `Φ^{(M)}` the lag function of the dressed zero measure `w_ρK_M(ρ)`. Under RH the induction closes by PD of the dressed measure: `|Φ^{(M)}| ≤ Φ^{(M)}(0) = G(M)`. Unconditionally the trivial bound gives only the trivial budget, and VK gives the sub-power budget law. REDISCOVERED (budget law). (c) Pole-free (edge-free) tests see zeros only through pair terms: self-pair exponent `2δ+½−σ`, so pole-free level `σ` ≈ edge level `σ/2+¾`, and pole-free level ½ is an RH receiver. REDISCOVERED (row 159 variance receiver, row 151 pair term). (d) Why complex receivers are chain-robust and real ones are not: the corpus's chain proofs twist at the target height (`z_p = e^{iφ}p^{iγ}`) or average a uniform phase, and the θ-average keeps the Rosati-diagonal `A_pB_p` terms bounded and ρ-free (the diagonal Bochner mechanism). Real tests have only `{±1}`, which aliases all odd orders. Survivor (paper): per-prime real SIGN twists `ε_p = sign cos(γ log p − φ)` emulate the Kronecker twist with coherence `2/π` and odd-harmonic ghosts at heights `(2k+1)γ` (Fourier series of sign cos). This route could carry the quasi-RH chain proof over to real signed tests; Θ=1 chains still need a uniform phase. Real per-prime positivity = positivity of the JAS density at the 2-torsion points `{0,π}^P` of the prime torus; complex = the whole torus | FAILED (producer) / insight | — |
| 166 | Phase-resource law (paper). Real per-prime amplitudes `t_p = t cos(γ log p + φ)` (allowed, `|t_p| ≤ 1`) emulate the complex Kronecker twist `z_p = t e^{i(γ log p + φ)}`. For the target `ρ = ½+δ+iγ` the big-side sum becomes `(t/4)e^{−iφ}Σ_p p^{−α}` (fully coherent, factor ¼) plus a `2γ`-twisted term of relative size `(1−α)/|1−α+2iγ|`. A competitor at height `γ'` sees only the two modes `γ' ± γ`, so there are no odd-harmonic ghosts (unlike 1-bit sign twists, which give coherence `2/π` with ghosts at `(2k+1)γ`). Every corpus argument that needs only a twist at the target height therefore transfers to real per-prime tests: maximal exclusion at all levels, large-prime maximal exclusion, and the quasi-RH chain exclusion (`τ`-alignment = varying the twist height). The diagonal Bochner, which closed [B] for Θ = 1 chains, needs a uniform phase with opposite signs on `A_p` and `B_p`; a real amplitude puts `cos θ` on both and aliases. Law: (1) one real constant phase suffices for attained configurations [L, row 164, via the range edge]; (2) real per-prime amplitudes suffice for all quasi-RH configurations [paper, this row]; (3) only Θ = 1 chains need a continuum of phases [corpus Bochner]. Real per-prime positivity = positivity of the JAS density at the 2-torsion points `{0,π}^P` plus interior radii. Not formalized: porting the ten quasi-RH chain modules to cosine twists only adds a side-condition variant of an existing capstone | PAPER (classification) | — |
| 167 | Edge-Fourier cycle (paper). Idea: real tests have no uniform phase, but the edge `u = log N` acts as a per-zero phase (`e^{iγu}` with growth `e^{δu}`). Fourier analysis of `u ↦ G(e^u, t) ≥ 0` against nonnegative windows bounds every frequency coefficient by the mean. (i) Fejér windows fail: the Fourier–Laplace transform at complex frequency `k(δ+iγ)` decays only polynomially and cannot beat `(t|E|)^k/k!`. (ii) Gaussian windows of width `H ≍ √(C log N)` give mean contamination `N^{δ²/2C}`, and the coefficient at the frequency of the largest term `w(tE)^k/k!` is isolated except for harmonic near-coincidences `kγ ≈ k'γ'` at resolution `1/H`. So the real chain case reduces to excluding harmonic near-coincidences among dominant off-line zeros; a quantitative linear-independence hypothesis would close it, and plain LI does not (no gaps). (iii) Edge averaging with width `≍ log N` kills every oscillating channel. It leaves the self-pair channel `(ρ, ρ̄)`, which for real tests is a real negative exponent, so each isolated off-line zero contributes `≈ Re(w_ρ)·(positive) > 0`, because `Re w_ρ > 0` throughout the strip. So edge-averaged real tests are blind to isolated off-line zeros; they detect only deep near-coincident distinct pairs (`|γ−γ''| < δ+δ''−½`). Producer reading: averaged real positivity is equivalent to a deep-pair repulsion law, which is weaker than RH but has no unconditional producer (Jensen local counts allow such pairs). VERDICT: no producer; survivors are two structural laws (harmonic-coincidence reduction; isolated-zero blindness of edge averages) | FAILED (producer) / insight | — |
| 168 | Real budget sharpness (Lean). Conjecture: the edge-term detection of row 164 already happens inside the prime budget `N^λ` for every `λ > ½ − δ`. Proof: the negative tests have amplitude `κ/E_{σ−δ}(N)` with `E_α(N) = N^{1−α}/log N`, and `Σ_{p≤N} p^{−(σ−½)} ≤ C·E_{σ−½}(N)` (Chebyshev upper bound, `½ < σ < 3/2`), so `t·Σ_{p≤N} p^{−(σ−½)} ≤ κC·N^{½−δ}`. Result `not_jasRealBudgetPositiveAbove_of_maximal_offLine`: real-amplitude positivity on the initial windows `(X, N]` within budget `N^λ` fails whenever an attained off-line zero has `δ > ½ − λ`. With the corpus producer `exists_jasBudgetProductPositive_of_halfPlane` this makes the real-test budget law two-sided in the attained case: real tests are not cheaper to produce than complex ones, and the exponent `½ − λ` is the width of the zero-free strip, with no Huxley windows and no Bochner step. Refactor: `frequently_jasReal_neg_of_maximal_offLine` (frequently-in-`N` negative tests with explicit amplitude). VERDICT: BRIDGE (sharpness of the real budget law); no producer | LEAN (MUST-1340) | R:JointAncestryRealAmplitudeDetection |
| 169 | Vertex reduction for real tests (Lean). Conjecture: the amplitude parameter of the real family is redundant. Each factor `1 + t c_p` is affine in `t`, so the aggregate is multi-affine in per-prime amplitudes and is decided at the vertices of the box. Constant `t ∈ [0,1]`: `K_{P,t} = Σ_{S⊆P} t^{|S|}(1−t)^{|P∖S|} K_{S,1}` (Bernstein polynomial of the subset function), so positivity for all `t` ⟺ full-amplitude positivity on every subset. Per-prime `|t_p| ≤ 1`: `1 + tc = ((1+t)/2)(1+c) + ((1−t)/2)(1−c)`, so positivity ⟺ all sign patterns `ε ∈ {±1}^P`. RH gives sign-pattern positivity at every level `σ ≥ 0` above every `X` (on-line factors `1 ± p^{−σ}cos(γ log p) ≥ 0`, weights `1/(¼+γ²) > 0`). Capstones: RH ⟺ ZAL ∧ ∃X untranslated prime-cosine positivity `Re Σ_ρ ∏_{p∈P}(1 + p^{−1}cosh((ρ−½)log p))/(ρ(1−ρ)) ≥ 0` (all finite `P` above `X`); same with sign patterns; FOL form. Bridge to corpus: full-amplitude kernel = `jasCosineKernel`; the corpus capstone (MUST-1270) uses all vertical translates `K_P(ρ − it)` plus quasi-RH; this one uses only `t = 0` plus ZAL. Producer reading: real random subsets do NOT average to a diagonal (the subset mean is the `t = ½` test itself), so real families have no typical-positivity producer, unlike random phases. VERDICT: BRIDGE (producer target reduced to the untranslated 0/1 family) | LEAN (MUST-1344) | R:JointAncestryRealAmplitudeVertex |
| 170 | Resonant edge-Fourier cycle (paper). Producer pre-check: the JAS producer target in prime form is `E[Φ(T−T')] ≥ 0` for symmetric prime random walks, with `Φ(log x) = −√x∫_x^∞(ψ(t)−t)t^{−2}dt + O(x^{−½})` (from `weilLagFunctionReal_eq_prime_side`). This is a one-sided √x-precision tail bound for the PNT error (wall 1), so no new producer. Receiver attempt on the real chain case: Fourier analysis of `u ↦ F(e^u) ≥ 0` (real full-window aggregate) at the exact ordinate `γ*` of a target zero, with log-weighted amplitudes `t_p = τ x^{−δ*}(log p)/p` so that the target's exponent is a pure rotation `τ e^{iγ* u}/(δ*+iγ*)`. Ingredients: (i) `|∫Fφ e^{−iγ*u}| ≤ ∫Fφ`; (ii) exact spiral identity `∫_{u₀}^{u₁} exp(A e^{(a+iγ)u})du = (u₁−u₀) + (F(ζ₁)−F(ζ₀))/(a+iγ)` with `F(z) = Σ_{k≥1} z^k/(k·k!)` (window means of exp(spiral) are `1 + O(a/γ)` between negative-real endpoints); (iii) the resonant part at `γ*` is a polynomial in `τ` with nonzero linear coefficient, unbounded on `[0,∞)`. Kills: (a) growing spirals of deeper zeros contribute endpoint terms `≍ e^{Re ζ₁}` that no compactly supported or analytic window suppresses (analytic flatness ⇄ growth conflict), so the mean is bounded only when every spiral is bounded on the window, i.e. `U ≲ 1/(Θ − δ*)`; (b) under quasi-RH this forces a peak/cluster analysis isomorphic to the corpus quasi-RH chain exclusion (Kronecker twist ↔ edge resonance), so it gives no new reach; (c) for `Θ = ½` the zero–zero spirals `x^{δ+δ'−½}` are not negligible, the resonant set becomes infinite, and the resonant part is an entire function of `τ`, which can be bounded on `[0,∞)` ("τ-entire obstruction"). VERDICT: no producer; receiver gain only isomorphic to existing quasi-RH machinery. Survivors: the exact spiral identity, and the τ-entire obstruction as the precise reason real tests stall at `Θ = ½` (in addition to the `p^{±(ρ−½)}` aliasing) | FAILED (producer) / insight | — |
| 171 | Producer cycle on the real family: on-line zeros are harmless for real kernels, so only off-line zeros threaten positivity. Conjecture: zero-density estimates (rare off-line zeros) give real positivity beyond the VK budget. Analysis: `Re Σ w K ≥ ∏(1−t/p)(Φ(0) − Σ_off|w|) − Σ_off|w|exp(tΣ_p p^{−1+δ_ρ})`. Density `N(½+δ,T) ≪ T^{A(½−δ)}` helps only for `δ > ½ − 1/A`. The decisive threats are deep zeros `δ = ½ − η`, `η < λ`. Their weighted count is `≈ exp(−cη^{−3/2})` (VK height threshold, density adds only polynomial factors), while their kernels reach `exp(N^{λ−η})`. Polynomial density savings cannot beat kernels that are exponential in `N^{δ}`. So the real producer frontier equals the complex one (VK, sub-power), and a power budget is quasi-RH. Integrality check (wall 5): integer Dirichlet-polynomial edges are exact (Euler–Maclaurin), but the pole term dominates them non-oscillatingly, so they are blind. Prime edges see zeros only through the explicit formula, and using integrality there is the Weyl/VK exponential-sum route (REDISCOVERED). Single-scale dressed cosine-twist detection (twist demodulates the target, competitors oscillate) still needs the local depth gap `g_j` of the target to beat the chain growth `log(γ_{j+1}/γ_j)`, the same chain obstruction. VERDICT: FAILED (producer); insight: exponential-kernel vs polynomial-density mismatch is the precise reason density estimates never help JAS producers | FAILED | — |
| 172 | Partial producer (Lean). Conjecture: real sign patterns are JAS-positive except on a vanishing proportion, uniformly over **all** finite large-prime sets (no budget). Mechanism: uniform random signs average every non-empty Walsh monomial to 0, so `E_ε A = Φ(0)` exactly (unlike random subsets, which average to the `t = ½` test). The variance is `Σ_ρ w (∏_P(1+|c_p(ρ)|²) − 1)`, a second-moment quantity that converges for every zero at `σ ≥ 1` because `2(σ − |Re ρ − ½|) > 1`. Uniformity in `P` comes from the Euler-product bound `∏_P(1+p^{−s}) ≤ 1 + 1/(s−1)`, the classical zero-free region (high zeros), `Σ|w| log(2+|γ|) < ∞` (corpus) and a low-height exponent gap. Proved and formalized: `exists_card_jasRealSign_re_neg_le`. Novelty vs corpus: the complex random-phase count (N ≥ 3) used the worst-case bound `|A_p| ≤ p^{½−σ}` and was budget-limited; the per-zero bound removes the budget. Limitation: typical, not all; RH needs the structured (resonant) patterns, which form a vanishing proportion. VERDICT: SURVIVES as an unconditional partial producer; not RH-bearing by itself | LEAN (MUST-1352) | R:JointAncestryRealSignTypical |
| 173 | Bochner bootstrap for real tests (paper). Conjecture: cosine-twisted real tests (`t_p = a_p cos(τ log p + φ)`) admit a diagonal-Bochner bootstrap like complex tests (`|f̂_τ(1)| ≤ f̂_τ(0)` in φ, then τ-averaging). Computation: the first φ-coefficient is the coherent window sum `Σ_W a_p p^{iτ}Φ(log p)` (≈ `A·Φ(u)` on a log-window of width `1/T₀`). τ-averaging of length `T₀` kills only cross pairs with `|log(p/q)| ≫ 1/T₀`, so inside the window the constant coefficient keeps the aliasing pairs `(pq)^{ρ−½}` of size `e^{2Θu}`. Bound: `A|Φ(u)| ≤ Φ(0) + W A² e^{2Θu}`, optimum `|Φ(u)| ≲ e^{Θu}`, i.e. the trivial bound. Complex tests have remainder `A² e^{αu}` (pairs `(p/q)^{ρ−½}` bounded on narrow windows, one factor `e^{αu}` from the moduli), so the bootstrap is `α ↦ α/2`, which contracts to RH. For real tests it is `Θ ↦ Θ`, a fixed point. VERDICT: FALSE (no real bootstrap). Insight: the aliasing law becomes a precise exponent law, remainder exponent `2Θ` (real) vs `Θ` (complex), and this is why only a continuum of phases closes Θ = ½ chains (consistent with rows 161/166/170). Side analysis of chains for real tests: every lower-hull vertex of `{(δ_ρ, log γ_ρ)}` dominates on an open log N interval, but its edge magnitude there can stay `< R` for tight hulls. An abstract chain model with all real tests positive therefore looks plausible; that would be a model no-go for "real positivity alone ⟹ RH" | FAILED / insight | — |
| 174 | Attack on the resonant patterns (paper). After row 172, RH sits in the patterns correlated with an off-line zero's phases (`ε_p ≈ −sign cos(γ log p − φ)`). Conjecture A: φ-averaged Kronecker patterns are unconditionally positive. Mean-value law: `E_φ exp(z e^{iφ}) = 1`, so the resonant zero contributes `≈ w`. Positive but blind, hence INSIGNIFICANT. Conjecture B: Kronecker patterns at non-ordinate `γ` are positive. FALSE: deep zeros are still seen through the pole edge `N^{δ}/|γ_ρ ± kγ|` (the constant pattern is the case `γ = 0`). Conjecture C: Gaussian-dressed cosine-twisted real tests (amplitudes `(1 + cos(γ_k log p − φ))/2`, target = deepest zero below height `H`) are RH-complete without side conditions. | 245 | **No-renormalization law** (paper). By Gaussian (Hubbard–Stratonovich) averaging along a line, positivity of Σ_ρ w e^{zA+z̄B} implies positivity of Σ_ρ w exp(z′A² + z̄′B² + κAB) for every κ ≥ 2\|z′\|. This is exactly the shell-N² family iff AB ≡ const, i.e. the family is undamped. Undamped families need ZAL. For damped families the cross mass κAB enhances on-line zeros by exactly the off-line gain, so detection stays at \|A\| > 2 and there is no amplification. Under ZAL a single undamped shell suffices: RH ⟺ Re Σ_ρ e^{zx^{ρ−½}+z̄x^{½−ρ}}/(ρ(1−ρ)) ≥ 0 ∀z for one x > 1 (positivity analogue of MUST-1372). Also checked: JAS large-prime positivity below σ = 3/2 is quasi-RH (abscissa ½+Θ, corpus). Verdict: the renormalization obstruction is the ZAL/AAL split again. Not formalized. |
| 246 | **VK cannot lower the large-prime threshold** (paper, killed). Take σ = 3/2 − ε and a zero at height γ with β ≥ 1 − η(γ), where η(γ) < ε. Its phase width is Σ_{p∈S} p^{−1+ε−η}, which is unbounded over finite large-prime sets S. The VK width η(γ) → 0, so arbitrary prime sets resolve arbitrarily high zeros. Hence σ = 3/2 is sharp for every zero-free region of shrinking width; going below it needs a uniform strip Θ < 1. Also recorded: mean-zero arithmetic shells (χ(p), Λ − 1, μ) are blind at zeros whenever β + Θ < 3/2, so for Dirichlet L-functions the arithmetic JAS shell criterion only detects Θ_χ > ¾. FALSE as a producer; the obstruction is wall 7/ZAL. |

Exactly, \|A + conj B\|² = 4 + 4δ²/(\|ρ\|²\|1−ρ\|²) and b = (2β−1)/(ρ(1−ρ̄)), so one exponential family with no shells and no side condition is RH-complete. This is the endpoint of the dichotomy line: shells → pole shadow → Cayley. The data are Li second differences. Verdict: SURVIVES as an exponential-Weil form of Li (REDISCOVERED-adjacent). | 249 | **von Mises form and Cayley positivity radius** (paper). With ψ_ρ = −i log(1−1/ρ) = φ_ρ − it_ρ, G(re^{iθ}) = Σ_ρ w e^{2r cos(θ+ψ_ρ)}. This is a mixture of von Mises kernels with complex locations, and RH ⟺ all locations are real. For a single off-line pair, positivity holds exactly for r < r_ρ ≈ 4\|ρ\|⁴ log(W\|ρ\|²)/(2β−1)², because the pair's modulus e^{2r cosh t} beats the on-line e^{2r} once e^{2r(cosh t − 1)} ≳ W/\|w\|. This defines a de Bruijn–Newman-type constant R* = sup{r : G(re^{iθ}) ≥ 0 ∀θ}, with RH ⟺ R* = ∞. Positivity up to R certifies \|β−½\| ≲ \|ρ\|²√(log\|ρ\|/R), i.e. the Li-type finite certificate. The Li lane owns the finite-index form ("no thinning gives a finite certificate"). INSIGNIFICANT beyond the reading. |
| 250 | **Schur/Verblunsky form of the Cayley criterion** (paper). (Δ²λ_m) is positive definite ⟺ the Carathéodory function Σ_ρ w(1+ζc_ρ)/(1−ζc_ρ) has Re ≥ 0 on the disc ⟺ all Schur (Verblunsky) parameters satisfy \|α_n\| ≤ 1. | 255 | **Bombieri–Stepanov dictionary** (paper, synthesis). JAS D_k(N) corresponds to the point-count defect over F_{q^k}, and the producer to Bombieri's Riemann–Roch auxiliary function. On ℤ the auxiliary functions are Chebyshev–Sylvester factorial ratios (bounded by (1±ε)x precision); their limit over all k is Möbius inversion Σμ(k)⌊x/k⌋ = 1, whose error is the MC route (wall 10). In F_q[t] the Möbius sums vanish exactly for degree ≥ 2, which is why the function-field case is free. No new mathematics; the routes JAS ↔ MC ↔ Stepanov are identified. |
| 256 | **ℤ-Stepanov via Fermat** (paper, killed; first auxiliary-function attempt in the corpus). We want an integer P(a) ≠ 0 divisible by Π_{N<p≤2N}p^M, with log\|P(a)\| ≤ Mθ_shell + O(M√N), as an upper prime count with √N precision. A Frobenius identity valid for every shell prime (a^L ≡ a mod p ∀p needs (p−1)\|(L−1)) forces L ≥ lcm(p−1) = e^{cN}, so log\|P\| ≥ e^{cN}. Derivative tricks (p \| d/dx x^p) are local to each p, and gluing them by CRT costs the full product of moduli. Mechanism of failure: Frobenius on ℤ is local at each p; Spec ℤ has no global Frobenius lift (Borger's Λ-structure on ℤ is trivial). Wall 5, now sharpened for auxiliary functions. |
| 257 | **Dolgopyat strip for the primes?** (paper, killed). ζ = Π(1−e^{−s·log p})^{−1} is the dynamical zeta of any flow whose primitive orbits have lengths log p. For Anosov flows, Dolgopyat's oscillatory estimates (non-integrable foliations) give a zero-free strip, which would be quasi-RH here. But every realization of the length spectrum {log p} is either trivial (a union of circles, with a diagonal transfer operator and no cancellation mechanism) or Connes' adèle-class flow, which is not smoothly hyperbolic. The full-shift suspension with roof log p has ζ_dyn = 1/(1 − P(s)), not ζ. The cancellation Dolgopyat extracts from geometry is exactly the unknown cancellation of Σ_p p^{−σ−it}. Wall 5/12. |
| 258 | **C252 lead: quantitative Pringsheim** (paper). Write a_n(s) = (−1)^nΛ^{(n)}(s)/n! for Λ(s) = Re Σc_i/(s−w_i). Rarity gives one-sided Taylor bounds a_n(s) ≥ −ε_n(s), with ε_n(s) ≈ (s−δ*)^{−n}e^{−F(n/(s−δ*))} (Φ⁺ moments). At a fixed base point, Pringsheim with errors needs ε_n ≤ C r₁^{−n} with r₁ above the true radius R₀(s) ≥ inf\|δ*−w_j\| > 0. Exponential rarity F = κt achieves this (the corpus Landau bootstrap). Subexponential F does not: the effective radius e^{f/(s−δ*)}(s−δ*) → s − δ* < R₀. So C252 needs a varying-base-point (s_n ↓ δ*) Pringsheim theorem. The threshold ∫F(t)dt/t² = ∫f(t)dt/t is exactly Levinson's log-integral / Denjoy–Carleman quasi-analyticity condition, which makes C252 plausible via a Levinson-type uniqueness theorem for Laplace transforms with one-sided coefficient errors. Status: OPEN, receiver-side, parked with this lead. |
| 259 | **C252 progress: weight–distance tradeoff** (paper). Let H(s) = ∫(M − min(Φ,M))e^{−st} (a Laplace transform of a nonnegative function) and D̂ the Laplace transform of the excursion part. Then σ_H = B (the top), H(B) < ∞, and \|H^{(n)}(B+iy)\| ≤ H^{(n)}(B) ≤ n!C/r₀^{n+1} + M_n, with M_n = ∫Dt^ne^{−Bt} ≈ sup_k k^ne^{−F(k)} (Denjoy–Carleman weights; the class is quasi-analytic iff ∫F/t² = ∞). At y = γ_j, Λ^{(n)} ≈ c_j n!/(2ε_j^{n+1}). Choosing n_j with f(k*(n_j)) ≈ 2ε_j gives **c_j ≲ exp(−c·F(f^{−1}(2ε_j)))**: near-top weights are super-exponentially small in the rarity scale. Consequently A(t) ≲ e^{δ*t − c′F(t)}, and the L¹/L² mean-zero argument forces K_eff(t) ≳ e^{F(t)} comparable active exponents at every scale t. Remaining step for C252: a multi-scale budget showing each scale needs fresh exponents within ~f(t) of the top, so the total real-part spread is ≳ ∫f/t. OPEN, receiver-side. |
| 260 | **C252: why the budget is ∫f/t** (paper). Thin positive excursions need coherent, commensurate blocks (Fejér-type, with frequencies in arithmetic progression). An incommensurate K-term block is approximately Gaussian and positive half the time, so its excursions are not rare. Coherent blocks have periodically recurring peaks of height ~K_eff·(typical) (by L¹/L² with zero mean, K_eff ≳ e^{F(t)}). The next dominant block must outgrow those peaks within time ~t, so ε(t) − ε(2t) ≳ F(t)/t = f(t), and ε(t) ≳ ∫_t^∞f(s)ds/s. Hence ∫f/t = ∞ is incompatible with a finite top: C252 is plausible. The rigorous gap is a coherence lemma: rare excursions force the dominant family at each scale to have recurring L¹-coherent peaks. Parked. |

Truncated Möbius (ν = Σ_{k≤K}μ(k)⌊x/k⌋ = 1 on [1,K+1)) gives error N·m(K)log N + K log N with m(K) = Σ_{k≤K}μ(k)/k, and m(K) = Ω(K^{−1/2}) unconditionally. | 266 | **Chebyshev method = transporter of Mertens bounds** (paper, closes C263 as a producer question). | 271 | **Aperture induction and boosted positivity** (paper, killed). (a) *Aperture induction*: show that Weil positivity at aperture a implies positivity at a + η with Ση = ∞ (unconditional at small a). By Krein–Boas, every PD test on [−2b,2b] is f⋆f̃ with supp f ⊂ [−b,b]. Split f = f₁ + f₂ + f₃ (edges of width η, core of aperture a). The core block is positive by induction and the edge blocks are positive by small aperture. But the edge–edge cross term W(f₁⋆f̃₃) sits at lag ≈ 2a, and its prime sum is ≍ e^{a}η\|f₁\|\|f₃\| (PNT scale). Only the pole term cancels it, and the remainder is exactly the zero sum of the edge pair. So the induction step is the full statement at aperture a + η: there is no locality, because pole cancellation is global. (b) *Boosted archimedean positivity* W_Λ + εB ≥ 0 at fixed ε (cf. the λ ≡ 1 boosted producer of MUST-1448): an off-line zero at depth δ gives negativity ≍ e^{2δa} at aperture a, which beats εγ^κ‖f‖², so fixed-ε boosted positivity for all apertures is still RH. Wall 8 (infinitesimal admixture) plus aperture growth. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |

So local positivity transports to a tautology and carries no zero information. | 277 | **Is 1/ξ(½+τ) positive definite on ℝ?** (paper; candidate structural low-height theorem). ξ(½+τ) = E[e^{τU}]·const is the moment generating function of the symmetric variable U with density ∝ Φ. The window norm ‖𝒫‖ ≍ Σ_{n≤e^{2a}}Λ(n)n^{−½} grows like e^{a}, so no estimate survives past small aperture (consistent with row 271's no-locality law). Similitude candidates (theta Fourier self-duality, Sonin/co-Poisson spaces) are owned by the Burnol/Sonin lanes (`soninSelfDualPart_isSelfDual`, BurnolSoninEigenWitness). No closable gap found in a producer position; the other gap markers are receiver interfaces of other lanes. |
| 280 | **Hecke similitude for 𝒫 ≤ Q** (paper, killed; sharpening of wall 6). The prime operator is a function of the commuting shifts U_p = e^{iD log p}: 𝒫 = Σ_p log p·2Re[p^{−½}U_p(1 − p^{−½}U_p)^{−1}] (minus pole), so Hecke-type operators p^{−½}(S_{log p} + S_{−log p}) generate exactly 𝒫's algebra. **Kronecker transfer principle:** if a symbol S(t) = F((p^{it})_p) with F continuous on the torus T^∞ is ≥ 0 on ℝ, then F ≥ 0 on the closure of the Kronecker line, which is all of T^∞ by the ℚ-independence of the log p. On the torus the Weil symbol Σ_p log p·2Re(z_p/(1−z_p)) + Ω(t) is unbounded below: each prime contributes down to −2 log p·p^{−½}/(1+p^{−½}), and Σ log p·p^{−½} = ∞. Hence 2Re ξ′/ξ(½+it) is not a torus-continuous symbol: its positivity is a property of the single deterministic point (analytic continuation through the functional equation), not of the Bohr lift. Every algebraic similitude built from Hecke/shift operators and archimedean convolutions is liftable, so it would prove positivity on the torus, where it is false. Such similitudes cannot exist. Any forcing of 𝒫 ≤ Q must use non-liftable structure, i.e. the functional equation (cf. row 254a, wall 6). The Riemann–Siegel phase θ(t) is the first non-liftable ingredient: the orbit (n^{it}, e^{iθ(t)}) is not a Kronecker line. Next: test whether a similitude in the extended algebra generated by U_p and e^{iθ(D)} can exist. |
| 281 | **CORRECTION to row 280** (paper). The Kronecker transfer principle (positivity of a torus-continuous symbol on ℝ propagates to the torus) is true, but it does not apply to the Weil symbol. The Weil symbol is A(t) − P_S(p^{it}), and its archimedean part A(t) ≈ ½log t is t-dependent and grows. Modulating a window test by e^{iTx} kills the pole term (f̂(±i/2 − T) → 0) and twists the prime sum by n^{iT}. Kronecker returns to a prescribed z ∈ T^S need T ≈ exp(c·#S·log(1/ε)), and by then A(T) ≳ #S, which exceeds sup_{T^S}P_S ≈ Σ_{p∈S}2log p·p^{−½} ≍ √X for S = {p ≤ X}. So positivity on the torus is consistent, and the claimed no-go ("liftable similitudes cannot exist") is FALSE as stated. What survives: at height T, window positivity is nontrivial only once e^{a} ≳ log T. Within the Kronecker-return range the twisted pole-free forms are dominated by the archimedean growth, so a liftable certificate must use the archimedean growth A(T) (i.e. height), never the pole. Recorded to keep the ledger faithful. |
| 282 | **Two-ended rigging for the Bender–Brody–Müller operator** (Lean read: `BBMMetricObstruction`; paper, killed). The corpus shows that the BBM pullback sends Hurwitz eigenstates to x^{−z}, whose density x^{−2Re z} is integrable at 0 iff Re z < ½ and at ∞ iff Re z > ½, so no eigenstate has finite norm (the critical line is the double boundary). Conjecture C282: some Gelfand triple Φ ⊂ L² ⊂ Φ′ carrying the x^{−z} as generalized eigenvectors of a symmetric H forces z ∈ ½ + iℝ. Kill: Gelfand–Maurin guarantees only that a complete family of generalized eigenvectors with real eigenvalues exists. It does not exclude non-spectral generalized eigenvectors with complex eigenvalue. For the dilation generator (xp + px)/2, which BBM's H is formally similar to via Δ = 1 − e^{−ip}, every Mellin monomial x^{−z} is a generalized eigenvector in a large enough dual, for every z. Reality would require the ζ-states to lie in the support of the spectral measure. That is the spectral-measure statement (wall 12), now in the form "the boundary condition selects spectral, not merely generalized, eigenvectors". The obstruction lies in the non-invertible similarity Δ, not in the choice of metric. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 283 | **Aperture induction with pole-orthogonal pieces** (paper, killed; refines row 271). Make each piece f₁, f₂, f₃ of the window decomposition pole-orthogonal (f̂_i(±i/2) = 0, by subtracting fixed multiples). Then the PNT main term ∫e^{x/2}(f₁⋆f̃₃) vanishes exactly, and the edge–edge cross term is a mean-zero prime sum Σ(Λ(n)−1)n^{−½}(f₁⋆f̃₃)(log n). Its size is ≍ e^{−a}·\|E(e^{2a+2η}) − E(e^{2a})\|·‖·‖ with E = ψ(x) − x. Unconditionally (Vinogradov–Korobov) this is e^{a−c(2a)^{3/5−ε}}, still exponentially large against the core energy ≍ 1. With an exponent Θ it is e^{(2Θ−1)a}, subexponential only at Θ = ½. So locality of Weil positivity in the aperture is equivalent to square-root PNT precision: wall 1, now in its sharpest geometric form ("cross-aperture coupling decays iff RH"). NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 283a | **Precision note on row 283.** "Equivalent" is too strong. Square-root PNT precision (Θ = ½) is necessary for subexponential cross-aperture coupling, but not sufficient for induction. Even under RH the cross term is ≍ a² while the minimal Weil eigenvalue on a window of aperture a tends to 0 (zeros become dense), so the induction step fails quantitatively under RH too. Correct statement: the coupling is e^{(2Θ−1)a+o(a)}, and aperture induction is unavailable in every regime. |
| 284 | **Every symmetry-compatible metric for BBM fails** (paper; completes the corpus's scalar `BBMMetricObstruction`). H = Δ^{−1}AΔ with A = (xp+px)/2 and Δψ(x) = ψ(x) − ψ(x−1). One has Δψ_z = −x^{−z} for the Hurwitz state ψ_z(x) = ζ(z, x+1), whose boundary condition ψ_z(0) = 0 is ζ(z) = 0. The right inverse is −T, with Tφ(x) = Σ_{n≥1}φ(x+n). (i) **Commutant law:** a positive G makes H symmetric (H*G = GH) iff G′ = Δ^{−*}GΔ^{−1} commutes with A. A has simple spectrum (Mellin transform ≅ multiplication by t on L²(ℝ)), so G = Δ*k(A)Δ for a measurable k ≥ 0. (ii) **No point spectrum:** the eigenvector norm is ⟨ψ_z, Gψ_z⟩ = ⟨Δψ_z, k(A)Δψ_z⟩. For z on the line, Δψ_z corresponds to δ_λ in the spectral representation, so the norm is k(λ)·δ(0) (∞ or 0·∞). Off the line, x^{−z} is not even a tempered generalized eigenvector. So in every metric compatible with the similarity, every zero (critical or not) is at best a generalized eigenvalue embedded in continuous spectrum. (iii) Survivor (principle): a Hilbert–Pólya realization obtained by similarity to the dilation generator must change the operator domain (a boundary condition producing discrete spectrum, as in Connes' cutoff), never only the metric. Normalizable weights such as w = (1+x)^{−4} do put ψ_z in L²(w), but they are not symmetry-compatible. Lean: needs unbounded-operator commutant theory, so not formalized. The scalar case is owned. |
| 285 | **Self-adjoint extensions (deficiency (1,1)) for the domain-change survivor** (corpus check, owned). The U(1)-family E + e^{iθ}E^# of a deficiency-(1,1) symmetric operator is the de Branges/Krein picture. The corpus has `riemannHypothesis_iff_strict_hermiteBiehler` (SuzukiXiDonoghueTarget: strict HB of Hedenmalm's Xi pair ⟺ RH), `riemannHypothesis_of_strict_hermiteBiehler`, the von Neumann construction (`exists_isSelfAdjoint_extension_of_deficiency_one`, HTGeometry/VonNeumannDeficiency), and the prime–arch self-adjoint vacuum realizations ⟺ RH (PrimeArchCanonicalReflectionPositiveEquivalence). Reading: HB of Ξ + iΞ′ ⟺ Ξ′/Ξ anti-Herglotz on the upper half-plane ⟺ Re ξ′/ξ > 0 on Re s > ½ (Hadamard sign, corpus abscissa ½ + Θ∞). Positivity fails within distance β − σ of any off-line zero (the term −1/(β−σ) → −∞), so the extension route is wall 4 exactly. Owned; no new producer. |
| 286 | **Deterministic all-height constraints inside the strip** (paper; corpus reference `ZetaNearOneLineLocalZeroCount`). (a) *Window count*: Jensen on a disc centred at 1+η with radius reaching ½+δ (anchor \|ζ(1+η+it)\| ≥ ζ(2+2η)/ζ(1+η) ≫ η, ceiling \|ζ\| ≤ t^{μ(σ)+ε}) gives #{ρ : β ≥ ½+δ, \|γ−T\| ≤ 1} ≤ c(δ,μ)·log T. Subconvexity (any μ(½) < ¼) only lowers c, and c → 0 needs Lindelöf-type bounds μ(σ) = 0 on [½+δ, 1]. So deterministic window counts are Θ(log T) by known methods, against RH's exact 0. Classical, not new. (b) *Fixed small-aperture Weil positivity* (unconditional at a ≤ a₀) is a deterministic constraint on configurations, but frequency resolution 1/a₀ averages ≍ (log T)/a₀ zeros. An off-line pair's negative term ≍ e^{2δa₀} is absorbed once log T ≳ a₀e^{2δa₀}, so it constrains only bounded heights (where it is numerics in disguise). (c) Hence the only deterministic all-height constraint is the zero-free region near σ = 1. Any first deterministic constraint inside the strip at all heights (e.g. a single excluded vertical band β ∈ [½+c, ½+2c]) would already be a strip-type theorem, i.e. quasi-RH on that band. Classified; no new producer. |
| 287 | **Inventory of middle-strip band receivers** (corpus). Squarefree and k-free cascades (`riemannZeta_ne_zero_of_squarefree_bound`, `riemannZeta_ne_zero_of_kBound`, `…_rhLevel`, `riemannZeta_ne_zero_of_expBound`), dilation and branching cascades (`riemannZeta_ne_zero_of_dilation`, `…_branching`, `…_dilationDescent`, `…_chosenDilation`), and the one-sided squarefree version (`riemannZeta_ne_zero_of_sqfError_oneSided`). Smallest gap: the k-free rung needs smoothed error t^{−(k−1)/k²−ε} against the trivial t^{−1/k}, a saving of only t^{1/k²}, and returns the strip Re s > 1 − 1/k. But power savings in the k-free error come only from the Möbius-weighted fractional-part sum Σ_{d≤x^{1/k}}μ(d){x/d^k}. Near d ≈ x^{1/k}, {x/d^k} is smooth (non-oscillating), so the saving is exactly short-range Möbius cancellation, i.e. a zero-free strip (wall 10). Every band receiver's gap is equivalent to its own conclusion. No easiest gap exists. |
| 288 | **Schur–Szegő downward induction for Jensen polynomials** (new to corpus; paper, killed). With G(x) = Σγ_kx^k/k! (Ξ(z) = G(−z²), γ_k > 0), RH ⟺ every J^{d,0} = ΣC(d,j)γ_jX^j is hyperbolic. Exact factorization J^{d,n} = J^{d,n+1} ⊠_d Q_{d,n}, with Q_{d,n} = ΣC(d,j)r_{n+j}X^j and r_k = γ_k/γ_{k+1} (Hadamard product with binomial weights). By Schur–Szegő composition, Q_{d,n} hyperbolic with nonpositive roots and J^{d,n+1} hyperbolic give J^{d,n} hyperbolic. Starting from GORZ (J^{d,n} hyperbolic for n ≥ N(d)) and inducting down would give RH. Conjecture RMC: (r_{n+j})_j is a multiplier sequence. Kill: (1) Pólya–Schur: a positive multiplier sequence has generating function Σr_kX^k/k! = cX^me^{σX}Π(1+X/x_i) ∈ LP⁺. Its growth is e^{σX} times a polynomial (finite product) or times a superpolynomial factor (infinite product). The saddle asymptotics of the moments of Φ give r_k ≍ 16k²/log²k, which is neither, so RMC is FALSE. (2) Even at fixed d the step fails for large n. Locally r_{n+j} ≈ A + Bj + Cj² + Dj³ + …, so Q_{d,n} ≈ P(XD)(1+X)^d has a (d−2)-fold root at −1 that the cubic and higher log-corrections split as (εc)^{1/(d−2)}ω: at most two real roots. So for d ≥ 5, Q_{d,n} is non-hyperbolic exactly where J^{d,n} is hyperbolic (Schur–Szegő is only sufficient). Same verdict for multi-step ratios γ_k/γ_{k+m} (growth k^{2m}/log^{2m}k) and for ratios to Pólya's Bessel kernel (ρ_k → const with 1/k corrections; e.g. {1/(k+1)} has generating function (e^X−1)/X with zeros 2πin, not a multiplier). Learning: multiplier sequences are too rigid, and the log-corrections in Ξ's coefficient growth (the prime/theta structure) break every Schur–Szegő factorization. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 288a | **Correction to row 288 (growth rate).** With γ_k = k!b_k (b_k = ∫u^{2k}Φ/(2k)! the Taylor coefficients of G, order ½), the moment saddle gives b_k/b_{k+1} ≍ 16k²/log²k, hence r_k = γ_k/γ_{k+1} = (b_k/b_{k+1})/(k+1) ≍ 16k/log²k, not k²/log²k. The kill stands, and is sharper: a positive multiplier sequence grows like a polynomial of integer degree (finite product times e^{σX}) or superpolynomially like Π(1+k/x_i) (infinite product). Growth k/log²k lies strictly between degree 0 and degree 1. (Sanity check: for cosh(π√x), zero count √r, the same computation gives r_k = 2(2k+1)/π², linear and a multiplier sequence. The extra log in Ξ's zero count is exactly what breaks it.) |
| 289 | **Gram-point (or any log-dense) comparison function** (paper, killed → REDISCOVERED, frontier LA exclusion). Positive multiplier sequences are exactly σ^kP(k) (generating function e^{σX} times a polynomial) or superpolynomial (infinite product). A bounded multiplier sequence is therefore geometric or constant. Comparing Ξ with Ξ_G = Π(1 − t²/g_n²) (Gram points θ(g_n) = nπ, same zero density, real-rooted by construction) gives ρ_k = γ_k/γ^G_k → c with fluctuating corrections driven by S(T). These are not of the form σ^kP(k), hence not a multiplier sequence. A multiplier relation would mean Ξ = P(θ)Ξ*(σ·) with P a finite-order operator in the Euler operator θ = x d/dx. The corpus already excludes Laguerre–Pólya preimages of Ξ under nonconstant real-rooted polynomial differential symbols (`XiRealRootedPolynomialPreimage`, `XiFirstOrderLaguerrePolyaMaximality`; frontier LA "Excluded"). So the multiplier/Schur–Szegő lane (rows 288–289) is closed. Also noted: Odlyzko–Poitou–Serre half-blind tests (g(±log n) ≤ 0 for n ≥ 2) give the unconditional inequality Re Σ_ρ ĝ(γ_ρ) ≥ arch(g) + pole(g). Excluding a bulk zero needs a sign for the other zeros' terms, which is circular; this is the corpus's explicit-formula positivity duality. |
| 290 | **Two unconditional mechanisms pushed into the strip** (paper, killed). (a) *Odlyzko–Poitou–Serre/dlVP bootstrap*: tests with Re ĝ(ρ) ≥ 0 for all zeros need kernels 1/(σ₀ − ρ) with σ₀ > sup Re ρ. The prime-side positivity Σ Λ(n)n^{−σ}(3 + 4cos + cos2) ≥ 0 needs absolute convergence, σ > 1. Bootstrapping to σ₀ < 1 from a hypothetical Θ < 1 requires prime-side positivity below the abscissa: wall 6. (b) *Kloosterman (Selberg 3/16) analogue*: by Kuznetsov, Σ_{c≤X}S(m,n;c)/c contains the continuous-spectrum term ∫h(t)X^{2it}τ_{it}(m)τ_{it}(n)/\|ζ(1+2it)\|²dt. A sharp cutoff forces contour shifts past zeros of ζ(1±2it), so the zeros of ζ contribute decaying terms ≍ X^{β−1}. RH ⟺ these are X^{−½+ε}, which needs averages of Kloosterman sums to precision X^{−½}: beyond Linnik–Selberg (X^{ε}, open) and far beyond the known X^{1/6+ε}. Weil's individual bound \|S\| ≤ d(c)√c only gives absolute convergence of the Kloosterman zeta at Re s > ¾ (the 3/16 mechanism), while ζ's zeros sit as poles at s = ρ/2 with Re < ½. Algebro-geometric square-root cancellation is again used up at the wrong scale. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 291 | **Similitude forcing 𝒫 ≤ Q** (paper, killed; target from `ArchimedeanPairingDefinite`). (i) *Tautology*: Q is positive definite, so V*QV = Q − 𝒫 is solvable iff Q − 𝒫 ≥ 0. Take V = Q^{−½}(Q^{−½}(Q−𝒫)Q^{−½})^{½}Q^{½}; equivalently, the Riccati equation T*Q + QT − T*QT = 𝒫 for V = I − T. A similitude has content only if V is given by an explicit arithmetic operator. (ii) *Arithmetic ansätze fail structurally*: Euler-factor operators V = Π_p(1 − p^{−½}S_{log p}) produce multiplicative symbols \|v(t)\|²Ω(t) (formally \|1/ζ(½+it)\|²Ω). But Q − 𝒫 has the additive log-derivative symbol 2Re ξ′/ξ(½+it), which on RH is the singular measure 2πΣδ_γ. No bounded outer v has \|v\|²Ω = 2Re ξ′/ξ, so any similitude is necessarily singular, a spectral object (wall 12). (iii) *First aperture* a = log 2 + ε: 𝒫 = (log 2/√2)(PS_{log 2}P + h.c.) is a partial isometry between edge intervals of length ε. For edge-concentrated f, Q(f) ≈ ‖f‖² (the continuous archimedean kernel sees only ∫ over tiny intervals), so W ≥ (1 − log 2/√2)‖f‖² > 0. Norm domination by the prime weights Σ_{p≤e^a}log p/√p stays below 1 only until log 3 (0.49 + 0.63 = 1.12 > 1), after which positivity needs the pole and archimedean structure (corpus explicit-aperture lane). NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 292 | **Λ = μ ⋆ log factorization of the prime operator** (paper, killed → MC/Báez-Duarte, owned). On L²(ℝ) the shift sums commute exactly: Σ_nΛ(n)n^{−½}S_{log n} = (Σ_dμ(d)d^{−½}S_{log d})(Σ_m log m·m^{−½}S_{log m}), i.e. symbols (1/ζ)(−ζ′) on the line, formal. Compression to a window destroys the product structure: P(AB)P ≠ (PAP)(PBP), because products dm beyond e^{2a} compress to 0 while their factors do not. So the "positive" factor M_{−ζ′} (coefficients log m ≥ 0) cannot be split off on windows. The uncompressed factor M_{1/ζ} is unbounded on L² of the line unless 1/ζ(½+it) is controlled, which is the Nyman–Beurling/Báez-Duarte density question (RH ⟺ d_N → 0, with the corpus's unconditional floor liminf d_N² log N ≥ Σ_ρ 2m_ρ²/\|ρ\|²). Producing d_N → 0 means constructing Möbius-type coefficients with half-power cancellation: route MC, wall 10. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
