| 293 | **Nested prime-by-prime organization of the single-prime criterion** (Lean read: `RHTower/PrimeBlindNodeCriticality`; paper, killed). The corpus interpolation theorem `riemannHypothesis_iff_repairedBlindPositive_vonMangoldt` says that for every node set S, RH ⟺ (Weil functional with weights Λ off S) ≥ 0 on the S-blind cone. So every stage of a nested intersection (S = {3}, {3,5}, …, all non-powers of 2) is exactly RH. There is no easier first stage: the hope that "blind at log 3 only" is decidable on paper is FALSE. Structural reading worth recording: (i) the weight cone C = {w : arch + pole − Σw_n n^{−½}2g(log n) ≥ 0 on all PD g} satisfies C ⊆ {Λ} unconditionally (`eq_vonMangoldt_of_weilPositiveFull`), so RH ⟺ C ≠ ∅, an existence problem for an archimedean-determined weight. (ii) By Hahn–Banach, C = ∅ ⟺ some blind PD combination has negative archimedean part ⟺ ¬PBAP. (iii) A variational route (minimize the convex negativity N(w) and use uniqueness) needs N(Λ) ≤ 0, which is circular unless a symmetry forces the minimizer's value. The archimedean form has only the reflection symmetry, and the node set {log n} carries only the multiplicative semigroup, so no averaging principle is available. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 294 | **Integer-dilation semigroup on the blind cone; nested-compactness existence** (paper; corpus check). (a) g ↦ g_k = g(k·) (k ∈ ℕ) preserves PD-ness and blindness (k log n = log n^k). Since Re ψ(¼ + it/2) increases in \|t\| and ĝ ≥ 0, arch(g_k) = ∫ĝ(u)Ω(ku)du is nondecreasing in k, and arch(g_k) ≥ arch(g) + π g(0)log k − O(1) → ∞. pole(g_k) = (1/k)∫g·2cosh(y/2k) → (2/k)ĝ(0). So compressed blind tests become positive automatically, and PBAP counterexamples cannot be compression-stable. But this says nothing about uncompressed g: no reduction to "primitive" rays, because the blind cone is not generated by compressions. (b) Nested compactness: C_a = weights positive on aperture-a tests is convex and compact (pair tests bound w both ways) and decreasing, with ∩C_a = C ⊆ {Λ}. Hence RH ⟺ ∀a C_a ≠ ∅ ⟺ ∀a PBAP_a (Farkas: archimedean positivity on PD tests of aperture a blind at the finitely many nodes log n ≤ 2a). Per aperture, PBAP_a is strictly weaker than Weil_a(Λ), since non-Λ weights may serve until C_a empties. Owned in substance: `exists_weilPositiveFull_iff_primeBlindPositive` (WeilFullConeOneSidedCriterion), SoninAllPrimeZerothMomentApertureFiberCompactness. Counting heuristic: ≍ e^{2a} quadratic blind constraints against ≍ ca negative archimedean directions makes PBAP_a "generically" true. But if RH fails, the off-line dual measures supply exact solutions, so arithmetic, not counting, decides. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 294a | **Correction to row 294(b) counting heuristic.** The blind condition is linear on the cone of PD functions (g(log n) = 0 for g = Σc_if_i⋆f̃_i), not quadratic on a single f. The blind cone of aperture a is therefore an infinite-dimensional cone cut by ≍ e^{2a} linear conditions, and the negative region of A (PD g with ĝ concentrated where Ω < 0, \|t\| ≲ 2π) is also infinite-dimensional. So nothing is overdetermined, and the "generically true" remark is withdrawn. |
| 295 | **First aperture needing more than norm domination (just above log 3)** (corpus check; route killed as theatre). Corpus state: `one_tenth_le_ccmRestrictedWeilPolarGroundEnergy_halfLogTwo` (μ₀(½log 2) ≥ 1/10) via a trace certificate with norm_num midpoint–radius enclosures. Literature: Zhu (interval arithmetic) reaches support [−0.8, 0.8]. The memory records that the certificate's negative part (α − W_a)₊ has support out to exp(4e^a), so every aperture extension is a finite certified computation whose size grows doubly exponentially. Extending past log 3 is finite RH verification in disguise: rule-3 "no computations" territory, non-scalable, and it moves no structural frontier. Not pursued. Side analysis: the obvious explicit negative tests from a hypothetical off-line pair (f̂ = (Ξ/((t−a)(t−a′)))·H, vanishing on every critical zero, with H tuned so that 2Re(F(a)\overline{F(a′)}) < 0) are not blind. The negative blind combination guaranteed by Hahn–Banach is non-constructive. Ξ-divisible tests f̂ = ΞH give W = 0 identically (null face, since Ξ vanishes at all zeros, including off-line ones). So constructive duality also gives nothing. |
| 296 | **Search for the first non-density, numerics-free, non-ZAL statement weaker than RH** (paper classification). (a) *About critical zeros*, deterministic at all heights: Hardy–Littlewood gap bounds (every [T, T + T^{θ}] contains a critical zero, with θ tied to the subconvexity exponent μ(½) via ∫\|Z\| against \|∫Z\| on short intervals). These are genuinely of the requested type but concern on-line zeros, so they exclude nothing off the line. Improving θ is subconvexity work, not RH progress. (b) *About off-line zeros*, deterministic: besides the zero-free region near σ = 1, every candidate is one of three kinds. (1) Strip or band exclusions, which are quasi-RH on that band (rows 286–287). (2) Rigidity (AAL: finitely many ⇒ none), with no mechanism, since the random Euler-product model allows isolated accidents (row 268). (3) Exclusions on null sets of ordinates (e.g. via Baker/transcendence: no zero with prescribed algebraic relations among p^{−ρ}), which are formally possible but irrelevant, because Λ-weights impose no multiplicative relations at zeros. (c) The integer-dilation monotonicity arch(g(k·)) ≥ arch(g) (PD g, k ≥ 1; Ω increasing in \|t\|) is true and elementary, but it is a statement about compressed tests, which are automatically positive; not formalized (anti-theatre). Conclusion: no statement of the requested type exists among known mechanisms. The first one would itself be a new kind of theorem. |
| 297 | **The weakest property separating ζ from its rational modifications ζR** (paper → BAG route, owned). (a) Almost periodicity on Re s = 2 separates exactly: ζR = ζ + ζ(R − 1) with R − 1 → 0 at ±i∞, and an almost periodic function tending to 0 is 0, so ζR is not AP unless R ≡ 1. (b) But AP on σ > 1 does not reach the strip. The rigidity mechanism one wants is Bohr recurrence: for a function uniformly AP on a vertical strip containing an off-line zero u₀, Rouché plus relative density of ε-almost periods gives zeros near u₀ + iτ for a relatively dense set of τ. So off-line zeros come in infinite families and AAL is automatic. Check on the class {FE, AP on vertical lines}: F = ζ·P with P = Σa_n(n^{½−s} + n^{s−½}), an FE-symmetric AP exponential polynomial, has either no off-line P-zeros or infinitely many, consistent with this. (c) ζ is not uniformly AP in ½ < σ < 1: it is unbounded there (Bohr–Landau) and only B²-almost periodic, and {ζ(·+iτ)} is not a normal family on compacts in the strip. Required input: deterministic self-recurrence of ζ in the strip, which is the frontier's BAG candidate (KR-BAG/KR-TOR/KR-LLM dead mechanisms; "weak zeta self-recurrence would collapse FOL/ZAL to RH, but is not proved"). Owned; NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 298 | **Almost-periodic renormalization of ζ on a strip** (paper; BRIDGE). Claim, proved on paper: for an open vertical strip S ⊂ {σ > ½}, ζ is zero-free on S ⟺ ∃ G analytic and zero-free on S with ζG uniformly almost periodic on S. (⇒) G = 1/ζ, ζG = 1. (⇐) Suppose ζ(ρ₀) = 0 with ρ₀ ∈ S. ζG is AP, so Rouché plus relative density of ε-almost periods gives zeros of ζG, hence of ζ (G zero-free), within ε of ρ₀ + iτ for a relatively dense set of τ. That is ≫ T zeros with β ≥ β₀ − ε up to height T, contradicting the unconditional density theorem N(σ,T) = o(T) for σ > ½ (Bohr–Landau). So AP-renormalizability is all-or-nothing and equivalent to its conclusion. Bohr–Landau unboundedness does not obstruct it (on RH, G = 1/ζ works). Weaker variants also fail: normality of {ζ(·+iτ)G(·+iτ)} would need the shift family to omit values or be locally bounded, but Voronin universality makes {ζ(·+iτ)} dense among nonvanishing functions, i.e. maximally non-normal. Learning: every regularity of ζ in the strip strong enough to transport zeros vertically is already equivalent to the zero-free statement it would prove. Not formalized (equivalence; anti-theatre). |
| 299 | **Fixed-width zero-free region on a positive-density set of heights** (paper; KNOWN, trivial). For fixed δ > 0, the density estimate N(½+δ, T) ≪ T^{1−cδ}log T bounds the number of unit windows [n, n+1] ⊂ [0, T] that contain a zero with β > ½ + δ by T^{1−cδ+ε} = o(T). So σ > ½ + δ is zero-free on a density-one set of heights, even with δ → 0 slowly, and the corpus already has the power-saving version (`GaussianCentralGapRatePowerSaving`: every rate holds off a power-saving set of heights). The exceptional height set E(δ) has counting ≪ T^{1−cδ}; better density exponents shrink it but never empty it. RH is exactly E(δ) = ∅ ∀δ. Structural questions about E(δ) beyond counting (additive structure, e.g. arithmetic progressions of off-line ordinates) lead to the corpus's lag-progression receivers (`WeilLagProgressionZerosApproachLine`: RH ⟺ ZAL ∧ one bounded progression). No new content. |
| 300 | **State of the lane, cycles 263–299** (synthesis). Formal output: MUST-1450 (Chebyshev rough slack, the elementary barrier survivor). Everything else is paper kills or bridges, each tied to a named wall. (1) *Producer side*: every candidate reduces to a zero-free strip/band (quasi-RH; JasDressedPSD < 3/2, band cascades, AP renormalization) or to the spectral measure (𝒫 ≤ Q similitudes, BBM metrics, extensions, Plancherel). (2) *Rigidity side (AAL)*: needs deterministic recurrence in the strip (BAG); AP renormalization is equivalent to zero-freeness (row 298); uniqueness theorems cannot exclude rational modifications ζR (row 296). (3) *Laguerre–Pólya coefficient side*: multiplier sequences are rigid, and the log in N(T) (archimedean density) breaks every Schur–Szegő transfer (rows 288–289). (4) *Locality*: Weil positivity has no aperture-local proof; the coupling is e^{(2Θ−1)a} and fails even under RH (rows 271, 283/283a). (5) *Deterministic constraints* inside the strip at all heights do not exist beyond the zero-free region near σ = 1. Window counts are Θ(log T), gap theorems concern only critical zeros (rows 286, 296). (6) *Algebraic square-root cancellation* (Weil/Kloosterman) is always used up at a scale blind to ζ's zeros (row 290). Corrections issued: rows 281, 283a, 288a, 294a. Big picture: RH = existence of an archimedean-determined weight (uniqueness is free, MUST-1440 lane), and every existence mechanism needs an exact individual-zero structure that no known object supplies. |
| 301 | **Fixed-point/contraction existence for the weight cone** (paper, killed). The map w ↦ Proj_{C_a}(w), iterated over increasing apertures, is defined only where C_a ≠ ∅, so it presupposes the existence it should produce. Totalize instead: the negativity N(w) = −min(0, λ_min[A − Σ_n w_n·node_n]) on aperture-a tests is convex, lower semicontinuous and coercive (pair tests bound w both ways), so minimizers exist by compactness or Brouwer for every a. But RH is the statement that the minimum value is 0 for every a. Fixed-point and compactness methods produce minimizers, never the value of the minimum. A value statement needs either a symmetry whose fixed points are known to have N = 0 (none: the archimedean form has only the reflection symmetry, and the nodes only the multiplicative semigroup, row 293) or a duality certificate, which is PBAP itself. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. Learning: all compactness tools stop at "a minimizer exists". |
| 302 | **A differential operator commuting with the full windowed Weil operator Q_a − 𝒫_a** (Lean read: `ProlateCommutationKernel.prolateAction_bandTransform_commutes`, `FuchsProlateScale.prolateSLAction`; paper, killed). The corpus has Slepian's commutation: the prolate Sturm–Liouville operator (1−x²)∂² − 2x∂ − c²x² commutes with the band transform, i.e. with the archimedean/time–band part. For the prime part: a compressed shift PS_uP is the partial isometry L²[−a, a−u] → L²[−a+u, a]. For f in any differential domain, PS_uPf has a jump at x = −a+u unless f(a−u) = 0 (and derivatives), so D(PS_uPf) − PS_uP(Df) carries δ- and δ′-terms at the cutoff for every second-order D. Only constant-coefficient D commute with uncompressed shifts, and those do not commute with the prolate band part (non-constant coefficients). Hence no second-order D commutes with Q_a − 𝒫_a for a > ½log 2 (as soon as the first node enters). The ground energy μ₀(a) has no exactly solvable "value" structure of prolate type. This matches the corpus's use of the prolate operator as an approximation (leakage gap) rather than an exact symmetry. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 303 | **Cross-lane producer combination: λ ≡ 1 boosted (MUST-1448) mixed with Λ** (paper, killed → wall 8/wall 2). weilFunction is affine in the weight, so Φ_t = t·W(Λ) + (1−t)·W(1) + c(s+½)^θ. W(Λ) is the ξ′/ξ-type function with residue +1 at every zero. Near an off-line zero ρ with Re ρ > ½, the term t/(s−ρ) dominates every polynomially growing piece, and Re Φ_t takes both signs on a small circle around ρ for every t > 0 and every boost. Hence the producer abscissa of every mixture with t > 0 equals max(½, sup Re ρ), with no continuity in t: t → 0⁺ recovers MUST-1448, but positivity fails at every t > 0 if RH fails. Structural reading of the two ladders: the λ ≡ 1 ladder (boost exponent θ) is the value/growth ladder (Lindelöf ⟺ every θ > 0 works). The Λ ladder is degenerate: every boost rung is RH, because zeros of ζ are poles of the log-derivative, which no growth boost absorbs. RH lives in the log-derivative world and Lindelöf in the value world. This is wall 2 (\|ζ\|² vs log\|ζ\|) restated in Herglotz-producer language. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 304 | **Dyadic Jensen-gap (BSY/Littlewood) rigidity** (paper, killed → wall 2, quantitative). Littlewood's lemma on the rectangle [½, 2] × [0, T] gives ∫_0^T log\|ζ(½+it)\|dt = 2πΣ_{β>½, γ≤T}(β−½) + O(log T), so the dyadic BSY pieces are exactly the off-line moment. RH ⟺ the mean of log\|ζ(½+it)\| over [0,T] is O(log T/T), i.e. relative precision 1/T. Unconditional inputs: (i) Selberg's CLT is distributional (variance ½log log T, error o(1) in probability), with no O(log T/T)-precision mean. (ii) Jensen/concavity on mollified moments, ∫log\|ζM\| ≤ (T/2)log(∫\|ζM\|²/T), gives the corpus off-line moment μ ≤ 3/25. Reaching 0 needs ∫\|ζM\|² = T(1 + o(1/T)), i.e. \|ζM\| essentially constant, while every mollifier of length T^θ has mean square ≍ T(1 + 1/θ). The strict Jensen gap E log X < log E X for non-constant X is exactly wall 2. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 305 | **C277, first Hankel levels** (paper). Write Ξ(y) = Σ(−1)^ka_ky^{2k} with a_k = m_{2k}/(2k)!, m_j = ∫u^jΦ, and 1/Ξ = Σb_ky^{2k}. Exponential convexity of 1/Ξ on (−γ₁,γ₁) ⟺ M_{2k} = (2k)!b_k is a Stieltjes moment sequence (in x²). Level 0: b₂ ≥ 0 ⟺ a₁² ≥ a₀a₂ ⟺ kurtosis κ := m₀m₄/m₂² ≤ 6 (Turán, known). Level 1: M₀M₄ ≥ M₂², i.e. 24b₀b₂ ≥ 4b₁², ⟺ 5a₁² ≥ 6a₀a₂ ⟺ κ ≤ 5, strictly stronger. Higher levels are polynomial inequalities in m₀, …, m_{4k}. The kernel Φ ~ exp(−πe^{2\|u\|}) has much lighter tails than a Gaussian (κ = 3), so the low levels hold with margin and are plausibly provable by Csordas–Norfolk–Varga-type kernel inequalities, with no computation. But the whole hierarchy ⟺ C277, whose tail (Darboux, row 278) is the reality of the lowest zero. So finite levels are INSIGNIFICANT for RH (moment inequalities at the origin, of the same type as the owned Turán/Jensen-at-origin lanes, e.g. JensenQuarticXi), and the full hierarchy encodes low-height numerics. C277 closed as a producer target; kept only as a classified receiver. |
| 306 | **A concrete Hodge-candidate inner product on Meyer's H¹** (Lean read: `DeningerArithmeticArakelovPoissonComplex`, H¹ ≃ Hminus/range Z; paper, killed; extends the corpus completion dichotomy MUST-138). (i) *Weighted L²_α completions* (any α): on the Mellin side Z is multiplication by ζ(α+it), nonzero a.e., so its range is dense and the quotient is 0. The flow there is e^{αt}·unitary, so any eigenvector would have Re ρ = α, but none exist. (ii) *Sobolev completions H^s(critical line)* (s > k+½): point and derivative evaluations at real t are continuous, so the cokernel is exactly the closed span of δ_γ^{(j)} over critical zeros (j < multiplicity). This gives a Hilbert H¹ that contains only critical zeros. Off-line evaluation functionals do not exist on functions on the line. Its H^{−s} inner product k_s(γ−γ′) is positive but not flow-invariant: e^{iut} shifts the dual variable, so distinct eigenvectors are not orthogonal. The flow-invariant positive forms on it are the diagonal atomic ones, which exist trivially. (iii) *Strip completions* (Hardy/Bergman spaces on a strip containing all zeros) see every zero, but the flow acts by e^{(ρ−½)u}, which is non-unitary off the line. **Dichotomy:** line-supported Hilbert realizations are unitary and RH-blind; strip-supported ones are complete and non-unitary. RH ⟺ the natural map from algebraic H¹ to the Sobolev H¹ is injective (no class supported off the line). "Geometrically produced positivity" therefore cannot be a choice of completion: it must be the specific Poincaré–Weil pairing, positive on all of algebraic H¹. That is the Hodge index statement, and it needs the missing surface. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE; the dichotomy is recorded as a sharpening of MUST-138. |
| 307 | **Minimal axioms for Weil's surface argument over "Spec ℤ × Spec ℤ"** (paper; corpus has only the P¹×P¹ toy, `p1xp1_riemannRoch_intersection`). Function-field template: classes H, V, Δ, Frobenius graphs Γ_n on C×C. Hodge index (D·(H+V) = 0 ⇒ D² ≤ 0) gives \|N_n − q^n − 1\| ≤ 2gq^{n/2}, and it is proved by Riemann–Roch: D² > 0 ⇒ nD or −nD is effective ⇒ positive against an ample class. ℤ-transcription (Connes-type): correspondences Γ_f = ∫f(λ)Ψ(λ)dλ over the scaling flow. Intersection form Γ_f·Γ_{g̃} = W(f⋆g̃), the Weil distribution. Orthogonality to H, V ⟺ pole-orthogonality f̂(0) = f̂(1) = 0, so Hodge index ⟺ Weil positivity ⟺ RH. The four axioms needed: (A) an effectivity cone (f ≥ 0 measures); (B) linear equivalence, i.e. principal divisors on X (the missing "ℚ ⊗_{F₁} ℚ" function field); (C) a Riemann–Roch inequality h⁰(nD) + h⁰(K − nD) ≥ n²D²/2 − O(n); (D) an ample class positive on nonzero effective classes. Test against corpus no-gos: in the blind formalization, effective blind tests have support ≤ log 2 (walls 12/13 refinement), so (A) + blindness leaves no room for (C). The only way out is (B): a non-trivial linear equivalence moving D to effective representatives outside the blind cone, i.e. principal divisors of an arithmetic surface. That is the missing object, not a missing lemma. Classified; no new mathematics without (B). |
| 307a | **Naive model test for axiom (B)/(C)** (paper). Take effective = nonnegative measure p on the scaling line, H = pole functional p ↦ p̂(±i/2), and numerically trivial = Ξ-divisible (n̂ = ΞH, null for the zero-sum pairing; with H(±i/2) = 0 they are also H-orthogonal). An effective representative of an H-orthogonal class f would be p ≥ 0 with p̂ = f̂ at all zeros and p̂(±i/2) = 0. But ∫p·e^{±x/2} = 0 with p ≥ 0 forces p ≡ 0, so every linear system \|nD\| of an H-orthogonal class is empty. In this model the RR inequality h⁰(nD) + h⁰(K−nD) ≥ n²D²/2 − O(n) is literally the statement D² ≤ 0 (RH): RR carries all the content, and the effectivity/ample side is trivial. In the function-field case RR is not an inequality to be proved directly. It is the Euler-characteristic identity χ(nD) = n²D²/2 + O(n) from sheaf cohomology plus Serre duality (h¹ ≥ 0). The missing object is thus a cohomology of line bundles on an F₁-surface with an Euler-characteristic formula, where dimensions are measured on both sides (Connes–Consani's Riemann–Roch for Spec ℤ is the curve-level version). It is not available in the corpus or by any construction found here. |
| 308 | **Candidate h⁰ for correspondences with a quadratic Euler characteristic** (paper, killed). Two kinds of h⁰. (i) *Theta-type*: h⁰(D) = log Σ_{v∈L_D}e^{−πQ_D(v)}. Poisson duality gives h⁰(D) − h⁰(K−D) = −½log det Q_D + …, and with a character shift, log Θ_Q(z) contains the quadratic term πz^TQ^{−1}z. To make this −½W(f⋆f̃) on correspondences, Q must be (the inverse of) the Weil Gram on the span of the Γ_f. That Gram, built from arch + pole − primes via the explicit formula, is positive definite iff RH, and theta series converge only for Q > 0. So theta-type h⁰ needs the positivity as input to be defined at all: circular. Sanity check: rank-2 lattice thetas on ℤ² give only the rank-2-bundle Riemann–Roch on the curve Spec ℤ̄ (log det, no D²). (ii) *Dimension-type*: h⁰ = (log-)dimension of a space of global sections. This needs no positivity, and in the function-field case positivity is an output: RR plus h⁰ ≥ 0. But it needs the space of sections of correspondence line bundles, i.e. functions on the F₁-surface (axiom B of row 307). Learning: an Euler-characteristic route to RH needs a counting-type h⁰ defined before any positivity. Every analytic (theta/Gaussian) h⁰ presupposes positivity. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 309 | **Counting-type h⁰ with quadratic growth** (paper, killed; no corpus arithmetic Hodge-index module, only Deninger-Hodge receivers such as `deningerArithmeticGradedTheta_hodgeStar_iff_riemannHypothesis`). (i) *Dirichlet ring*: sections {a : supp a ⊂ [1,N], supp(ζ⋆a) ⊂ [1,M]} would mean A(s)ζ(s) = B(s) with A, B Dirichlet polynomials, impossible (ζ is not a ratio of Dirichlet polynomials). So h⁰ = 0 identically. (ii) *Log-counts in fixed dimension*: #{v ∈ ℤ^k : \|v_i\| ≤ e^{d_i}} gives log-counts linear in the degrees (curve-type RR). (iii) *Quadratic growth*: in log-coordinates a quadratic term needs lattices of growing rank, e.g. integer polynomials P(x,y) of bidegree ≤ (n,n) with \|coeff\| ≤ e^c, with log-count ≈ c(n+1)². These are exactly the sections ĥ⁰ of arithmetic varieties of relative dimension ≥ 1 over Spec ℤ (Gillet–Soulé arithmetic RR, with arithmetic Hodge index proven by Faltings–Hriljac and Yuan–Zhang). But those pairings are height pairings of the generic fibre (Néron–Tate), not ζ's Weil distribution. The surface that would carry the Weil form is the self-product of the base, and over ℤ that is ℤ⊗_ℤℤ = ℤ (the diagonal); only over F₁ is it a genuine surface. Learning: proven arithmetic Hodge index theorems live one dimension too high. Their quadratic counting growth comes from fibres, while ζ's zeros need the base squared. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 309a | **Why counting-type quadratic growth fails for Spec ℤ × Spec ℤ** (paper). For a Weil-type surface, χ(aH + bV) ≈ (aH+bV)²/2 = ab requires log #H⁰ ≈ ab in Arakelov degrees (logs of bounds). Over Spec ℤ × P¹ this holds: integer polynomials of degree ≤ b with coefficients ≤ e^a have log-count ≈ a(b+1), with b a geometric (fibre) degree. When the second factor is also arithmetic, its degree b enters through the number of coordinates ~ e^b (integers or primes up to e^b), giving log-counts a·e^b, exponential, not ab. A bilinear degree pairing would need #H⁰ = e^{ab} = (e^a)^b, i.e. a tensor product that exponentiates: "ℤ_{≤e^a} ⊗_{F₁} ℤ_{≤e^b} = ℤ_{≤e^{ab}}", powering by a log-sized degree. Exponentiation is not a ring map of ℤ, and Borger's Λ-structure on ℤ is trivial (ψ_p = id, row 256). So no Frobenius lift supplies it. Learning: the arithmetic surface needed for a Weil-type argument must realize multiplication of Arakelov degrees as an operation on sections. That is the precise form of the "missing Frobenius" (wall 5) in the counting-cohomology setting. |
| 310 | **Degree-multiplying operations on Arakelov bundles** (paper, killed). (i) Tensor powers: for a rank-1 Arakelov bundle L_a = (ℤ, \|·\| = e^{−a}), L_a^{⊗b} = L_{ab} (integer b; real t via metric scaling, Pic(Spec ℤ̄) ≅ ℝ). This gives a consistent toy on span{H, V}: ĥ⁰(aH + bV) := log#{n ∈ ℤ : \|n\| ≤ e^{ab}} ≈ ab + log 2 = ½(aH+bV)² + O(1), with H·V = 1, H² = V² = 0. So the quadratic Euler term exists on the H, V part. (ii) The Weil form must come from correspondences Γ_f with Γ_f² = −W(f⋆f̃), and their sections need a Frobenius-type action on lattices. Rank-1 lattices have only the trivial Hecke structure (the unique index-p sublattice pℤ), so Γ_λ acts by degree shift, a ≐ a + log λ, with no prime-dependent self-intersection. Rank-2 lattices form the modular surface, where Hecke correspondences T_p are genuine. On X(1)×X(1) or X₀(N)×X₀(N) the Hodge index holds (complex algebraic surfaces), and Weil's argument plus Eichler–Shimura gives Ramanujan bounds for cusp-form Hecke eigenvalues, not ζ's zeros. ζ sits in the Eisenstein, non-cohomological part (H¹(X(1)) = 0; the Hirzebruch–Zagier intersection numbers of Hecke curves are Eisenstein Fourier coefficients, i.e. class numbers and σ₁, not zeros). Learning: the counting toy reproduces χ = ½D² on degrees. The prime-sensitive part of D² needs Hecke structure, and the only genuine Hecke surfaces put ζ outside their cohomology. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 311 | **Pontryagin signature of the resonance realization** (corpus check, owned). Plan: realize the modular resonances (poles of ξ(2s−1)/ξ(2s)) as eigenvectors of a non-self-adjoint operator carrying an indefinite form, and use Pontryagin's theorem (≤ κ non-real eigenvalue pairs in Π_κ) for a signature count of off-line zeros. The corpus already has the abstract index bound (`realization_card_le`, STCUniversalBoundaryPontryaginCountermodel: a B-self-adjoint operator on a Pontryagin space of index κ carries at most κ of the eigenvalues), the finite-section inertia law (`kernelFiniteSectionNegIndex_eq_card_neg_eigenvalues`), the Weil negative-index horizon (ZetaWeilNegativeIndexHorizon), and the quantitative receiver `zeta_hodge_index_excludes_low_offLine_zeros` (LowHeightHodgeExclusion: level-a Hodge index excludes an off-line zero at (δ, γ₀) under an explicit inequality in (a, δ, γ₀, H, n, A₀), with the unconditional local count). Every natural indefinite form here is the Weil form, whose negative index is the number of resolved off-line pairs, so the "new invariant" is the owned negative index. Unconditional Hodge index is known only at small level (past ½log 2), where the exclusion inequality bites only at bounded height. Owned; no new content. |
| 312 | **ξ′ proportion transfer; identification of the degree-multiplying structure** (paper + corpus). (a) For real entire functions of genus ≤ 1, differentiation does not increase non-real zeros, so off-line(ξ′) ≲ off-line(ξ). Information flows ξ → ξ′ only. The corpus's ≥ 2/3 for ξ′ (`XiDerivCriticalLineProportion`) gives nothing back for ξ. Killed; proportion combinations are retired. (b) The operation demanded by rows 309a–310 is multiplication of Arakelov degrees on sections. Equivalently the semiring structure (ℝ₊, ·, ⋆) with x ⋆ y = x^{log y}, which log carries to (ℝ, +, ·). ℤ is not closed under ⋆ (n^{log m} is transcendental in general), but in characteristic one it exists as Frobenius: on ℝ_max, x ↦ λx is a semiring endomorphism for every λ > 0 (the "λ-th power" in max-plus notation). This is exactly the Frobenius family of Connes–Consani's arithmetic/scaling site, present in the corpus only as a doctrine index (`FieldOneDoctrine.arithmeticScalingSites`, HTGeometry/FieldOneDoctrineAtlas; organizational, not a scheme theory). Toy consistency check: on a tropical circle ℝ/(log p)ℤ (genus 1), the degree-k map has k − 1 fixed points, and the Weil-type bound \|(k−1) − (k+1)\| ≤ 2√k holds. Conclusion: the precise missing theorem is the Hodge index (via an Euler-characteristic RR) on the square of the scaling site, with Frobenius correspondences Ψ_λ. That is Connes–Consani's stated open problem, not a lemma I can supply. |
| 313 | **Toy scaling-site square, truncated at p ≤ P** (paper; classified). Periodic orbits C_p = ℝ₊^*/p^ℤ ≅ ℝ/(log p)ℤ. In log coordinates the scaling Frobenius ψ_λ acts on C_p by translation by log λ, not by multiplication, so its graph Γ_λ ⊂ C_p×C_p meets Δ only if log λ ∈ (log p)ℤ, i.e. λ = p^k, and then Γ_λ = Δ, an excess intersection on a genus-1 torus where Δ² = 0. The Weil prime terms (log p)·p^{−k/2}·g(k log p) arise only through a regularized excess/Lefschetz formula for the flow: each periodic orbit of length log p contributes ℓ/\|1 − P_γ^k\|^{1/2} in Guillemin–Sternberg form. This is Connes' distributional trace formula, and the archimedean term comes from the place at infinity. The individual tori C_p×C_q carry only the trivial genus-1 Hodge structure (\|(k−1)−(k+1)\| ≤ 2√k, row 312), and every prime-sensitive term is an excess intersection invisible to transversal tropical intersection theory. Consequence: no truncation at finitely many primes has non-trivial Hodge content. Positivity, and with it ζ's zeros, appear only on the global square, where the open Hodge index theorem lives. The toy programme is classified; the global object is Connes–Consani's open problem. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE at finite truncation. |
| 314 | **Deforming Frobenius translations off the diagonal** (paper, killed). On C_p ≅ ℝ/(log p)ℤ the graph of translation T_c is {(x, x+c)}. For c ∉ (log p)ℤ it is parallel to Δ and disjoint from it, so the transversal intersection number is 0. As c → k log p it jumps to the excess case Γ = Δ. Every deformation limit therefore gives 0, never the weights (log p)p^{−k/2}. Diagnosis: those weights are Lefschetz indices ℓ_γ/\|det(1 − P_γ^k)\|^{1/2} of the scaling flow's transversal dynamics, where scaling by p contracts the p-adic direction ℤ_p of the adèle class space. A circle (tropical, one-dimensional) model has no transversal directions, hence no Poincaré map and no weights. Any toy carrying the Weil form must keep the p-adic transversals (the adèle class space, or Deninger's foliated space with its transversal structure). There the positivity question becomes the corpus's leafwise-Hodge gate (F₁/Deninger lane, receiver at every level per memory). NO POSITIVE SALVAGE; finite tropical models are closed. |
| 315 | **Formalization check for row 298 (⇐: AP-renormalizable on a strip ⇒ zero-free)** (corpus). Inputs present: the zero-density window bound `eventually_zeroCount_fixedSigma_le` (ZetaFixedSigmaDensity: zeros with Re ρ ≥ σ > ½ are o(T) in Littlewood windows) and Hurwitz tools in sequence form (`eventually_zeroCount_eq_analyticOrder`, `zero_mem_closed_of_tendstoLocallyUniformly`, `disk_zero_free_of_uniform_limit`). Missing: a quantitative Rouché step at the fixed tolerance ε = min_{∂D}\|F\|. The sequence form yields only infinitely many shifted zeros, and with growing almost-period gaps L(ε_k) the count can be sublinear, which gives no contradiction with o(T). Decision: not formalized. The result is a bridge (equivalent to its conclusion, row 298), so by the anti-theatre rule it is a side task, not a producer. Recorded so that a later lane needing quantitative Rouché knows the gap. Also checked: the Bernoulli/special-value angle (ζ is determined by (ζ(2k)) via Carlson) is the owned Riesz criterion (frontier "Classical decay": RH ⟺ Riesz O(x^{¼+ε}) [L]). |
| 316 | **Single-node energy inf E₂ > −∞: weaker than RH?** (Lean read: `RHTower/ArchimedeanUnitNodeEnergy`; killed). The corpus answers it: `riemannHypothesis_iff_bddBelow_unitNodeEnergies` says that for any single m ≥ 2, E_m bounded below ⟺ RH. If RH fails, a fully blind c₀ with A(c₀) < 0, added with a large coefficient to a normalized pair test at m, sends E_m to −∞. So every single node is RH-equivalent: E_m bounded below ⟺ PBAP. Dual reading (paper): for blind PD k, Mellin–Plancherel gives Σ_{n≥1}n^{−½}(k(log n)+k(−log n)) = 2k(0) = (1/2π)∫k̂(t)·2Re ζ(½+it)dt + pole terms. So A(k) = (1/2π)∫k̂·S(t) for an explicit symbol S built from Re ζ(½+it) and Ω(t), and A is unchanged on blind k when S is shifted by any Σ_{n≥2}a_n·2cos(t log n). Hence PBAP ⟸ (⟺ by Hahn–Banach) a certificate S + Σa_n·2cos(t log n) ≥ 0 as a measure. Natural choices: a_n = n^{−½} leaves c − Ω(t) → −∞, and a_n ∝ Λ(n)n^{−½} gives the Weil symbol 2Re ξ′/ξ, which is the critical-zero counting measure on RH. Wall 12 (forced dual measure) again, and MUST-1447 owns the periodization dual. NO POSITIVE SALVAGE. |
| 317 | **Strategic synthesis: every wall, the input that would pass it, and its distance from known mathematics** (paper). Equivalent to RH or quasi-RH (no shortcut): wall 1 (one-sided √x prime count, by Landau), wall 2 (outerness of ζ on Re s > ½, BSY), wall 4 (an HB function E with Ξ = Re E), wall 6 (positivity below absolute convergence, i.e. a zero-free strip), wall 7 (deterministic individual-zero control), wall 10 (half-power Möbius cancellation). Open but strictly weaker, and not sufficient: wall 3 (deterministic local off-line counts o(log T)), which is Lindelöf-strength; μ(½) ≤ 13/84 is known, and LH alone does not give RH. Structural (needs a new object): wall 5 (degree-multiplying Frobenius on sections, the characteristic-one Frobenius of the scaling site, rows 309a–312); wall 8 (a non-affine positivity mechanism); wall 9 (exact use of the Ramanujan coefficient size); wall 11 (multiplicative structure seeing prime powers, the Euler product); wall 12 (spectral realization); wall 13 (Bochner-to-pointwise transfer compatible with blindness; the only linear candidate is the Fourier transform, which reduces to the certificate measure, i.e. wall 12, row 316). Geometric core: Hodge index on the square of the scaling site via a counting Riemann–Roch, which requires wall 5's input. Ranking by distance: the most developed structural object outside the corpus is the scaling site. The corpus holds only a doctrine index (`FieldOneDoctrine.arithmeticScalingSites`), finite truncations are Hodge-trivial (row 313), and the weights need the p-adic transversals (row 314). So the closest sustained target is foundational: build the adèle-class/scaling-site square with its Frobenius correspondences and their excess (Lefschetz) intersection numbers. That is infrastructure towards an open conjecture, not a producer. |
| 318 | **Semi-local adèle-class square with Frobenius correspondences, S = {∞} ∪ {p ≤ P}** (paper; first check passes, second kills). Space X_S = A_S/Γ_S (Γ_S the S-units), with the scaling action of C_S. Check 1: the distributional (Lefschetz) trace of U(h) is supported on the periodic orbits (points with x_v = 0 at one place v). It gives 2h(1)log Λ + Σ_{v∈S}∫′_{ℚ_v^*}h(u^{−1})/\|1−u\|_v d*u, i.e. exactly the semi-local Weil form W_S. At v = p, for unramified h, this is Σ_k log p·p^{−k/2}(h(p^k) + h(p^{−k})), Connes' semi-local trace formula. So Ψ_h·Δ = W_S(h) with Δ² regularized by log Λ. PASS (known). Check 2 (Hodge index on the S-square): W_S is the explicit-formula functional of ζ_S = Γ_ℝ·Π_{p≤P}(1−p^{−s})^{−1}, which has no zeros, only poles at s = 2πik/log p on Re s = 0, i.e. at t = 2πk/log p + i/2, off the real axis. Their contributions −k̂(2πk/log p + i/2) are complex, so W_S is indefinite as soon as the aperture exceeds log of the first prime not in S; below that aperture W_S = W. So no finite-place truncation of the adèle square satisfies a Hodge index: positivity can only emerge in the global limit S → all places. That is node criticality (wall 11) in geometric form, and it matches the small-aperture certificates (positivity only below log of the first missing prime). Consequence for the programme: the Hodge-index object is irreducibly global, so no finite semi-local model can be built and certified first. Foundational building would have to be global from the start. KILLED as a staged programme. |
| 319 | **Symmetry-restricted Weil positivity; blind cone as prime-wandering vectors** (paper; killed → REDISCOVERED, with one structural survivor). (a) *Symmetry*: W is a translation-invariant (convolution) form on log-scale tests, so every operator commuting with W is a spectral multiplier φ(D). Its isotypic restrictions are spectral windows, i.e. height restrictions (high → ZAL, low → numerics). Inversion x ↦ −x gives the self-dual class, which is still RH-equivalent. Non-commuting symmetries (g ↦ g(k·)) are row 294. (b) *Prime-power blindness is a tiling condition*: by Poisson, g = f⋆f̃ vanishes on (log p)ℤ∖0 ⟺ ν = \|f̂\|² has constant ω_p-periodization, ω_p = 2π/log p ⟺ f is a wandering vector for the dilation D_p (the system {D_p^k f} is orthogonal). (c) *Akhiezer–Krein factorization*: a nonnegative integrable entire function of exponential type is \|F\|² with F of half the type. So every compactly supported PD g is a single autocorrelation, and PBAP ⟺ Q_∞(f) ≥ 0 for every compactly supported f that is wandering for every D_p separately. (d) *Survivor, the marginal law*: push ν to the Bohr torus 𝕋^∞ = Π_pS¹ by t ↦ (p^{−it})_p. The prime part of W is Σ_p⟨f, K_p(D_p)f⟩ with K_p = log p·(Poisson_{p^{−1/2}} − 1), a sum of one-coordinate functions (Λ is supported on prime powers). Hence it depends only on the one-dimensional marginals of ν, and blindness at the prime powers ⟺ every marginal is Haar. Joint Haar is impossible (log ℚ₊^* is dense, so g ≡ 0). This is wall 11 in measure form: log 4 is a coordinate mode of z₂, log 6 = z₂z₃ is a mixed mode that W never sees. PBAP = archimedean positivity on line measures with uniform one-dimensional torus marginals. No new inequality: Bagchi's independent model satisfies the analogue trivially, and the off-line content is the deterministic coupling t ↦ (p^{−it}). Not formalized (Akhiezer factorization is absent from mathlib; the reformulation is Poisson summation). |
| 320 | **Pointwise periodic certificate for PBAP; JAS balanced/unbalanced split** (paper; killed twice). (a) *Conjecture*: there are mean-zero ω_p-periodic F_p (frequencies k log p) with Ω(t) − Σ_pF_p(t) ≥ 0 pointwise, Ω(t) = Re ψ(¼+it/2) − log π. Every blind ν annihilates each F_p (row 319), so this would give ∫νΩ ≥ 0, i.e. PBAP with the pole term dropped. *False*: the pole term is indispensable. Ω < 0 on \|t\| < t₀ (t₀ slightly above 2π, Ω(0) = ψ(¼) − log π < −5). A window test (supp f ⊂ [0, log 2], automatically blind) of prolate type has time–band product ≈ 2.2 and puts most of \|f̂\|² in \|t\| < t₀, so ∫\|f̂\|²Ω < 0 there, while W = pole + arch > 0 (small-aperture positivity). So arch alone is negative on the blind cone and only the off-axis evaluation ĝ(±i/2) restores the sign. In zero language, on blind g: Σ_zeros ĝ(γ) − Σ_poles ĝ(γ) = arch(g). The poles at t = ±i/2 act as maximal-depth "anti-zeros", and any certificate must couple the line to the strip edge; no density on ℝ can do it. (b) *JAS constant vectors*: expanding Π_p(1 + uA_p + ūB_p) over coprime squarefree n₁, n₂ gives T = Re Σu^{ω(n₁)}ū^{ω(n₂)}(n₁n₂)^{−1}K(n₂/n₁), K(x) = Σ_ρx^{ρ−½}/(ρ(1−ρ)). Balanced terms (ω(n₁) = ω(n₂) = k on a dyadic shell) have n₂/n₁ ∈ (2^{−k}, 2^k), so x^{δ} ≤ 2^{kδ}: they are blind to the depth δ at leading order (the Laplace transform of the balanced log-ratio walk sees \|Σp^{iγ}\|², with the δ-weights cancelling). Detection lives only in unbalanced terms with x ≍ N^k, where K needs √x-precision of ψ (wall 1), which matches the JAS terminal classification (abscissa ½+Θ). No survivor beyond these two diagnoses. |
| 321 | **Multiplicative generation of the blind cone** (paper; killed → INSIGNIFICANT; structural facts only). (a) The blind PD cone B is closed under Schur products (ĝ₁g₂ = ĝ₁∗ĝ₂/2π ≥ 0, and g₁g₂ vanishes wherever g₁ does) and under integer dilations (row 294). Neither operation increases the support, so B is not generated from window tests: support-increasing blind constructions must be built at full aperture. (b) *Single-prime pieces are Golay structures*: f is D_p-wandering iff its Zak transform Z_pf(x,θ) = Σ_jf(x+j log p)e^{ijθ} has ∫_0^{log p}\|Z_pf(x,θ)\|²dx constant in θ. Example: a, a on the two halves of [0, ℓ), then b, −b on the two halves of [ℓ, 2ℓ) (ℓ = log p), since \|a+be^{iθ}\|² + \|a−be^{iθ}\|² = 2(a²+b²). A finitely supported step sequence alone cannot be wandering (its last-lag autocorrelation is c₀c_{L−1} ≠ 0), so wandering vectors of support Mℓ are function-valued complementary sets. Products Π_{p≤e^a}g_p of single-prime-blind PD g_p are blind at aperture a. (c) *Kill*: Q_∞ has no product law. ∫(ν₁∗ν₂)Ω = ∫∫ν₁(s)ν₂(u)Ω(s+u) with Ω even, increasing, convex near 0 and concave (≍ log t) at infinity, and the pole term ∫g₁g₂cosh(x/2) does not factor. So positivity of Q_∞ on products says nothing about irreducible blind tests with concentrated ν, which are exactly where PBAP is sharp (node criticality). Spreading by products only makes tests safer. |
| 322 | **Automorphic positivities against Eisenstein series; Weil ground states** (paper; killed → walls 6 and 12, ground-state mechanism owned). (a) *Rankin–Selberg*: L(s, f×f̄) = ζ(s)L(s, Sym²f), so an off-line zero ρ makes E(·,ρ) (regularized) orthogonal to \|f\|²y^k for every holomorphic or Maass Hecke eigenform f, and to every Bergman diagonal Σh(t_f)\|f\|² ≥ 0. No contradiction: on-line zeros do the same (the Eisenstein coefficient of \|f\|² vanishes at every γ), QUE limits pair with the regularized ⟨1, E(·,s)⟩ = 0, and E(z,s) is positive only for s > 1 (Chowla–Selberg real zeros of E*(z,s) in (½,1) for large y). Pairing \|f\|² with positive Poincaré series P_ψ reduces to \|a_f(n)\|² ≥ 0, which is wall 6. The Maass–Selberg norm ‖Λ^TE(·,s)‖² ≥ 0 excludes poles of φ only in Re s > ½, i.e. it gives the edge non-vanishing ζ(1+it) ≠ 0 and nothing inside. (b) *Horocycle rate*: RH ⟺ error o(y^{3/4−ε}) for horocycle averages. The ζ-zeros enter only as cusp resonances (poles of φ at s = ρ/2) behind the contour, and no mixing or spectral-gap input sees them (wall 12). (c) *Ground states*: W − μ(a) on PW_a is PSD with a kernel, so by Carathéodory–Fejér/Krein the ground state has only real zeros unconditionally. Owned: `exists_real_rooted_weil_groundState`, `CaratheodoryFejer*`. Under ¬RH, μ(a) ≍ −e^{cδa} (the anti-aligned F(γ₀)² term), so RH ⟺ μ(a) ≥ −e^{o(a)} is the abscissa law again. |
| 323 | **Off-line content of the Kronecker coupling t ↦ (p^{−it})** (paper; killed → wall 1; corpus owns the a.e. side, `RandomEulerObservableAbscissa`, `PrimeTorusKroneckerDensity`, `BohrHullContinuity`). C_σ = {z ∈ 𝕋^∞ : Σ_p z_pp^{−σ} converges} has full Haar measure for σ > ½ (Rademacher–Menshov). C = ∩_{σ>½}C_σ is invariant under the Kronecker flow T_τz = (z_pp^{−iτ}): convergence of a Dirichlet series at σ gives convergence on Re w > σ. Arbitrary rotations (e.g. by (χ(p))_p) do not preserve C. The orbit of 1 is the pole orbit: Σ_pp^{−σ−iτ} diverges for every σ < 1 because of the PNT main term ∫x^{−s}dx/log x (modulus x^{1−σ}/log x). So 1 ∉ C, and the statement that holds is: RH ⟺ the renormalized orbit Σ_pp^{−s} − ∫_2^xu^{−s}du/log u converges for all Re s > ½ ⟺ π − li = O(x^{½+ε}). The deterministic coupling's off-line content is exactly √x-precision of the prime count (wall 1). The random model escapes the wall because z = 1 (the pole) has Haar measure zero. No survivor beyond the invariance of C, which is too weak to formalize. |
| 324 | **Why RH ⟺ PBAP is a conductor-one phenomenon** (paper; structural survivor, not formalized; corpus has the ingredients: `archimedeanWeilSymbol_div_conductor`, `riemannHypothesis_iff_primeBlindPositive_unconditional`). (a) *Twisted PBAP is vacuous at large conductor*: for primitive χ ≠ χ₀ the archimedean density of L(s,χ) is Re ψ(¼+κ/2+it/2) + log(q/π) (κ ∈ {0,1} the parity). Since Re ψ(a+iy) = ψ(a) + Σ_ny²/((n+a)((n+a)²+y²)) ≥ ψ(a), it is pointwise positive once log(q/π) > −ψ(¼) (even χ, q ≥ 216) or > −ψ(¾) (odd χ, q ≥ 10). There is no pole, so arch_χ ≥ 0 on the whole PD cone and the weight 0 is admissible. PBAP_χ is then unconditionally true and says nothing about GRH: for these L-functions GRH ⟺ χΛ ∈ C_χ is a membership problem, not an existence problem. (b) *For ζ the pole makes Q_∞ unbounded below*: f = φ(·−L) − φ(·+L) gives g(0) = 2‖φ‖² but pole = 2AB ≍ −e^L(∫φe^{x/2})². So 0 ∉ C(c) for every constant shift c, and admissible weights must compensate at log n ≈ 2L. That forces Σ_{e^{2L}<n≤(1+ε)e^{2L}}w_n ≈ εe^{2L} at every scale, i.e. mean one (a PNT shadow), which is the first step of uniqueness (C ⊆ {Λ}). (c) *Lesson*: the existence form of RH (cone nonempty ⟺ RH) comes from the pole together with Ω(0) < 0. A shifted family Ω + c (c > 0) gives RH ⟺ Λ ∈ C(c), which is again the lower-boundedness criterion inf W/g(0) > −∞. No new producer: an explicit non-Λ weight in C_a (e.g. a Selberg truncation Λ_R) would perturb W_Λ by Σ(Λ−Λ_R)n^{−½}g(log n), which has no sign, and C_a shrinks to {Λ}. |
| 325 | **Energy (Dirichlet-to-Neumann) representation of the blind Weil form** (paper; killed → wall 12; idea from the stream function in `TwistedArcMinimumPrincipleProof`). (a) *Conjecture*: there is a linear map f ↦ ψ_f to fields on an arithmetic domain D with W(f⋆f̃) = ∫_D\|∇ψ_f\|²dω + flux(f), where the flux vanishes on blind f. This would be a Bochner-cone (quadratic) positivity, avoiding the pointwise cone of wall 13. (b) *Kill*: by Green, ∫_D\|∇ψ\|² = ⟨f, Λ_{DN}f⟩, so the conjecture asks for the blind Weil operator as a Dirichlet-to-Neumann map. Every positive DN map of a one-dimensional boundary is the spectral form of a string or canonical system (Krein, de Branges), so this is exactly a spectral realization of the zero measure (wall 12, route SZ). The corpus's stream functions yK₁(ky)sin(kx) enter the uniqueness proof only through a minimum principle (a pointwise sign), not through an energy identity. (c) *Local reading* (survivor, interpretive): Ω(t) = Re ψ(¼+it/2) − log π is the phase derivative of the archimedean factor Γ_ℝ(½+it)π^{−it/2}, i.e. the Wigner time delay of the local scattering. It is negative at low energy (\|t\| ≲ 2π), and on blind tests only the pole term ĝ(±i/2) compensates (row 320). So the blind explicit formula is a Levinson-type balance: zeros = archimedean time delay + bound-state (pole) term. A spectral-shift positivity (Krein: 0 ≤ ξ ≤ 1 for rank-one positive perturbations) controls ξ, not dξ, so it gives no sign for the zero measure. |
| 326 | **Three foreign spectral or recurrence mechanisms** (paper; all killed). (a) *Iwasawa main conjecture as a template*: Mazur–Wiles gives an honest finite-dimensional spectral realization (zeros of L_p = eigenvalues of γ−1 on X_∞⊗ℚ_p). But all the pairings on Iwasawa modules (Spiegelung, Perrin-Riou/Schneider p-adic heights) take p-adic values, so there is no order and no positivity. The archimedean analogue (flow = γ) is Deninger/Meyer H¹, owned; wall 5/12. (b) *Bost–Connes / primon gas*: Z(β) = Tr e^{−βlog N} = ζ(β), so RH = Fisher zeros on Re β = ½. Lee–Yang theorems control fugacity zeros of interacting ferromagnets, not temperature zeros. The free gas's fugacity product Π(1−z_pp^{−β})^{−1} has no zeros, and the fermionic one factorizes, with zeros at z_p = −p^β. No mechanism. (c) *Weak recurrence is not weaker*: by Bagchi's support theorem the closure of {ζ(·+iτ)} in H(D) is the closure of the zero-free functions on D, which by Hurwitz is the zero-free functions together with 0. So ζ ∈ closure (weak recurrence on D) ⟺ ζ has no zero in D ⟺ strong recurrence on D. Hence "weak recurrence ∧ ZAL ⟹ RH" has a hypothesis already equivalent to RH, and the BAG ladder collapse gives no reduction. This matches the BAG core (row 707 of that lane: ζ is the non-generic point z = 1) and row 323. |
| 326a | **Correction to row 326(c).** The orbit closure of ζ(·+iτ) contains the orbit itself, which includes translates with zeros, so it is not the support of the random Euler product. The relevant object is the ω-limit set Ω_D (accumulation as τ → ∞). Bagchi gives Ω_D ⊇ supp = {zero-free on D} ∪ {0}. Under ZAL, for D ⊂ {σ > ½+δ} the translates are eventually zero-free on D, so Ω_D ⊆ supp, and weak recurrence on D ⟺ ζ zero-free on D. Without ZAL, Ω_D can contain functions with zeros. So weak recurrence (WR) is NOT known to be RH-equivalent. The corpus reduction "WR ∧ ZAL ⟹ RH" stands, and RH ⟹ WR. What is unconditionally available is B²-almost periodicity of ζ on σ > ½. Along almost periods τ_k with ε_k → 0 this gives WR at a density-one set of base points t₀, by a Borel–Cantelli subsequence for each t₀. Base points near off-line zeros form a density-zero set, so it says nothing there (wall 7). Open core of BAG, restated: pointwise WR at the single base point t₀ = 0, i.e. a non-statistical recurrence of the shift orbit. |
| 327 | **Edge taming by node density; non-diagonal Laguerre–Pólya preservers** (paper; two kills). (a) *x-space form of the blind energy*: Ω = −γ−log π + Σ_m[1/(m+1) − (m+¼)/((m+¼)²+t²/4)] gives arch(g) = cg(0) − p.v.∫g(x)K_∞(x)dx with K_∞(x) = 2e^{−\|x\|/2}/(1−e^{−2\|x\|}) = 2Σ_me^{−(2m+½)\|x\|} (the real place's "orbit sum"). So on blind g, W(g) = cg(0) + p.v.∫g(x)κ(x)dx with κ = 2cosh(x/2) − K_∞, which grows like e^{\|x\|/2}. The pole dominates at large aperture with the sign of the edge correlation f(b)f(−b), but nodes near x have spacing ≍ e^{−x}. A blind g with ‖g‖_{C¹} ≤ M therefore has \|g(x)\| ≤ Me^{−x}, and the tail \|x\| > X contributes O(Me^{−X/2}). The RH content of PBAP sits in the sparse-node region (small x, the first few primes). *Kill*: this is not an effective finite reduction. Under RH, inf W/g(0) = 0 over blind g is approached only by tests whose ‖g‖_{C¹}/g(0) blows up (ground states of PW_b are wild at the critical height ≍ e^{2b}), so the tail error is not uniformly small; this is the cofinal law. (b) *Borcea–Brändén*: an LP-preserver T has symbol T[e^{−zw}] in two-variable LP, and RH would follow from Ξ = T(F) with explicit F ∈ LP and explicit T. The natural representations are diagonal: Ξ = ∫Φ(u)cos(zu)du gives T(z^k) = m_kz^k, but the moments are not a multiplier sequence (corpus). The splitting Ξ = g·f (multiplication preserver) is circular, and the heat preserver needs F at negative time, which is not LP (Rodgers–Tao). No arithmetic non-diagonal candidate exists. This restates the open MSC need ("transport must mix Dirichlet coefficients across n") without a new candidate. |
| 328 | **Pointwise weak recurrence at base point 0 (BAG open core)** (paper; killed → circular; corpus: `ZetaWeakRecurrenceLadderCollapse`, `isStripAlmostPeriodic_zeta`, which is for Re s > 1 only). Mechanism attempt: by the Hardy–Littlewood AFE, ζ(s+iτ) = Σ_{n≤x}n^{−s−iτ} − x^{1−s−iτ}/(1−s−iτ) + O(x^{−σ}) with x ≍ τ. Kronecker alignment (p^{−iτ} ≈ 1 for p ≤ x₀) makes the x₀-smooth part ≈ E_{x₀}(s) = Π_{p≤x₀}(1−p^{−s})^{−1}. So weak recurrence on D requires the rough part at the aligned τ to approximate ζ_{>x₀}(s) = ζ(s)Π_{p≤x₀}(1−p^{−s}). If ζ has a zero in D, so does ζ_{>x₀}. Voronin/Bagchi approximants of the rough part are (truncated) Euler products, which are zero-free, so by Rouché the needed τ lie in the measure-small exceptional set where ζ deviates from its Euler model. Those are exactly heights carrying off-line zeros near ρ+iτ. Hence pointwise WR at t₀ = 0 ⟺ the off-line zero configuration in D recurs at arbitrarily large heights. It is a statement about zeros with no independent arithmetic content: RH ⟹ WR trivially, and ¬RH ⟹ WR needs recurrence of off-line zeros, which no mechanism forces. BAG open core classified: circular through zero recurrence. |
| 329 | **The square over the sphere spectrum: Spec(ℤ⊗_Sℤ) and THH(ℤ) as diagonal self-intersection** (paper; killed; new to the corpus, which has CC ζ-cycles in `CCMConnesConsaniZetaCycles` but no THH). In the derived picture the square of Spec ℤ over S is Spec(ℤ∧_Sℤ) with π₀ = ℤ: a homotopical thickening of the diagonal, not a surface. The derived self-intersection of the diagonal is THH(ℤ) = ℤ⊗_{ℤ⊗_Sℤ}ℤ, and Bökstedt gives THH_*(ℤ) = ℤ (degree 0), ℤ/k (degree 2k−1), 0 otherwise. (a) *The only arithmetic in it is torsion orders*: the regularized torsion Euler characteristic Σ_k(−1)^{2k−1}log\|ℤ/k\|·k^{−s} = ζ′(s), i.e. Tr(log N·N^{−s}) for the number operator. That is the primon-gas / Bost–Connes partition data (row 326b), which reproduces the Dirichlet series, not a spectral realization of its zeros. (b) *Rationally trivial*: THH(ℤ)⊗ℚ = ℚ and TP(ℤ)_ℚ ≈ ℚ((t)). There is no characteristic-zero cohomology with infinitely many eigenvalues, and so no carrier for zeros or a Hodge form. Hesselholt's TP description of Hasse–Weil zeta needs a Frobenius (char p). Over ℤ the only ring endomorphism is the identity, so there are no Frobenius-twisted bimodules to trace (the same obstruction as rows 310 and 313). (c) Connes–Consani ζ-cycles live on the scaling site, not on ℤ⊗_Sℤ (owned). No survivor: the sphere-spectrum square is the wrong (too small) square. |
| 330 | **Maass–Selberg Gram matrices at the resonances s = ρ/2** (paper; killed → wall 4; corpus: `cutoffConstantTerm_ne_zero`, the Lagarias–Suzuki line law via a self-adjoint pseudo-Laplacian). The line law confines zeros of the combination c_Y = Λ(2s)Y^s + Λ(2s−1)Y^{1−s}, never zeros of Λ(2s) itself. Attempt: use the truncated Eisenstein series at the resonances s_j = ρ_j/2 (where A = Λ(2s) vanishes) as vectors in L²(SL₂(ℤ)\H). Maass–Selberg gives ‖Λ^YE*(·,s_j)‖² = \|B_j\|²Y^{1−2σ_j}/(1−2σ_j) > 0, positive for every β because σ_j = β_j/2 < ½. The full Gram is G_{jk} = B_jB̄_kY^{1−s_j−s̄_k}/(1−s_j−s̄_k). Since Re(1−s_j−s̄_k) > 0, 1/(1−s_j−s̄_k) = ∫_0^∞e^{−(1−s_j−s̄_k)x}dx is a Cauchy/Pick kernel, PSD for every configuration. The FE E*(z,s) = E*(z,1−s) adds nothing. Diagnosis: automorphic L² Gram structures pair s with s̄ (always PSD), whereas RH needs a pairing of ρ with 1−ρ̄ that is PSD only on the line. That is the Weil form, which has no L² carrier (wall 12). The pseudo-Laplacian mechanism inherently confines only axis combinations (wall 4). |
| 331 | **Interfaces audit and the Lorentzian reading** (corpus audit + paper; nothing usable). (a) *Open Prop interfaces in RHTower* (~60 listed with `rg "^def …(Principle\|Hypothesis\|Law\|Bound): Prop"`). The sampled ones are at RH strength or stronger. Example: `MobiusAdditiveTriangleLinearUpperBound` (Σ_{m+n≤N}μ(m)μ(n) ≤ C(N+1)) gives \|Σμ(n)e^{−nx}\|² ≤ C/(1−e^{−x}), i.e. Σμ(n)e^{−nx} = O(x^{−½}). That already excludes off-line zeros (they give x^{−β} growth) and is consistent with RH plus simple zeros. So none is an RH-free tractable target. `TwistedArcMinimumPrinciple` is the one interface that was proved, and it fed uniqueness, not existence. (b) *Lorentzian/Hodge–Riemann reading*: Weil positivity ⟺ the intersection form on span{H, V, D_f = ∫f(λ)Γ_λ} has signature (1, ∞) (Hodge index ⟺ Lorentzian volume quadric). Brändén–Huh closure (products, derivatives, nonnegative linear substitutions) needs a basis with nonnegative intersection numbers. The arithmetic basis {Γ_n} has Γ_m·Γ_n = W(log(m/n)), whose archimedean density is negative near 0 (Ω(0) < −5) and whose prime part has the minus sign. So no nonnegative generating cone exists and the Lorentzian toolkit has nothing to propagate. Same wall as row 309a (no counting-type positivity on the square). |
| 332 | **Redheffer spectral route** (paper; killed → wall 6; new to the corpus, which has no Redheffer module). R_n (R_ij = 1 if j = 1 or i \| j) is a rank-one perturbation of the divisibility matrix D_n (unipotent, D_n⁻¹ = Möbius matrix): R_n = D_n + (𝟙−e₁)e₁^T. Hence det R_n = 1 + e₁^TD_n⁻¹(𝟙−e₁) = ⟨e₁, D_n⁻¹𝟙⟩ = M(n). With N = D_n − I nilpotent of index ⌊log₂n⌋+1, M(n) = Σ_k(−1)^k⟨e₁,N^k𝟙⟩ is P. Hall's alternating count of divisor chains 1 < d₁ \| … \| d_k ≤ n. The k-th count has Dirichlet series (ζ−1)^k, so the expansion is the Neumann series of 1/ζ = 1/(1+(ζ−1)), convergent only for Re s > α with ζ(α) = 2 (α ≈ 1.73). The chain residues at s = 1 cancel exactly (1/ζ is regular there), and what remains is analytic-continuation data: wall 6. *Spectral restatement* (assuming the known asymptotics λ_± ≍ ±√n for the two dominant eigenvalues): det R_n = λ₊λ₋Π_{other}λ_i over the ⌊log₂n⌋−1 non-unit, non-dominant eigenvalues. So RH ⟺ their geometric mean ≤ 2^{−½+o(1)}, and PNT ⟺ ≤ 2^{o(1)}. *Kill*: R_n is highly non-normal (a nilpotent part of index log₂n), so eigenvalue products carry no norm or positivity structure, and the bound is again a cancellation statement about chain counts. No survivor. |
| 333 | **Weil positivity on log-concave spectral tests** (paper; killed → empty class or owned heat trace). (a) *Compact support makes the class empty*: a nonnegative integrable entire function of exponential type satisfies ∫log ĝ/(1+t²) > −∞ (Krein; Paley–Wiener). A log-concave integrable ĝ has log ĝ ≤ −c\|t\| + C, so the log-integral diverges. No compactly supported test has log-concave ĝ, and interior zeros (Fejér-type kernels) are excluded too. (b) *Schwartz extension*: even log-concave ĝ ≥ 0 is unimodal at 0, so for an off-line zero a+iδ the on-line zeros with \|γ\| ≤ a (at least N(a) of them, each ≥ ĝ(a)) outweigh 4\|ĝ(a±iδ)\| unless the first zero is off-line. The class therefore cannot detect high zeros, and its positivity is "lowest zero on the line + density", i.e. numerics. The canonical member ĝ = e^{−ct²} is the zero heat trace H(c) = Σ_ρe^{−cγ²} (route TH: "heat trace complete monotonicity is another RH receiver"). Its explicit-formula side is a cancellation of pole ≍ 2e^{c/4} against the prime sum, so no structural proof. Lesson: positivity classes defined by shape (unimodal, log-concave, PF) at the origin are blind to high zeros; RH-sensitive classes must localize at arbitrary heights, which forces sign-changing g (walls 12/13 refinement). |
| 334 | **A Petersson-norm lift of the blind Weil energy** (paper; killed → walls 12 and 4). Corpus link: the (N1)/blind world in additive form is functions f on ℝ with f(1/x) = \|x\|f(x) (inversion) and 1-periodization a degree-1 trigonometric polynomial (f̂(n) = 0 for \|n\| ≥ 2). These are the S and T constraints of SL₂(ℤ), which is why the modular arc carries the uniqueness proof (`TwistedArcGenerator`, `TwistedArcN1Reduction`). *Conjecture*: an automorphic lift h ↦ F_h on SL₂(ℤ)\H with W(h⋆h̃) = ‖F_h‖² (regularized Petersson) on blind h. *Kill*: (i) The Petersson spectral side is Σ_j\|⟨F,u_j⟩\|² + (1/4π)∫\|⟨F,E(·,½+it)⟩\|²dt + constant term. It is a sum over the Laplace spectrum and the flat line density, and can never produce atoms at ζ-zeros: those are resonances, poles of φ(s) = ξ(2s−1)/ξ(2s) at s = ρ/2 off the unitary axis. (ii) For incomplete Eisenstein series E(·\|ψ), unfolding gives ‖E(·\|ψ)‖² = ∫\|ψ\|²dy/y² + Re(1/4π)∫ψ̃(½+it)\overline{ψ̃(½−it)}φ(½+it)dt. Since \|φ(½+it)\| = 1, its positivity is the trivial bound \|⟨ψ,Uψ⟩\| ≤ ‖ψ‖² for a unitary U. (iii) Matching the negative archimedean density Ω (Ω(0) < −5) to a positive Plancherel density would need the discrete part to absorb it exactly, which is again a spectral realization. Lesson: modular geometry gives exact pointwise principles (uniqueness), but its L² structures see ζ only through the unimodular scattering phase. |
| 335 | **Linear (subspace) form of blindness: jointly wandering subspaces and distance-avoiding sets** (paper; killed → INSIGNIFICANT/numerics). Single-f blindness is quadratic, but a subspace V with V ⊥ D_p^kV for all primes p and k ≠ 0 consists of blind vectors, and PBAP restricted to V is linear: P_VAP_V ≥ 0. A line span{f} is wandering iff f is blind, so PBAP ⟺ A ≥ 0 on every jointly wandering subspace. *Geometric examples*: L²(E) for E ⊂ ℝ avoiding the distance set D = {k log p}, i.e. an independent set of the distance graph on ℝ with edge set D. Then g = f⋆f̃ is supported in E − E ⊂ ℝ∖D ("geometrically blind"). Constraints: a single interval has length ≤ log 2, since (−log 2, log 2) is the largest gap of D ∪ (−D), and two intervals of length ℓ need a gap of length 2ℓ. Example: [0, 0.2] ∪ [0.895, 1.095] (difference set inside (log 2, log 3)), total 0.4 < log 2. The pieces of E at distance x have size < gap(D near x) ≍ e^{−x}. *Kill*: geometrically blind tests are porous, and by the edge-taming estimate (row 327) their large-x pieces carry O(e^{−x/2}) of the energy. So PBAP restricted to them is a bounded-aperture statement plus a tame tail: numerics in the sense of row 295. By node criticality the RH content sits in cancellation-blind tests (g(log n) = 0 by sign changes, not by support), which no wandering-subspace geometry captures. |
| 336 | **Synthesis of cycles 319–335** (no formal output; every row a kill or classification). (1) *PBAP structure*: blind ⟺ D_p-wandering ⟺ Haar torus marginals ⟺ constant fibre norms in every p-Zak fibration. The p = 2 fibration alone leaves a full unitary-loop gauge freedom U(θ) on L²[0, log 2), which makes PBAP_2 fail, and the other primes cut that freedom exactly (node criticality). PBAP is the balance (1/2π)∫νΩ + 2ν(i/2) ≥ 0: low-frequency negative mass against the off-axis value at the strip edge, mediated by multi-period tiling. Every shape, support, uncertainty or subspace restriction either loses the pole coupling or becomes bounded-aperture numerics. (2) *Carriers*: every L² or automorphic positivity tested (Rankin–Selberg, Bergman, Maass–Selberg Grams, Petersson lifts, ground states, DN energies) pairs s with s̄ or sees ζ only through the unimodular scattering phase. RH needs the reflection pairing ρ ↔ 1−ρ̄, which only the Weil form has (walls 4/12). (3) *Recurrence (BAG)*: the pointwise core is circular through off-line zero recurrence. Weak recurrence alone is not RH-equivalent; with ZAL it is. (4) *Foreign frameworks* (Iwasawa, Bost–Connes, THH over S, Redheffer, Lorentzian, Borcea–Brändén): each lacks either an order (p-adic), a carrier (rationally trivial), convergence (Neumann series of 1/ζ beyond Re s = 1.73) or a nonnegative generating basis. (5) *Durable rules*: shape classes at the origin are blind to high zeros; the pole is indispensable on the blind cone; modular geometry yields pointwise principles (uniqueness), not quadratic positivity (existence). Open shape unchanged: a carrier whose natural Hermitian pairing is the functional-equation reflection, i.e. the geometric Hodge form. |
| 337 | **Quantum-graph Hilbert–Pólya realizations of the prime side** (paper + Lean; CANNOT-546). *Conjecture tested*: a metric graph whose edges have lengths log p, with unitary vertex coupling, has a Kottos–Smilansky trace formula reproducing the prime side Σ_nΛ(n)n^{−½}δ_{log n} (up to amplitudes), with the coupling producing the zeros as interference. *Obstruction*: log det(1 − σD(z)) = −Σ_mTr((σD(z))^m)/m with z_e = e^{ikL_e}. Its z^α coefficient is the total amplitude of orbits traversing edge e exactly α_e times, at length Σα_eL_e. Since the log p are ℚ-independent, distinct α give distinct lengths, and Λ = 0 off prime powers kills every mixed monomial. So det(1 − σD(z)) = Π_edet(1 − σ_eD_e(z_e)). *Theorem* [L] `RHTower/QuantumGraphPrimePowerDecoupling`, standard axioms, wired: `eq_zero_of_secular_eq_prod`, `eq_zero_of_det_one_sub_smul_eq_prod`, `eq_zero_of_det_eq_prod_principalBlock`. A unitary σ whose secular determinant factors over the blocks, or merely whose spectrum with multiplicity is the union of the spectra of its principal blocks, is block diagonal. Proof: setting z ≡ t turns the identity into det(t − σ) = Π_edet(t − σ_e) for t ≠ 0; continuity at t = 0 gives det σ = Π_e det σ_e; Hadamard on each block bounds \|det σ_e\| by its restricted column norms, and with \|det σ\| = 1 and unit columns this forces every restricted column norm to be 1. Hence no coupling: the spectrum is a union of single-edge lattices (the spurious Euler-factor lattices of row 318), and the decaying amplitudes p^{−k/2} additionally need lossy edges (resonances). *Survivor*: the block-rigidity law. *Lesson*: in any periodic-orbit realization the composite-length cancellation cannot come from orbit interference; the coupling that creates zeros must live in the non-orbit (archimedean or pole) term. This agrees with rows 320/325, where the pole and archimedean balance carries all off-line content. |
| 338 | **Where an off-line zero hides in PBAP: the odd sector and the sinh moment** (paper; mechanism, no producer). (a) *Lagrangian reading*: the KKT conditions of min⟨f, A_∞f⟩ under ⟨f, S_nf⟩ = 0 (S_n = cos(D log n)) give A_∞ − Σλ_nS_n with Lagrange multipliers λ_n, i.e. the Weil operator with weights λ. Corpus uniqueness ("unique repair") says the multipliers are Λ(n)n^{−½}. Off the aperture cut, every term except the rank-two pole is a function of D, so the Weil operator is multiplication by the zero measure in Fourier space. (b) *Parity split of the pole*: pole(f⋆f̃) = 2AB with A = F(i/2), B = F(−i/2). Even f give 2A² ≥ 0, odd f give −2B² ≤ 0 with B = ∫f(y)sinh(y/2)dy. So PBAP's sharp content is the odd sector: (1/2π)∫\|F\|²Ω ≥ 2\|F(−i/2)\|² for odd blind f, a reproducing-kernel inequality for the indefinite weight Ω on odd PW_b. Odd F vanish at t = 0 (avoiding Ω(0) < −5) but can concentrate near \|t\| ≈ 3, where Ω < 0. (c) *Dangerous tests under ¬RH*: with an off-line zero at depth δ, f ≈ sgn(y)e^{−(½−δ)\|y\|} on [−b,b] has ‖f‖ = O(1), bounded arch, and pole ≍ −2e^{2δb}/δ². The node-criticality theorem says prime-power-wandering corrections can then be added without losing this gain, and arch alone is ≥ Ω_min·g(0). So RH ⟺ "slowly decaying (rate < ½) near-odd functions cannot be made D_p-wandering for all p without destroying their sinh moment". This identifies the battleground (the decay rate ½ ↔ the strip edge; the sinh moment ↔ the pole) but supplies no inequality. An earlier "end-mass/Cauchy–Schwarz" heuristic for a bounded-below pole is refuted by these spread tests. |
| 339 | **Pole-depth rigidity conjecture** (paper; [OPEN], partially analysed). Define L_c(g) = arch(g) + 2∫g(x)cosh(cx)dx on the ζ-blind PD cone (L_½ = PBAP), with the pole moved to depth c. *Conjecture*: L_c ≥ 0 on the blind cone ⟺ c = ½ ∧ RH, i.e. the blind cone determines the pole depth, in the spirit of "the archimedean place determines Λ". *Evidence*: for c > ½ the parity split (row 338) makes odd tests pay −2B_c², B_c = ∫f sinh(cy), which outgrows the e^{y/2}-balanced blind energy. This is the temperedness wall in PBAP form: ζ(s−a)ζ(s+a) has poles at depth ½+a. *Null-face attempt*: corpus Ξ-divisible blind tests have ĝ = Ξ²R with R(z) = Σ_jw_jQ_j(z)Q_j(−z), Q_j = Π_{k<j}(k+¼+iz/2), and W_ζ = 0, so L_c(g) = 2[ξ(½−c)²R(ic) − ξ(0)²w₀]. Since Q_j(i/2) = 0 for j ≥ 1, R(i/2) = w₀ and L_½ = 0. For c > ½: ξ(½−c) > ξ(0) (ξ is minimal at ½ on ℝ), while R(ic) − w₀ = Σ_{j≥1}w_j(1/16 − c²/4)Π_{1≤k<j}((k+¼)² − c²/4) < 0 for c < 5/2. The sign of dL_c/dc at c = ½ is 2ξ(0)\|ξ′(0)\|w₀ − (ξ(0)²/4)Σ_{j≥1}w_jP_j, which is decided by the arc weights w_j = 4√2·4^jm_j/(2j)!, fixed by the twisted-arc measure and not free. *Status*: not decided. Needs the arc moments m_j of an annihilating measure, i.e. the existential part of the minimum principle, so it cannot be done on paper without the measure. |
| 340 | **Pole-depth rigidity: the repair-cost computation** (paper; reduces to the abscissa dictionary, REDISCOVERED in spirit; corpus `error-exponent-abscissa-excess-law`, JasDressedPSD levels). Odd test f ≈ sgn(y)e^{−α\|y\|} on [−b,b] with ½ < α < c: B_c = ∫f sinh(cy) ≍ e^{(c−α)b}, while B_½ and Σ_γ\|F(γ)\|² stay bounded. So L_c = W_ζ + 2B_½² − 2B_c² → −∞, provided blindness costs less than the gain. The node values to repair are g(log n) ≍ n^{−α}. With pair tests h = φ⋆φ̃, φ = ψ − τ_{log n}ψ (negative at the node), the naive repair cost is Σ_{prime powers ≤ e^{2b}}n^{−α} ≍ e^{2(1−α)b}/b. (i) *Unconditional (PNT level)*: the gain e^{2(c−α)b} beats the cost iff c > 1, so PBAP_c fails for every c > 1. (ii) *Under RH*: the repair signs follow the prime-error oscillation and cancel to ≍ e^{2(½−α)b}·b, so PBAP_c fails for every c > ½ and c* = ½. Hence unconditionally ½ ≤ c* ≤ 1, and c* = ½ ⟺ RH-level cancellation in the repair sum. This is the familiar pattern: unconditional knowledge reaches the edge Re s = 1, RH is the precision at ½, with the pole depth playing the role of the abscissa. No new mechanism, but the dictionary entry is "PBAP pole-depth threshold c* ∈ [½, 1], RH ⟺ c* = ½". Not formalized, since (i) needs the full PBAP test machinery and a quantitative pair-test cost. |
| 340a | **Correction to row 340.** "½ ≤ c* ≤ 1" assumed that PBAP_c holds for c < ½, which is not established. Row 339 suggests null-face witnesses with dominant w₀ give L_c ≈ 2w₀(ξ(½−c)² − ξ(0)²) < 0 for 0 < c < ½. The correct statements are: (i) unconditionally, L_c takes negative values on the blind cone for every c > 1 (naive repair); (ii) under RH, for every c > ½; (iii) at c = ½, L_½ ≥ 0 ⟺ RH (corpus). Whether c = ½ is the only depth with L_c ≥ 0 (two-sided rigidity) remains [OPEN]. |
| 341 | **Interlacing families (MSS) and Heilmann–Lieb for Ξ** (paper; killed; new to the corpus, which has no MSS module). RH ⟺ A(w) = Σa_kw^k (Ξ(z) = A(−z²), a_k = ∫Φu^{2k}/(2k)!) has only negative real zeros, the form of a monomer–dimer (matching) partition function. (a) *Average representations*: Ξ = ∫Φ(u)cos(zu)du averages the real-rooted family {cos(zu)}. Its roots r_k(u) = (k+½)π/u interlace for u₁ < u₂ only while u₂/u₁ < (k+3/2)/(k+½) → 1, so transcendental members with different root densities never share a common interlacer. The Jensen polynomials J^{d,n}(X) = ∫u^{2n}(1+u²X)^d dμ(u) average polynomials each with a d-fold root at −1/u²; a common interlacer must have all roots at that point, so it exists only for a point mass. MSS's "expected polynomial of an interlacing family" mechanism has no input. (b) *Matching realization*: limits of normalized matching polynomials are real-rooted (Heilmann–Lieb + Hurwitz), but the realization of A is the weighted matching polynomial of disjoint edges with weights 1/γ_ρ², i.e. the Hadamard product. That is circular, and an arithmetic graph with k-matching counts ∝ a_k would need a_k to be a Pólya frequency sequence, which is RH (Jensen/LA route; row 288). |
| 342 | **Flows versus graphs: Anosov/Ruelle realizations of ζ** (paper; BRIDGE; corpus has Ihara/Ramanujan, e.g. `CavityHierarchyIhara`, and a large Deninger flow programme with 1539 modules, but no Ruelle/Dolgopyat module). Unlike quantum graphs (CANNOT-546), flows have the right orbit combinatorics automatically: closed orbits are iterates of primitive ones, so a flow with primitive periods {log p} has a Ruelle zeta Π_γ(1−e^{−sl(γ)})^{−1} = ζ(s) with prime-power support built in. The prime orbit theorem (h_top = 1, Margulis ~ e^T/T) and Parry–Pollicott (pole at s = h, nothing else on Re s = h for weak mixing) match PNT and ζ(1+it) ≠ 0. *Bridge*: if the flow were contact Anosov (or had Dolgopyat's non-integrability), the Ruelle zeta would have a pole- and zero-free strip h−ε < Re s ≤ h, i.e. a zero-free strip for ζ. That is an unknown quasi-RH statement, so such a realization is a producer of strips, not of RH. *Limits*: (i) Pollicott–Ruelle resonances come from a non-self-adjoint transfer operator, so nothing confines them to Re s = ½ (wall 12); (ii) the Ruelle zeta carries no Γ-factor or functional equation, so FE would be an extra miracle (geodesic flows on hyperbolic surfaces have FE but lengths 2 log ε_d, not log p); (iii) the order of ζ_R at s = 0 for contact 3-flows is topological (b₁-type), while ζ(0) = −½ forces order 0, which constrains but does not exclude. Lesson: the orbit-interference no-go of CANNOT-546 does not apply to flows; the flow route's gate is self-adjointness of the resonance problem, i.e. the Deninger Hodge gate. |
| 343 | **Cross-check of rows 338–340a against the corpus (memory `pole-weight-rigidity-srp`)** (audit). The parity split of the pole (row 338(b): even 2A², odd −2B²) is REDISCOVERED: "parity decoupling: pole = 2L_e² − 2L_o²; each sector alone is RH-equivalent". The pole-depth deformation L_c (rows 339–340a) is a variant of the owned pole-*weight* deformation W^β (β < 1 empty, β = 1 {Λ}, β > 1 open, C190). The null-kernel formula pole = H(i/2)/2 = w₀/2 and the fact that "finite level is free" (pole and finitely many node values of null PD kernels are onto ℝ^N) settle row 339's w₀-dominance question at every finite level. Rigidity is only compactness at infinity, so the two-sided pole-depth question reduces to the C190/C193-type problem at infinity. The Redheffer, THH, MSS and quantum-graph rows (329–337, 341) remain new to the corpus; de Branges and Helson are owned (`SuzukiDeBrangesVector`, `HelsonDivisorResponse`). Next: attack the open corner case C193 directly, since it decides route SRP. |
| 344 | **Weyl-law mismatch for finite-dimensional flow realizations; the universality of the abscissa law** (paper; two classifications). (a) *Flows*: for contact Anosov flows on compact (2d+1)-manifolds, Pollicott–Ruelle resonances in the first band have density ≍ Vol(M)\|ω\|^d at height ω (Faure–Tsujii band Weyl law, an external theorem). ζ's zeros have density (1/2π)log(\|γ\|/2π), which no integer d ≥ 1 matches, and d = 0 is a circle. A smooth compact contact Anosov realization (which would have given the zero-free strip of row 342) is therefore excluded on counting grounds. Any flow realization must live on an infinite-dimensional or solenoidal phase space, consistent with the corpus's forced infinite-rank H¹ (`f1-object-forced-shape`). The same mismatch kills surfaces (Weyl ≍ T²) and quantum graphs (≍ T; CANNOT-546 independently). (b) *Abscissa universality*: every RH-detecting quantity met in rows 319–344 (sinh moment of blind tests, Weil ground energy μ(a), pole-depth threshold, shell power sums, Riesz means) grows with exponent sup Re ρ − ½ in the aperture or log-scale. The trivial bound is exponent ½ (from β < 1), and any improvement to ½ − ε is a zero-free strip Re s > 1 − ε, which is unknown. So no elementary blindness, positivity or combinatorial argument can improve a trivial exponent without simultaneously proving quasi-RH. The real intermediate gate is a power-saving PNT, which is blocked by wall 6: dlVP-type positivity exists only where the Euler product converges absolutely. |
| 345 | **Can a local, height-uniform mechanism give a zero-free strip?** (paper; killed, folklore; corpus `Sieve.ZetaUniformStripZeroFree` is the classical shrinking region pinned to a contour parameter). Landau's lemma: if \|ζ\| ≤ e^{φ(t)} on σ ≥ 1−θ near height t, a dlVP/Hadamard positivity gives no zeros for σ ≥ 1 − cθ/φ(t). Littlewood's Ω-results (\|ζ(1+it)\| and \|1/ζ(1+it)\| ≥ c log log t infinitely often) force φ(t) → ∞, so every local argument has a width shrinking with t: dlVP 1/log t, VK (log t)^{−2/3}(log log t)^{−1/3}. Density methods bound N(σ,T) by T^{A(1−σ)}log^B T, which is never < 1, so they give almost-all heights only (wall 7). A fixed-width strip therefore needs the same ingredient as RH: an exact global structure that excludes individual zeros at every height at once. The intermediate milestone named in row 344 is thus not easier in kind, only in precision. Consequence for the loop: producers must be global and exact (spectral/Hodge); local positivity (wall 6) and statistics (wall 7) are exhausted even for quasi-RH. |
| 346 | **Langlands' reality of Eisenstein poles in the positive chamber as an RH mechanism** (paper; killed → the universal "+1 shift"; extends row 322). *Mechanism*: for any finite-volume Γ\H (any weight or multiplier) and any rank-one Eisenstein series, poles of the scattering coefficient in Re s > ½ give L² residues, i.e. eigenvalues s(1−s) of a self-adjoint Laplacian, so they are real. If some constant term had a denominator ξ(2s − c) with c ≥ ½, every zero with β > 1 − c would become a non-real pole in Re s > ½, which is impossible. For c = ½ that gives RH. *Kill*: by the Gindikin–Karpelevich formula every constant-term ratio is Π_αξ(⟨λ,α^∨⟩)/ξ(⟨λ,α^∨⟩ + 1) in unitary normalization, also for higher rank and, after scaling, for Kazhdan–Patterson n-fold covers (ζ(n⟨λ,α⟩)/ζ(n⟨λ,α⟩+1)). Denominator zeros sit at ⟨λ,α^∨⟩ = ρ − 1 with Re < 0, i.e. always in the negative (non-physical) chamber, where the residues are not L² and reality fails. This +1 shift is exactly why Langlands–Shahidi reaches only L(1+it) ≠ 0. A shift c ∈ (0,1) would require a non-standard intertwining normalization incompatible with the Plancherel measure. No survivor; the automorphic reality mechanism is confined to the edge of the strip. |
| 347 | **Classical-criteria sweep: Riesz, Volchkov, Salem, Amoroso** (corpus audit + paper). Riesz is owned (`RieszFunctionExponentLaw.riemannHypothesis_iff_rieszPowerSeries_le`). Volchkov is a Littlewood-lemma identity owned as BSY (`BalazardSaiasYorCriterionEquivalence`). Salem (no bounded solution of ∫e^{−σy}φ(y)dy/(e^{e^{x−y}}+1) = 0 for ½ < σ < 1) is a Wiener-type statement about zeros of the Mellin transform Γ(s)η(s) of the Fermi–Dirac kernel, i.e. the owned Fermi Nyman–Beurling lane. *Amoroso* is not in the corpus. Since Π_{d\|n}Φ_d = x^n − 1, Möbius inversion gives F_N := Π_{n≤N}Φ_n(x) = Π_{d≤N}(x^d−1)^{M(⌊N/d⌋)}. Hence log\|F_N(e^{iθ})\| = −Σ_m(cos mθ/m)Σ_{d\|m, d≤N}dM(N/d). The orders of vanishing at roots of unity reproduce Σ_{d≤N}M(N/d) = 1, and the Mahler measure is 1. The log-height of F_N grows with the Mertens exponent: trivially O(N log N), and O(N^{½+ε}) ⟺ RH. So it is the Mertens function in polynomial form (walls 1/10), another instance of the abscissa universality of row 344(b); integrality adds no constraint beyond the classical identity. The identity is not formalized (Möbius inversion, no content). |
| 348 | **Pick-matrix criterion transported into the region of absolute convergence** (paper; REDISCOVERED/BRIDGE; corpus has Nevanlinna–Pick and Carathéodory interpolation, `HTGeometry.SchurNevanlinnaPick`, `CaratheodoryNevanlinnaPick`, and Herglotz ⟺ Weil-positive, MUST-1400). *Statement*: with F = ξ′/ξ, RH ⟺ for every finite set {s_i} ⊂ {Re s > 1} (or any open U ⊂ {Re s > ½}) the Pick matrix [(F(s_i) + \overline{F(s_j)})/(s_i + s̄_j − 1)] is PSD. (⇐) Pick interpolation on a set with an accumulation point gives a Herglotz G on Re s > ½ with G = F on U, so ξ′/ξ continues holomorphically and ξ has no zeros in Re s > ½. Every entry is computable from absolutely convergent data (ζ′/ζ = −ΣΛ(n)n^{−s}, ψ, poles). This is not a wall-6 escape: writing 1/(s_i+s̄_j−1) = ∫_0^∞e^{−(s_i+s̄_j−1)x}dx, the Pick form is exactly the Weil form on one-sided tests f(x) = e^{x/2}Σc_ie^{−s_ix}1_{x>0}. These span L²(0,∞), so by translation invariance the criterion is Weil's. Absolute convergence was never the obstacle; positivity of a non-termwise kernel is. With the half-plane Re s > 1 kernel 1/(s_i+s̄_j−2), PSD is unconditional (Re ξ′/ξ = Σ Re 1/(s−ρ) > 0 there), and moving the kernel's vertex from 1 to ½ is exactly RH. |
| 349 | **Probabilistic (Biane–Pitman–Yor) representation** (corpus check; owned). ξ is the Mellin transform of a positive random variable built from the theta function (Brownian-excursion/Kolmogorov type, Laplace transform Π_n(1+λ/(cn²))^{−1}), so RH ⟺ the characteristic function of the tilted log-variable has only real zeros. Newman/Lee–Yang classes need an exp(−V) structure that the theta tilt lacks, and the corpus already shows the relevant tilted measures are not infinitely divisible (`ThetaTiltXiInfiniteDivisibilityNoGo`, `ThetaCuspLikelihoodProbabilityNondivisibility`). Infinite divisibility of the untilted variable gives Lévy–Khintchine positivity for its Laplace transform, not for Mellin zeros (the same mismatch as rows 322 and 330). No survivor. |
| 350 | **Signature characters and topological intersection arguments** (paper; two kills). (a) *Unitarity via signature bookkeeping (Adams–van Leeuwen–Trapa–Vogan style)*: view the explicit formula as the character of the scaling action on "H¹", and Weil positivity as unitarity of an invariant Hermitian form. Deform a parameter from a unitary end and track signature changes only at degeneracy (reducibility) points, using Jantzen-type vanishing orders. For the Weil form compressed to PW_b the degeneracies are the zeros of the restricted Weil (Fredholm) determinant D(b), i.e. crossings of the ground energy μ(b).
