Since positivity at small b is known, RH ⟺ D(b) ≠ 0 for all b. This is routes RW/WL (corpus `CCMRestrictedWeilFriedrichsOperator`, relative-determinant lanes), so signature bookkeeping adds no input: the crossing set is the unknown. (b) *Topology*: A = arch + pole has a negative eigenspace E₋ of dimension ≈ 4b on PW_b (Ω < 0 for \|t\| ≲ 2π), cut by ≍ e^{2b}/2b blind quadrics ⟨f,S_nf⟩ = 0 (S_n = cos(D log n)). PBAP means the blind quadric misses the open negative cone of A. A topological intersection theorem (Borsuk–Ulam or common zeros of traceless quadrics) could only force an intersection when dim E₋ exceeds the number of constraints, so it can disprove but never prove PBAP; and the map f ↦ (⟨f,S_nf⟩) is even, so Borsuk–Ulam does not apply. A sharp inequality on mixed E₋ ⊕ E₊ vectors has no topological certificate. |
| 351 | **Existence by slack and compactness: ε-boosted Weil cones** (paper; BRIDGE, circular; related to MUST-1448 "λ ≡ 1 boosted", row 303, and the SRP pole-weight memory). Let C_ε be the set of weights w with arch + pole + εg(0) − Σ_nw_nn^{−½}·2g(log n) ≥ 0 on the PD cone. Uniqueness can fail at ε > 0 because of the slack, so C_ε could be nonempty unconditionally. If C_ε ≠ ∅ for every ε with uniformly growth-bounded members, a compactness limit lies in C₀ ⊆ {Λ}, and RH follows. *Kill*: (i) w = 0 ∉ C_ε, since the two-bump pole ≍ −2e^Lg(0) forces mean-one weights at every scale (row 324). (ii) w ≡ 1 needs a growing boost c(s+½)^θ, not a constant (MUST-1448), so it is not in C_ε for small ε. (iii) If an off-line zero ρ = ½+δ+iγ exists, W_Λ has directions ≍ −e^{δb}g(0), and a compensating deviation Δ = w − Λ must reproduce that zero's explicit-formula trace, Δ(n) ≍ n^δcos(γ log n)·(√n-normalized). This is not growth-bounded relative to Λ ≍ log n. Hence "C_ε contains a growth-bounded weight for every ε" ⟺ RH: slack buys nothing because the obstruction to existence grows exponentially (abscissa law, row 344(b)), while ε is a constant. A positive survivor would need a quantitative uniqueness C_ε ⊆ B(Λ, δ(ε)) with δ(ε) → 0, which by (iii) is again a zero-free statement. |
| 352 | **Characters of U(∞) (Edrei–Thoma–Voiculescu) as a carrier** (paper; killed → LA, circular). Write Ξ(z) = A(−z²) with A(w) = Σa_kw^k, a_k = ∫Φu^{2k}du/(2k)! > 0. A has order ½ (genus 0), so by Aissen–Schoenberg–Whitney–Edrei, RH ⟺ (a_k) is a Pólya frequency (totally positive Toeplitz) sequence ⟺ a_k/a₀ is the generating sequence of an extremal character of U(∞) (Voiculescu–Edrei parametrization) with only β-parameters, β_i = 1/γ_i², and γ = α = 0. A proof would exhibit a unitary (or spherical (U(∞)×U(∞), U(∞))) representation whose character has these coefficients, making total positivity automatic. *Kill*: the character's parameters are the zeros themselves, so any construction must already know them (circular). Total positivity of (a_k) is the Jensen/Laguerre–Pólya condition (LA route: "kernel moments are not a multiplier sequence", Jensen hyperbolicity ⟺ RH; GORZ gives each fixed degree only for large shifts). There is no natural U(∞)-object attached to Φ: theta lifts give representations of SL₂/metaplectic groups, not of inductive-limit unitary groups. No survivor. |
| 353 | **Lévy–Khintchine form of the Weil functional as a Bochner-to-pointwise transfer (wall 13)** (paper; exact identity, no producer). Using Re ψ(a+iy) = ψ(a) + Σ_ny²/((n+a)((n+a)²+y²)), the archimedean term is exactly arch(g) = Ω(0)g(0) + ∫(g(0) − g(x))dν_∞(x) with Ω(0) = ψ(¼) − log π < −5 and ν_∞ = Σ_m(m+¼)^{−1}k_m(x)dx, k_m(x) = (2m+½)e^{−(2m+½)\|x\|}: a positive Lévy measure, ≍ dx/\|x\| at 0 and ≍ e^{−\|x\|/2}dx at ∞. For PD g, g(0) − g(x) ≥ 0 pointwise, the one Bochner-to-pointwise transfer that is always available, so arch ≥ Ω(0)g(0). In the same form the prime part has positive atoms 2Λ(n)n^{−½}δ_{±log n} and the pole has negative density −2cosh(x/2)dx. The combined Lévy measure is μ = ν_∞ + 2e^{−\|x\|/2}d(ψ(e^{\|x\|}) − e^{\|x\|}) (the PNT error measure). The g(0)-coefficient Ω(0) + 4·2sinh(a/2) − 2Σ_{n≤e^a}Λ(n)n^{−½} is Ω(0) plus O((ψ − id)(e^a)e^{−a/2}). So W ≥ 0 on PD tests of aperture a ⟸ μ ≥ 0 on [−a,a] and the coefficient ≥ 0, which needs a one-sided, √x-precise PNT error (wall 1). On blind g the atoms drop out and the obstruction is the negative pole density, i.e. the odd sector of row 338. The Lévy form makes wall 13 concrete: the only pointwise positivity (g(0) − g ≥ 0) controls arch, never the pole. |
| 354 | **Fixed-point and electrostatic characterizations of the zero set** (paper; killed). (a) *Real-preserving contraction*: if a map T on symmetric configurations had Z_ζ as its unique fixed point, sent critical-line configurations to critical-line configurations and contracted, RH would follow by closedness. Natural candidates fail. Newton's map for Ξ is real-preserving, but every zero, real or not, is a separate attracting fixed point, so there is no uniqueness. Explicit-formula self-consistency (zeros of a function built from Z and the primes) is the identity on Z_ζ, because the primes alone determine ξ. The heat flow is expanding backwards (Rodgers–Tao, de Bruijn–Newman). (b) *Stieltjes electrostatics*: any entire F satisfies Σ_{j≠k}2/(ρ_k−ρ_j) = F″/F′(ρ_k) at its zeros. A confining explicit field V with Z_ζ as the unique log-gas minimizer would force reality, but minimizers of log-gases are crystalline (Sandier–Serfaty, Abrikosov-type rigidity), while ζ-zeros have GUE fluctuations and are not crystalline (corpus `anti-crystal-gapless-realization`). The field would have to encode every fluctuation, i.e. be as complicated as Ξ″/Ξ′. No survivor. |
| 355 | **A Hasse-type local–global principle for Weil positivity** (paper; killed). W = W_∞ + pole − Σ_pW_p is a sum of local terms, so one could ask for a Hasse principle: global positivity from local conditions. *Kill*: (i) no local term is positive. W_∞ has density Ω with Ω(0) < −5, and W_p = −log p·Σ_kp^{−k/2}·2g(k log p) is an indefinite periodic multiplier with mean zero (row 319). Positivity is not a local property at any place. (ii) Hasse–Minkowski concerns isotropy (existence of zeros) of finite-rank forms over ℚ. Definiteness is detected at the real place alone, and the Weil form is an infinite-rank form over ℝ with no ℚ-structure, so the principle has no content. (iii) The interpolating criteria (`riemannHypothesis_iff_repairedBlindPositive_vonMangoldt`, every node set S) already express the exact local–global trade: any subset of local terms can be traded against blindness at those nodes, and every stage is RH-equivalent. The local–global structure is fully owned and carries no slack. |
| 356 | **Two more crisp Hilbert–Pólya obstructions** (paper; companions to CANNOT-546). (a) *Hermitian transfer models*: if σ is Hermitian (a discrete Schrödinger or Hückel transfer matrix) and det(1−tσ) = Π_edet(1−tσ_e) for all t, the spectrum of σ is the union of the block spectra. So tr σ² = Σ_etr σ_e², i.e. ‖σ‖_F² = Σ_e‖σ_e‖_F², and every cross-block entry vanishes. The multi-variable secular factorization gives the same from the z_ez_f coefficient −Σ_{i∈e,j∈f}\|σ_ij\|². For entrywise-nonnegative (Perron–Frobenius, subshift-of-finite-type) σ the orbit expansion has no signs, so prime-power support forbids every cross-block cycle (block-triangular). So prime-power orbit support decouples unitary, Hermitian and nonnegative transfer models alike. Only non-normal signed couplings can cancel composite orbits, and those carry no self-adjointness. (b) *Dilation commutation*: an operator on L²(ℝ₊^*, d*x) commuting with the dilations D₂ and D₃ commutes with the dense group they generate, hence with all dilations, so it is a Mellin multiplier h(D). Its spectrum is the essential range of h: continuous, or eigenvalues of infinite multiplicity. A Hilbert–Pólya operator must break every pair of prime dilations, which is why Connes needs the cokernel/quotient (owned in spirit). Neither part is formalized: (a)'s Hermitian case needs second charpoly coefficients, and (b) is folklore. |
| 357 | **Why uniqueness was provable and existence is not: null-face decomposition certificates** (paper; killed, with structural lesson). Uniqueness (C ⊆ {Λ}) works because null-face tests ĝ = Ξ²H vanish at every zero wherever it lies, so W_Λ = 0 on them unconditionally and the zero locations never enter. *Conjecture*: every PD test decomposes as g = n + p with n in the closed null face and p in a cone P whose positivity is known without zeros (window tests of support ≤ log 2, nonnegative blind tests, or positive combinations). Then W(g) = W(p) ≥ 0 and RH follows. *Kill*: (i) the decomposition requires p̂(γ_ρ) = ĝ(γ_ρ) at every zero, i.e. interpolation of arbitrary data on the zero set by functions of type ≤ log 2. The zeros have density (1/2π)log T, which exceeds every finite Nyquist density, so they are a sampling set and such interpolation is overdetermined (the same counting as rows 320 and 344). (ii) Ξ has infinite exponential type, so compactly supported null tests exist only as truncations, and the decomposition is approximate with errors that are exactly the zero contributions. (iii) Without a zero-free class P the certificate is circular. Lesson: uniqueness uses tests that annihilate all zeros, existence needs tests that see all zeros with sign, and no zero-independent cone spans the latter. That is wall 12 seen from the null face. |
| 358 | **Finite-aperture cones and compactness** (paper; owned: `WeilFullConeCompactness.abs_le_weilConeBound`, pair tests `WeilFullConeOneSidedCriterion.pairTest_weight_pos`). Let C_a be the weights on nodes n ≤ e^a with arch + pole − Σw_nN_n ≥ 0 on PD tests of support ≤ a. A pair test supported near {0, ±log j} with width ε < gap(log j) involves only the node j. It bounds \|w_j\| ≤ C_j uniformly in a ≥ log j + ε, so nested nonempty C_a have a pointwise-convergent subsequence whose limit lies in C ⊆ {Λ}. Hence RH ⟺ C_a ≠ ∅ for every a ⟺ PBAP at every finite aperture. "Finite level is free" (pole-weight memory) refers to null kernels, not cones, so C_a ≠ ∅ is a genuine finite-aperture positivity question (bounded-aperture numerics, row 295) whose uniform answer is RH. No slack enters: each C_a is a finite-node LP over an infinite-dimensional test space, and its feasibility for all a is the whole problem. |
| 359 | **The boundary of CANNOT-546: ℚ-dependent lengths and the all-to-all star** (paper; classification). With edge lengths ½log n for all n ≥ 2 (ℚ-dependent), distinct words can share a length and composite orbits can cancel. The rank-one star coupling σ = −𝟙𝟙^T (amplitude −1 for every transition) has det(1 − σD(z)) = 1 + Σ_nz_n = ζ(s) at z_n = n^{−s}, and log ζ = Σ_{p,k}p^{−ks}/k: the Euler product *is* the cancellation of all composite orbits in this star. But σ is unbounded (‖σ‖ = ∞ on ℓ² of the edges). The normalized rank-one projection −(1/N)𝟙𝟙^T secularizes to 1 + N^{−1}Σ_{n≤N}n^{ik} → 1, and the unitary (Householder) version σ = −(I − 2P_v) gives the self-adjoint secular equation Σ_nw_n tan(kL_n/2) = 0, whose orbit sum runs over all words, not prime powers, and is not ζ. So the dichotomy is sharp: with ℚ-independent lengths, unitarity forces decoupling (CANNOT-546); with ℚ-dependent lengths, cancellation needs the unbounded rank-one coupling, which has no unitary dilation. "ζ = det(1 + rank-one)" is only the identity ζ = 1 + (ζ−1) on Re s > 1. No survivor. |
| 360 | **Carleman-regularized Euler product** (paper; owned: `SquarefreeCarlemanDeterminant`, prime-diagonal Hilbert–Schmidt modules). D_P(s) = diag(p^{−s}) on ℓ²(primes) is Hilbert–Schmidt for σ > ½. Hence det₂(1 − D_P(s)) = Π_p(1−p^{−s})e^{p^{−s}} is holomorphic and zero-free on σ > ½, and 1/ζ(s) = det₂(1−D_P(s))·exp(−P(s)) with P the prime zeta function. Every zero of ζ in the strip is a logarithmic singularity of P(s) = Σ_kμ(k)k^{−1}log ζ(ks). So RH ⟺ P continues analytically to σ > ½ (Landau–Walfisz singularity structure). The regularization moves all zero information into the trace term P, i.e. into prime-sum precision (wall 1), and a Fredholm determinant on σ > ½ carries no positivity. No survivor. |
| 361 | **Habiro ring / Λ-ring F₁-structures with integer Frobenii** (paper; new area for the corpus, which has no Habiro or q-de Rham module; killed by the owned `FrobeniusLatticeRigidity`, `DeningerCarrierNoIntegralLattice`). The Habiro ring Ĥ = lim ℤ[q]/((q;q)_n) is a Λ-ring with Frobenius lifts ψ_n: q ↦ q^n acting on its points μ_∞ ≅ ℚ/ℤ by multiplication by n. That is Borger's F₁-structure; ψ_n has n − 1 fixed points, which is G_m over F₁ (Kurokawa zeta (s−1)/s, no zeros). Carrying ζ's zeros needs a joint eigencharacter n ↦ n^ρ of the commuting monoid {ψ_n}. (i) In ℤ[[q]] the eigen-equation a_{j/n}[n\|j] = λ_na_j for all n forces a_j = 0 for every j ≥ 1 (take n = j+1), so only constants are joint eigenvectors. (ii) On a finitely generated integral lattice the corpus shows no three Frobenii have a joint eigenfunctional with eigenvalues p^ρ, q^ρ, r^ρ, and unimodular lattice Frobenii are torsion (Kronecker). (iii) Functions on ℚ/ℤ with f(nx) = λ_nf(x) lead to the Bost–Connes system (row 326b). So any F₁-cohomology carrying the zeros must be infinite-rank and non-integral (the corpus forced shape: a cokernel of Σ[n] in the Dirichlet ring), and the Habiro ring's integrality is the wrong kind. A real-parameter (scaling-site) Frobenius is needed, as in Connes–Consani. No survivor. |
| 362 | **Hermitian companion to CANNOT-546 formalized** (Lean, extends row 356(a); module `RHTower/QuantumGraphPrimePowerDecoupling`, built, wired, standard axioms). `trace_mul_self_eq_sum_of_det_one_sub_smul_eq_prod` (any σ): det(1−tσ) = Π_edet(1−tσ_e) ∀t ⟹ tr σ² = Σ_etr σ_e². Proof: multiply the identity at t and −t to get det(1+uσ²) = Π_edet(1+uσ_e²) for all u (every u is −t², ℂ algebraically closed); then the polynomial identity (Polynomial.funext) and first coefficients (`coeff_det_one_add_X_smul_one`, first coefficient of a product of constant-term-1 polynomials). `eq_zero_of_isHermitian_det_one_sub_smul_eq_prod`: for Hermitian σ both traces are squared Frobenius norms, so the cross-block mass Σ_{blk a ≠ blk b}\|σ_ab\|² = 0. Together with the unitary theorem, prime-power orbit support decouples every unitary or self-adjoint transfer model with ℚ-independent lengths. STATUS CANNOT-546 updated. |
| 363 | **Weil positivity as Néron–Tate positivity** (paper; reduces to rows 292 and 308–310; no corpus height-machine module). Faltings–Hriljac's arithmetic Hodge index for arithmetic surfaces is proved through the canonical height ĥ(P) = lim h([N]P)/N². It is ≥ 0 because naive (counting) heights are ≥ 0, and quadratic because [N]^*L ≅ L^{N²} up to O(1) (theorem of the cube / height machine). That is a positivity mechanism of its own kind: a limit of non-quadratic nonnegative counts made quadratic by a geometric endomorphism. *Transfer attempt*: points = tests f, W = lim h(T_Nf)/c_N for a natural nonnegative h and an endomorphism T_N with W∘T_N = c_N·W. (i) T_N = scalar N works for any quadratic form, but then h − W must be o(N²) on Nf, i.e. h ≈ W: circular. (ii) Translations (c = 1) and dilations (not homogeneous: arch grows like log k, row 294) are the only geometric symmetries of W. (iii) A counting h as a norm of translate combinations, ‖Σa_nf(·−log n)‖², produces ratio nodes log(m/n); matching W forces a_n ∼ μ(n)n^{−½}, and compression destroys the product (row 292). (iv) A counting h⁰ on Arakelov divisors with quadratic growth is row 309a: degree multiplication on sections is missing, i.e. the characteristic-one Frobenius. So the Néron–Tate route needs the same missing object, a degree-multiplying endomorphism of an arithmetic "Jacobian of Spec ℤ" with a theorem of the cube. |
| 364 | **Five bold carriers, each fatal on inspection** (paper; batch kill). (a) *ζ as a limit of Selberg zetas of X₀(N)*: their positive (discrete) parts satisfy RH up to finitely many exceptions (Kim–Sarnak), but their zeros also include scattering resonances at s = ρ/2 (from φ_N ∋ ξ(2s−1)/ξ(2s)), which sit off their critical line. The normalized discrete spectra tend to the Weyl density, not to ζ-zeros. (b) *Hecke eigenvalue limits*: T_p-eigenvalues equidistribute to the p-adic Plancherel measure (Serre), which carries no ζ-zeros. (c) *CCM ground states F_a → Ξ*: F_a has real zeros unconditionally (owned), so convergence would give RH by Hurwitz. But under ¬RH, μ(a) → −∞ and F_a concentrates on the off-line direction, so convergence is RH-equivalent (route RW). (d) *W as the Hessian of a convex free energy / a covariance*: every Hessian of a log-partition function is a covariance (PSD), but W is a covariance only if a field with covariance W exists, which is W ≥ 0 itself. Bohr–Bagchi random models give the covariance of the prime part, not of W. (e) *Osterwalder–Schrader reflection positivity*: in one variable it means complete monotonicity of the half-line restriction (Bernstein), while W(x) = Σ_γcos(γx) oscillates and its Wick rotation Σ cosh(γy) diverges. No survivor; each lands on wall 12 (spectral realization) or wall 7. |
| 365 | **Faltings–Hriljac decomposition applied to the Weil form** (paper; diagnostic). In the arithmetic Hodge index for arithmetic surfaces, positivity comes from two sources. (1) *Archimedean*: the Arakelov Green kernel is conditionally negative definite, so degree-0 measures have positive energy. (2) *Finite places*: local intersection multiplicities of distinct effective divisors are ≥ 0 (counting). The Weil form has (1) exactly: by row 353, Ω(t) − Ω(0) = ∫(1−cos tx)dν_∞ is a negative-definite (Lévy–Khintchine) function, and the archimedean term is Ω(0)g(0) + (conditionally positive Lévy energy). But two things are missing. The "degree" normalization leaves the negative self-energy Ω(0)g(0) = (ψ(¼) − log π)g(0), and PD tests have g(0) = ‖f‖² > 0, so there is no degree-0 subspace. On the pole-blind subspace (ĝ(±i/2) = 0, the analogue of D·H = D·V = 0) the constant remains. And (2) fails: the finite-place terms −Λ(n)n^{−½}·2g(log n) carry no effectivity sign, because g is not a divisor class with positive local multiplicities. In surface terms, PBAP asks the Lévy energy of a doubly-blind (pole- and prime-blind) test to beat 5.37·g(0), i.e. a missing "effectivity of Frobenius intersections" (rows 307–310). The decomposition names both missing ingredients: a degree-0 normalization that removes Ω(0), and an effective-cycle interpretation of the prime terms. |
| 366 | **Counting route: Riemann–Siegel domination and Gram/Rosser blocks** (paper; killed → wall 7; corpus has the sign-change receivers `HardyZSignChange`, `HardySignFlipOddOrder`). RH ⟺ N₀(T) = N(T), and N₀ is bounded below by sign changes of Z. The n = 1 term cos θ(t) of the Riemann–Siegel sum has exactly one sign change per Gram interval, so Gram's law holds wherever \|Σ_{2≤n≤√(t/2π)}n^{−½}cos(θ−t log n)\| < 1 at the Gram points. That sum is unbounded (typical size ≍ √(log log t), large deviations unbounded), so Gram's law fails on a positive proportion of intervals. Rosser blocks repair most failures, but Rosser's rule itself fails (Lehman), so no bounded-block deterministic pattern exists. A multiscale count could give N₀(T) ≥ N(T) − O(1), i.e. finitely many off-line zeros (FOL), only with a uniform block bound. Even then FOL needs the asymptotic abscissa law to reach RH (ZAL ∧ AAL split). Each step is a deterministic individuation at every height (wall 7). |
| 366a | **Correction to CANNOT-548 (row 344(a))**. "Any realization must be infinite-dimensional" is too strong. ζ's zero density (1/2π)log(T/2π) = θ′(T)/π is entirely archimedean, and one-degree-of-freedom non-compact hyperbolic models do have T log T Weyl laws: regularized xp (Berry–Keating, BBM), Schrödinger operators with V = e^{\|x\|} (Lagarias/Morse, N ≍ √E log E), and cusp scattering windings. The correct exclusion covers compact models: closed manifolds, finite quantum graphs, compact contact Anosov flows. Realizations must be non-compact, and the primes enter only through S(T). The STATUS entry has been corrected. |
| 367 | **Zeros as a self-adjoint prime perturbation of an archimedean reference (Krein spectral shift)** (paper; killed, with a crisp size law; related to corpus `SourceHorizontalFiniteRankReflectionQuadraticCarrierNoGo` "forces infinite rank"). The density is archimedean (row 366a), so try zeros = spec(H_∞ + V) with H_∞ self-adjoint, explicit, and counting θ(T)/π + O(1) (e.g. eigenvalues at the θ-points cos θ(t) = 0). V self-adjoint would force RH. The spectral shift N_{H_∞+V} − N_{H_∞} is then S(T) + O(1), and Z(t)/E_∞(t) must be the Krein perturbation determinant det(1 + V(H_∞ − z)^{−1}). *Size law*: (i) rank V = r ⟹ \|N_{H+V} − N_H\| ≤ r (min–max), but S(T) is unbounded, so no finite-rank V works for any reference whose counting misses S by O(1). (ii) Rank one ⟺ Z/E_∞ − 1 is a Herglotz partial fraction ⟺ ζ-zeros interlace the reference eigenvalues, which is Gram's law for cos θ and fails (row 366). (iii) V trace class ⟹ ∫\|ξ\| ≤ ‖V‖₁, but ∫_0^∞\|S(T)\|dT = ∞ (mean square ≍ log log T/2π²). So V must be infinite-rank and outside the trace class, beyond Krein–Birman spectral-shift theory, or the reference must already encode S up to O(1), i.e. know the primes' fluctuations. No survivor. The law to keep: "prime perturbations of an archimedean model are never trace class". |
| 368 | **Domain changes (self-adjoint extensions) cannot carry S(T) either** (paper; extends the size law of row 367; corpus owns the deficiency machinery `HTGeometry.VonNeumannDeficiency`, `JacobiDeficiencyRankTwoAmbiguity`, the Berry–Keating modules, `SuzukiTaSelfAdjointExtension`, and row 285). For a symmetric operator A with deficiency (n,n), the self-adjoint extensions form a U(n)-family whose resolvents differ by rank ≤ n. Their counting functions therefore agree up to ±n (interlacing), and for n = 1 the spectra are the zeros of Re(e^{iθ}E) for one Hermite–Biehler E (de Branges/Krein, wall 4; `riemannHypothesis_iff_strict_hermiteBiehler`). If any extension has an explicit, archimedean spectrum with counting θ(T)/π + O(1), every extension has counting θ/π + O(1), while ζ's counting carries the unbounded S(T). So no finite-deficiency domain change of an archimedean operator (BBM's Δ⁻¹AΔ with a boundary condition, Berry–Keating with Sierra–Townsend cut-offs, Morse/Liouville on a half-line) can realize the zeros. The symmetric operator itself must already be arithmetic, with the primes in its intrinsic counting, i.e. infinite deficiency or a domain built from the primes (row 284: "any realization must change the domain"). Sharpened statement: the domain change must have infinite deficiency index. |
| 369 | **Euler factors as dissipative perturbations** (paper; heuristic, not decided, follows from CANNOT-549). By CANNOT-549 the primes must enter as an infinite-rank, non-trace-class perturbation, and the natural decomposition is one piece per prime. Each Euler factor 1 − p^{−½−iz} is entire, (2π/log p)-periodic, with zeros at z = 2πk/log p + i/2 (row 318's spurious lattices). As a Krein perturbation determinant it corresponds to a dissipative, non-self-adjoint V_p = A_p + iB_p with B_p ≥ 0, whose "eigenvalues" lie on Im z = ½ (resonances). Its spectral-shift size is ≍ p^{−½}, and Σ_pp^{−½} = ∞ matches the non-trace-class requirement. No finite partial product vanishes in the strip: the zeros emerge only in the continuation of the infinite product, which is the emergent-at-infinity picture of row 318. A self-adjoint total H_∞ + Σ_pV_p would need Σ_pB_p to be cancelled exactly by an anti-dissipative archimedean/pole counterterm. Positive operators cannot sum to zero, so the cancellation must be a renormalization (an operator-level form of the PNT balance pole ≍ Σ_pΛ(p)p^{−½}, rows 324/353). RH would be the statement that this renormalized sum is self-adjoint. Not testable as stated; it is recorded as the operator form of the Lévy-measure balance of row 353 (ν_∞ + prime atoms − 2cosh dx). |
| 370 | **Lax–Phillips wave decay on SL₂(ℤ)\H as a producer** (paper; killed; new to the corpus, which has the cutoff line law but no Lax–Phillips semigroup). The compressed wave group Z(t) = P_KU(t)\|_K, on the Eisenstein part, has generator B whose eigenvalues are λ = s − ½ at the poles s = ρ/2 of φ(s) = ξ(2s−1)/ξ(2s) in the unphysical half-plane. So Re λ = β/2 − ½, which is −¼ under RH, and the slowest decay rate is Θ/2 − ½. Cusp forms are L² eigenvectors on which Z acts unitarily (no decay), so they must be split off. Sufficient: ‖Z(t)\|_{Eis}‖ ≤ Ce^{−t/4} ⟹ \|e^{λt}\| ≤ Ce^{−t/4} ⟹ RH. RH alone does not give it (non-normality); it needs RH plus resolvent bounds on Re λ > −¼, i.e. 1/ζ growth bounds on Re s > ½. So the decay estimate is an RH-strength producer target. *Kill of the geometric attack*: decay tools for hyperbolic surfaces need small trapping. The Bourgain–Dyatlov fractal uncertainty principle and Naud/Patterson–Sullivan pressure gaps give an essential resonance-free strip only for convex co-compact groups (limit-set dimension δ < 1), with width → 0 as δ → 1. The modular surface has finite volume (δ = 1, full trapping in the compact part), and Schottky approximations Γ′ ⊂ SL₂(ℤ) have resonances unrelated to ζ. The resonances at ρ/2 are arithmetic (constant-term scattering), not dynamical, so no microlocal input reaches them. Abscissa law again: decay exponent = Θ/2 − ½. |
| 371 | **Big-picture synthesis of cycles 337–370** (synthesis; formal output CANNOT-546 [L, unitary + Hermitian], CANNOT-547/548/549 [P]). What the research says the solution must look like: (1) *Density vs fluctuation*: the zero density θ′(T)/π is purely archimedean and comes from a non-compact hyperbolic (xp-type, cusp) structure; the primes contribute only the unbounded, non-integrable fluctuation S(T). (2) *Size law (CANNOT-549)*: the primes must act as an infinite-rank, non-trace-class perturbation or an infinite-deficiency domain of the archimedean structure. (3) *Combinatorics (CANNOT-546, row 342)*: the orbits must be flow-like (iterates of primitive orbits of length log p), never graph-like interference. With ℚ-independent lengths and unitary or Hermitian coupling the orbits decouple, so the zeros must come from the archimedean/pole coupling. (4) *Individual Euler factors are dissipative* (resonances on Im z = ½, the spurious lattices of rows 318/359/369). Self-adjointness can only emerge for the full product, by an exact renormalization (operator-level PNT balance), matching "positivity emerges only when all primes are present". (5) *Automorphic and dynamical reality theorems* stop at the edge: the +1 shift (CANNOT-547), the δ = 1 FUP failure (row 370), Maass–Selberg pairing s ↔ s̄. (6) *Geometric readings* name the missing pieces consistently: a degree-multiplying (characteristic-one) Frobenius with a theorem of the cube (Néron–Tate), a degree-0 normalization removing Ω(0) = ψ(¼) − log π, and an effectivity sign for the Frobenius intersections (Faltings–Hriljac). (7) *Abscissa universality*: every intermediate quantity has exponent Θ − ½, so no partial (strip) result is easier in kind. Every constraint points to Connes' adèle class space with the scaling flow: archimedean hyperbolic place, p-adic periodic orbits C_p, global positivity ⟺ RH. The open point is an intrinsic Hilbert structure on the cokernel, i.e. a proof that the renormalized dissipative prime coupling is self-adjoint. |
| 372 | **Rosati positivity: Weil's abelian-variety proof as a template** (paper; reduces to rows 306–310; consistent with `f1-object-forced-shape`). For curves over F_q there is a second proof of RH besides the surface Hodge index. A polarization (ample theta divisor) on the Jacobian J makes the Rosati involution φ ↦ φ† positive on End(J)⊗ℚ (tr φφ† > 0). Since FF† = q, the Frobenius eigenvalues satisfy \|α\|² = q. Here the infinitely many dissipative Euler factors become one unitary operator on a finite-dimensional H¹ through rationality, and the polarization supplies unitarity. *Transfer*: for Spec ℤ, take the algebra generated by the scaling operators U_λ on the cokernel H¹ with U_λ† = λU_λ^{−1}. For φ = ∫f(λ)U_λd*λ, Tr(φφ†) is the Weil distribution at f⋆f̃, so Rosati positivity is Weil positivity, not new. The function-field proof of Rosati positivity uses ampleness of the theta divisor, i.e. a geometric positivity of a line bundle on J. The analogue needs an ample class on a "Jacobian of Spec ℤ". Theta-type h⁰ presupposes the positivity (row 308), and counting-type h⁰ needs the characteristic-one degree multiplication (rows 309–310). The template therefore isolates the open object precisely: a polarization of the scaling H¹ whose positivity is geometric (ampleness), not spectral. |
| 373 | **Griffiths positivity of quotient bundles for Connes' cokernel** (paper; killed, with a general lesson). Griffiths: if E(z) ⊂ M × H is a holomorphic subbundle of a flat Hilbert bundle, the quotient Q = H/E is positively curved (Θ_Q = BB* ≥ 0, B the second fundamental form). Connes' spectral realization is a cokernel H¹ = H/closure(Im E), so one could hope for curvature positivity = Weil positivity. *Kill*: over the spectral parameter t the Mellin fibres of E are E_t = ζ(½+it)·(·)^∧(t). The cokernel is the *torsion* sheaf supported at the zeros t = γ_ρ (complex if off-line), not a locally free quotient, so Griffiths' formula does not apply. The only positive object attached to a torsion cokernel is the Poincaré–Lelong current i∂∂̄log\|ζ\|² = Σ[ρ], which is positive for every zero configuration. More generally ∂²_σlog\|ξ\| = −Σ_ρ((σ−β)²−(t−γ)²)/\|s−ρ\|⁴, so log\|ξ(σ+it)\| is not convex in σ even under RH (≈ log\|σ−½\| at t = γ). Only the monotonicity Re ξ′/ξ > 0 (LA) is RH-equivalent. *Lesson*: positivity of curvatures and currents never constrains where a divisor sits. Support location needs a reality structure (unitarity of the scaling action on the quotient), which is exactly what weighted L²_δ completions lose and L² completions kill (row 306). |
| 374 | **Finite approximants with an explicit pole correction** (paper; killed → wall 7; ties to the CANNOT-546 lesson). Hardy–Littlewood: ζ(s) = Z_x(s) + O(x^{−σ}) with Z_x(s) = Σ_{n≤x}n^{−s} − x^{1−s}/(1−s) for \|t\| ≤ 2πx/C. By Rouché/Hurwitz, ζ-zeros near height T are limits of zeros of Z_x for x ≍ T. The pole correction is indispensable: Σ_{n≤x}n^{−s} alone has zeros up to Re s ≈ 1 + (4/π−1)log log x/log x (Montgomery). This is the additive form of row 337's lesson that the zeros come from the coupling of a finite arithmetic sum with the pole (archimedean) term, not from the arithmetic alone. *Kill*: RH for Z_x in the window \|t\| ≤ x/C, σ > ½ + ε is equivalent to RH there (Rouché both ways, with margins controlled by \|ζ\| lower bounds that are themselves RH-hard near zeros). Z_x is finite, but it has x ≍ T terms with no structure beyond ζ's. Each height window is its own deterministic individuation (wall 7), and there is no uniformity in x. No survivor. |
| 375 | **The primes are not the length spectrum of a compact hyperbolic surface** (paper; crisp no-go, dual to CANNOT-548; new to the corpus). Suppose a cocompact Fuchsian group Γ has primitive closed geodesics of lengths exactly {log p}, each once. Its Selberg zeta would be Z(s) = Π_γΠ_{k≥0}(1 − e^{−(s+k)l_γ}) = Π_{k≥0}ζ(s+k)^{−1} on Re s > 1. Each factor 1/ζ(s+k) has poles at s = ρ − k. Cancellation would need a zero of some 1/ζ(s+k′), i.e. s = 1 − k′, and ρ − k = 1 − k′ is impossible because no ζ-zero is an integer. So the continuation of Z has poles at every ρ − k, while Selberg's theorem makes Z entire for cocompact Γ (elliptic elements only change Γ-factors). Contradiction, since ζ has zeros. Hence no compact hyperbolic surface or orbifold has length spectrum {log p} with multiplicity one. The prime geodesic theorem (≍ e^T/T) is consistent with this, so the obstruction is the existence of ζ-zeros, not counting. Variable negative curvature (Ruelle zeta, merely meromorphic, Dyatlov–Zworski) and finite-volume surfaces (poles from scattering) escape the argument, consistent with row 342 and CANNOT-548. Not formalized: Selberg's entireness theorem is far outside the corpus. |
| 375a | **Precision for row 375.** Selberg's product runs over oriented primitive hyperbolic classes, and γ, γ^{−1} are distinct, so a surface realizes each length with even multiplicity. The argument works verbatim for any constant multiplicity m ≥ 1: Z = Π_kζ(s+k)^{−m} has poles of order m at every ρ − k. It also works for any multiplicity function m(p) that is eventually constant, since finitely many corrected Euler factors are entire and zero-free near ρ − k off Re s = 0. The statement is: no cocompact Fuchsian group has primitive length spectrum {log p} with eventually constant multiplicities. |
| 376 | **Extension of row 375 to variable negative curvature (Dyatlov–Zworski)** (paper, using an external theorem). For a compact surface Σ of variable negative curvature, the Ruelle zeta ζ_R(s) = Π_γ(1 − e^{−sl_γ}) (or its inverse, by convention) is meromorphic on ℂ. By Dyatlov–Zworski (Ruelle zeta at zero for surfaces) its order of vanishing at s = 0 is \|χ(Σ)\| = 2g−2 ≥ 2. If the primitive length spectrum were {log p} with constant multiplicity m (m even by orientation), then ζ_R = ζ^{±m} near 0 (up to a finite correction regular and nonzero at 0), with order 0 at s = 0 because ζ(0) = −½. This contradicts \|χ(Σ)\| ≥ 2. Hence the primes are not the length spectrum of any compact negatively curved surface. Unlike row 375 this uses only ζ(0) ≠ 0, not the zeros. Remaining open shapes: non-compact or infinite-type phase spaces (CANNOT-548 after its 366a correction) and higher-dimensional contact flows, where the order at 0 is topological (b₁-type) and could vanish. This is consistent with the forced non-compact/solenoidal shape. |
| 377 | **Unitarizability of the scaling action on Meyer's H¹ (Sz.-Nagy)** (paper; circular; owned as CANNOT-544, completion dichotomy MUST-138). A uniformly bounded representation of ℝ on a Hilbert space is similar to a unitary one (Sz.-Nagy), so RH would follow from a Hilbert norm on H¹ = H⁻/range Z with sup_λ‖U_λ‖ < ∞. The eigencharacters of U on H¹ are λ ↦ λ^{ρ−½} with multiplicity, and \|λ^{ρ−½}\| = λ^{β−½} is unbounded for β ≠ ½. So such a norm exists iff RH (⟸ by Sz.-Nagy, ⟹ by the eigencharacter bound). The natural norms are already classified (row 306): L²-type completions make H¹ zero (dense range), weighted L²_δ completions bound U by (1 + \|log λ\|)^δ and hence see only critical zeros, and strip completions lose unitarity. RH ⟺ the comparison map algebraic H¹ → L²_δ-cokernel is injective (CANNOT-544). An antiunitary involution J from the functional equation and complex conjugation gives only the quadruple symmetry {γ, γ̄, −γ, −γ̄}, not reality. Reality needs exactly the missing positivity. No survivor. |
| 378 | **An SL₂(ℝ) extension of the scaling flow on H¹ (Weil-representation reality)** (paper; killed; no SL₂-action on H¹ in the corpus). Idea: the dilation generator D = (xp+px)/2 is the A-generator of the oscillator (Weil) representation of Mp₂(ℝ) on L²(ℝ), with the Fourier transform as the Weyl element. ζ's functional equation is that Weyl element acting on Tate's zeta integral, and Connes' map E(f) = \|x\|^{½}Σ_{q∈ℚ*}f(qx) is a theta-type distribution with metaplectic Γ_θ-invariance. If H¹ carried a *unitary* SL₂(ℝ)-representation extending U_λ, the spectrum of D would be controlled by unitarity. *Kill*: in every unitary irreducible representation of SL₂(ℝ) (principal, complementary, discrete series) the A-generator has absolutely continuous spectrum ℝ, never isolated eigenvalues. The discrete D-spectrum of H¹ (the zeros) therefore cannot sit inside a unitary SL₂(ℝ)-module with D as the A-generator; only a direct sum of A-characters (no SL₂) or a non-unitary module can carry it. Theta/Weil-representation equivariance explains the functional equation (the γ ↦ −γ symmetry), not reality. Lesson: reality of a discrete spectrum must come from an abelian (A-only) Hilbert structure (Sz.-Nagy, row 377) or a compact-type operator, not from non-compact semisimple symmetry. |
| 379 | **The primes are not the length spectrum of any hyperbolic surface: elementary proof, formalized** (Lean, `RHTower/HyperbolicPrimeLengthSpectrumObstruction`, built, wired, standard axioms; strengthens CANNOT-550 in constant curvature to all covolumes and all multiplicities). A closed geodesic of length k log r has trace ±(√r^k + √r^{−k}), and parabolic elements and the identity have trace ±2. `false_of_mem_of_primeLengthTraces`: a subgroup G ≤ SL₂(ℝ) all of whose traces have this form contains no A, B with traces ±(√p + 1/√p), ±(√q + 1/√q) for primes p ≠ q. *Proof*: Fricke's identity tr(AB) + tr(AB⁻¹) = tr A·tr B (`trace_mul_add_trace_mul_adjugate`, with B⁻¹ = adj B) gives a sum of two admissible traces equal to ±(p+1)(q+1)/(pq)·√(pq). Each admissible trace is rational or a rational multiple of some √r (`rat_or_rat_mul_sqrt_of_isPrimeLengthTrace`). pq and pqr are never squares, so √(pq), √r and √(pqr) are irrational. The two-term independence lemma `eq_zero_of_mul_sqrt_add_mul_sqrt_eq_rat`, applied once after squaring away √r₂, forces the √(pq)-coefficient (p+1)(q+1)/(pq) to vanish, which is impossible. *Meaning*: a dynamical realization of the primes as primitive periodic orbits cannot be the geodesic flow of any hyperbolic surface (no elliptics), compact or not. The obstruction is the trace field: Fricke relations among two prime lengths force a √(pq)-length that no prime power supplies. This is independent of ζ-zeros, unlike row 375. |
| 380 | **Limits and companions of row 379** (paper). (a) *SL₂(ℂ)*: Fricke's identity holds verbatim. If every loxodromic element has zero holonomy angle (real traces ±(√r^k + √r^{−k})), a non-elementary group with all traces real is conjugate into SL₂(ℝ), so row 379 applies. With free holonomy angles θ_γ, traces ±2cosh((k log r + iθ)/2) are complex with free phases, and the Fricke equation is generically solvable. Hyperbolic 3-manifolds whose *real* length spectrum is {k log r} are therefore not excluded by trace fields. (b) *Transversal-holonomy reading*: for a suspension or Deninger-type flow whose periodic orbit of length log p has Poincaré map P_p ∈ SL₂(ℝ) with eigenvalues p^{±½}, one gets \|det(1−P_p^k)\|^{−1} ≈ p^{−k/2}, the explicit-formula weight, and tr P_p = √p + 1/√p. If every element of the holonomy group is the holonomy of some periodic orbit, row 379 forbids it. Arithmetic flows with SL₂(ℝ) transversal holonomy must therefore have non-orbit group elements, or non-discrete or elliptic holonomy. (c) *Euler graphs with lengths log n (ℚ-dependent)*: if a finite unitary star coupling σ on edges E ⊂ ℕ_{≥2} has secular determinant equal, as a Dirichlet series, to Π_{p∈E}(1−p^{−s}), comparing coefficients of m^{−s} gives σ_pp = 1 at primes (hence prime rows decoupled), zero diagonal at composites, and det(1−σ_CD_C) ≡ 1 on the composite block. The top Dirichlet term (Π_{n∈C}n)^{−s} has coefficient ±det σ_C ≠ 0, so C = ∅. Unitary graphs reproduce only the Euler-factor lattices, consistent with CANNOT-546 and row 359. |
| 381 | **Which compact 3-dimensional flows could have the primes as periodic orbits?** (paper; atlas status, external theorems cited). Wanted: a flow on a compact 3-manifold M whose primitive periodic orbits have lengths log p (arithmetic topology: primes ↔ knots), so that ζ_R = ζ^{±m}. *Excluded*: (1) geodesic flows of hyperbolic surfaces, i.e. SL₂(ℝ)-holonomy suspensions, of any area (row 379 [L]); (2) geodesic flows of compact negatively curved surfaces (Dyatlov–Zworski: ord_{s=0}ζ_R = \|χ\| ≥ 2, while ζ(0) ≠ 0 gives order 0; CANNOT-550); (3) compact contact Anosov 3-flows (Faure–Tsujii first-band density ≍ \|ω\| ≫ log\|ω\| for the zeros, CANNOT-548); (4) suspensions with constant roof (periods in ℤc, but the log p are ℚ-independent). *Not excluded*: non-contact transitive Anosov 3-flows with variable roof functions, for which no band Weyl law is known; the order at 0 is topological (b₁-type), and b₁(M) = 2 is admissible. The residual compact candidates are therefore non-contact Anosov flows, a narrow class. Together with the non-compact or solenoidal shape of CANNOT-548 (Connes' adèle class space), this narrows a dynamical (Deninger) realization to infinite-type or non-contact phase spaces. No RH input. |
| 382 | Pole-depth rigidity: does Weil positivity of Λ pin the pole's location as well as its weight? | BRIDGE (one null test; same mechanism as the pole-weight rigidity) | [L] MUST-1466 `WeilPoleDepthRigidity`: with weights Λ, a pole of weight β at depth c (|c| < 3/4) is compatible with positivity only if c = ±½ and β = 1. Witness F̂ = Ξ(z)(z² + c²): zeta side 0, F̂(ic) = 0, removed pole ½(c² − ¼)² > 0. Lesson: the null space Ξ·ℂ[z] sees every polar perturbation; polar data cannot carry RH content. |
| 383 | Riemann–Roch step of Weil's Hodge-index proof, transplanted to test functions. Dictionary: numerically trivial ↔ N₀ = Ξ·(z²+¼)ℂ[z] (radical of W with the pole classes e^{±x/2} adjoined); effective ↔ f ≥ 0; D·h, D·v ↔ f̂(i/2), f̂(−i/2); primitive ↔ polar-constrained. Conjecture (★): a primitive f with W(f,f) < 0 is effective modulo N₀. | CIRCULAR | (★) ⟹ RH, because f + h ≥ 0 with f̂(±i/2) = ĥ(±i/2) = 0 forces f + h = 0. But for f ∉ N₀ the coset f + N₀ never meets the nonnegative cone, so (★) is literally RH. The content would be a quantitative RR (a "dimension" of (nf + N₀) ∩ C₊ growing like n²(−W)), and no candidate h⁰ exists. The rank-2 Arakelov version leads to Weng zeta functions, whose RH is known but sits behind wall 4. The corpus already owns the Hodge-index framing (`HodgeIndexAtLevel`, `RelaxedHodgeDictionary`: relaxation exponent = zero depth). |
| 384 | PD-module structure of the blind cone. The blind cone is closed under pointwise multiplication by every PD function (Schur; supports stay compact; Boas–Kac in 1D keeps the result a sum of autocorrelations). Hence PBAP ⟺ for every blind PD k the distribution k·D_Γ is positive definite, with D_Γ = pole + archimedean Weil distribution; equivalently σ_k(s) = A(k e^{isx}) = (Z ⋆ k̂)(s) ≥ 0 for all s. | BRIDGE | On blind k, k·D_Γ = k·Ẑ, where Ẑ is the zero distribution, so this is Schur positivity of kẐ, which is RH. A reduction to a small generating family of "cores" would need B = cone(G·PD) for an explicit G; none is known, and the support of b·g never shrinks below supp b. The positive survivor is the modulation invariance B·e^{isx} ⊆ B_ℂ. It shows that every PBAP certificate is one-parameter translation-covariant, so a proof must work uniformly along spectral translates; the translates tend to +∞ like Ω(s)k(0). Not formalized: elementary. |
| 382a | Strongest survivor of row 382 (paper). Finite polar-divisor classification. Let D = β_½[½] + Σ_i β_i[c_i] be a finite real polar divisor with distinct depths 0 ≤ c_i < 3/4, c_i ≠ ½, used in place of ζ's pole with weights Λ. If W_Λ^D ≥ 0 on all real C² compactly supported autocorrelations, then D = [½] + β_0[0] with β_0 ≥ 0; conversely, under RH every such D is Weil-positive (P_0 = 2(∫F)² ≥ 0). | PROVED (paper) | Witnesses: Ξ·Q with Q ∈ {∏(z²+c_i²), iz∏(z²+c_i²)} force β_½ = 1; Q ∈ {(z²+¼)∏_{i≠j}(z²+c_i²), iz(z²+¼)∏_{i≠j}} force β_j ≥ 0 and β_j ≤ 0 (c_j ≠ 0). Each needs only P_c(Ξ·Q) = 2Ξ(ic)²Q(ic)Q(−ic) and a vanishing zeta side. Lean: the single-depth case is MUST-1466; the general case needs strip decay of Ξ·P for arbitrary P (finite combinations of the shifted-Gamma family). Not formalized, since it adds no RH content. |
| 385 | Blind-cone pole depth (row 339, open). Define PBAP_c: arch + 2∫k cosh(cx) ≥ 0 on the ζ-blind PD cone. On the blind null face N_B (A = 0), L_c(k₀) = pole_c(k₀) − pole_½(k₀) = 2Σ_j w_j[Ξ(ic)² q_j(c) − Ξ(i/2)² δ_{j0}], with q_j(c) = ∏_{k<j}((k+¼)² − c²/4) (q_j(½) = 0 for j ≥ 1). | BRIDGE (sharp reduction) | (i) The modulation/PD-module structure (row 384) makes PBAP_c fail as soon as some k₀ ∈ N_B has L_c(k₀) < 0. (ii) For ½ < c < 5/2, q_j(c) < 0 for every j ≥ 1. So PBAP_c fails on all of (½, 5/2) as soon as N_B contains an element with w₀ = 0 (zero pole) or with negative pole (corpus C190). (iii) For c < ½ the window-sector tests do not refute PBAP_c: a Gaussian estimate gives L_0 ≈ 0.016 − 0.008 > 0. So the c < ½ side is genuinely open and depends on the TMP weights. Pole-depth rigidity on the blind cone is therefore equivalent to a sign question about N_B, which is the other agent's C190 lane. Not pursued further. |
| 386 | Liouville/Bessel Hilbert–Pólya. Pólya's Ξ* is real-rooted because K_{iν}(a) = 0 ⟺ ν² is a Dirichlet eigenvalue of −d²/du² + e^{2u} on [log a, ∞) (corpus `PolyaBesselHeatKernel`). Conjecture: Ξ is the Dirichlet determinant of one Liouville-type operator −d² + e^{2u} + W(u), or of a star graph of Liouville half-lines (one edge per n, walls at log(πn²)). | FALSE | (i) Star graph: each edge n contributes ≈ (√E/π) log(√E/b_n)₊ eigenvalues, so with b_n ≍ n² the count is ≍ E^{3/4} log E, far above the zero count. (ii) Single edge: the corrections from n ≥ 2 decay like e^{−π(n²−1)e^{2u}}, i.e. they are short-range near the wall. A short-range perturbation has a bounded spectral shift at high energy, but N(T) − N*(T) = S(T) + O(1) is unbounded (Selberg/Tsang Ω±). This is the size law CANNOT-549 in Sturm–Liouville form. The perturbation W would have to be long-range and energy-resonant at the frequencies k log p at every scale, i.e. fractal (Wu–Sprung). Positive survivor: the zero density of ζ is exactly that of ONE Liouville edge, and every arithmetic correction to Pólya's operator lives in the long-range tail, never at the wall. |
| 387 | Variation of the JAS Bohr spectral measure. Conjecture: an off-line zero makes the T^∞-spectral measure of the dressed aggregate have infinite total variation, because Π_p‖ν_{p,ρ}‖ ≍ Π(1 + cp^{−1/2}) = ∞, so RH would be finiteness of variation. | FALSE | The prime side lies in ℓ²(ℚ_{>0}), since Σ_p(log p/p)² < ∞. Its transform on T^∞ is therefore an L² ⊂ L¹ density, and the on-line zeros give positive L² product densities Π_p P_{1/p}(θ_p − γ log p) (a martingale with Σ r_p² < ∞). Finiteness of variation is free; RH is only the sign of the density, which is the corpus row-177 lower-tail form. Survivor: individually divergent off-line products must cancel collectively, which is the same "compensation between zeros over infinitely many primes" that the frontier names as the open JAS core. |
| 388 | Direct PBAP attack: two-scale energy. Bound the Lévy energy below by the log-bandwidth gain ½ log(m₂/k(0)), bound the pole tail by blind decay, and close by comparison. | FAILED | The RKHS triangle inequality gives the two-sided node bound \|k(x)\| ≤ √(2k(0)m₂)·dist(x, ±log ℕ≥2) + m₂ dist²/2, so blind PD tests decay like √(k(0)m₂)e^{−x} (owned: `BlindLogNodeDecay`). That kills the pole tail beyond x ≈ 2 log B (B = √(m₂/k(0))). But on [log 2, log B] the a priori pole bound is ≍ √B·k(0) (from \|F̂(±i/2)\|² ≤ ‖F‖²e^{a}), while the archimedean gain is only log B. Positivity therefore needs the exact pole/arch balance; on the blind null face A = 0 with no slack, so no estimate with a margin can work. Wall 12 again, with the precise loss: √B against log B. |
